{"id": "f7414407b94d587a", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T15:35:16.023984+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "C28 asymptotic capacity-filter failure: the palindrome ((1/2)(3/2)^i for i=0,...,k-1),1,reverse has length2k+1 and capacity2(3/2)^k, passes EVERY exact primitive single-cut test strictly and has zero full-curvature product, yet is nonprimitive for every k>=15. One fixed curvature1/2 quadratic proves the rejection, and the evidence gives a finite strict simplex-projection realization.", "evidence": "# Projection capacity calibration and an exponential false-positive family\n\nStatus: complete candidate calculations, NOT verifier-accepted. No solver or\nfact_submit. All new ingredients are proved below. Accepted fact\n1252b8823bb5a290 supplies the smooth-convex interpolation inequalities;\nb98bb807d514e80d supplies only the named exact benchmark recursion.\nThis is a capacity test, not a proof of the all-length capacity conjecture.\n\nFor a nonnegative schedule h of length n, put S=sum h_i, D=1+S, and\n\n    V_h=2sum_(i<n)h_i(f_i-f_n)\n       +sum_(i<n)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-SD||g_n||^2.\n\nPrimitive means V_h>=0 on every actual convex differentiable 1-smooth\nGD run. All witnesses and certificates below use the physical steps.\n\n## 1. Exact projection witnesses give two necessary tests\n\nFor any compact interval C containing zero, the function\nF_C(x)=max_(v in C)(xv-v^2/2) is convex, differentiable and 1-smooth,\nwith derivative equal to the nearest-point projection onto C. This follows\nby completing the square; the projection normal inequality gives its\n1-Lipschitz property. At every actual sample, f_i=x_i g_i-g_i^2/2.\nThe squared-distance telescope therefore gives exactly\n\n    V_h=x_0^2-(x_n+Sg_n)^2.                           (1)\n\nChoose one checkpoint b=h_j>1. Put\nA=1+sum_(i<j)h_i, B=1+sum_(i>j)h_i, so D=A+B+b-1.\nChoose u>0, v=(b-1)u/B, C=[-v,u], and x_0=A u.\nEvery preceding ordinary step stays on the positive plateau, with\ngradient u, and the checkpoint input is exactly u. Its output is\n-(b-1)u=-Bv. Every following ordinary step stays on the negative plateau,\nwith gradient -v, and the final point is -v. All function values are\nthose of the ONE fixed projection function, so (1) gives\n\n    V_h=A^2u^2-D^2(b-1)^2u^2/B^2.\n\nThus every primitive schedule satisfies the exact cut conditions\n\n    D(h_j-1)_+ <= (1+sum_(i<j)h_i)(1+sum_(i>j)h_i).   (2)\n\nThere is a second projection witness: start the scalar unit quadratic\nat1 and let x_i=product_(t<i)(1-h_t). A compact interval containing zero\nand ALL these finitely many points has projection gradient g_i=x_i at\nevery sample and the same values x_i^2/2. Therefore every primitive h has\n\n    D |product_(i<n)(1-h_i)| <=1.                    (3)\n\nCondition (3) tests curvature1 only, not all curvatures.\n\n## 2. Exact two-step capacity upper bound\n\nFor any primitive h=(h_0,h_1), (2) gives\n\n    h_0,h_1 <= M_D:=2D/(D+1),  D=1+h_0+h_1.          (4)\n\nIf a step is at most1 this is automatic; otherwise rearrange its own\ncut inequality D(h_i-1)<=D-h_i.\nLet D_two be the unique root larger than4 of\n\n    D_two^4-4D_two^3-1=0.                            (5)\n\nWe claim D<=D_two. There is nothing to prove if D<4. For D>=4,\n(4) and h_0+h_1=D-1 imply both steps lie in\n[L_D,M_D], where L_D=D-1-M_D>1. The function\n(x-1)(D-2-x) is concave and has the same value at these two endpoints.\nConsequently\n\n    (h_0-1)(h_1-1)\n      >= (M_D-1)(L_D-1)\n      = (D-1)(D^2-3D-2)/(D+1)^2.\n\nMultiplying by D and applying (3) yields\n\n    D(D-1)(D^2-3D-2)<=(D+1)^2,\n\nwhich is precisely D^4-4D^3-1<=0. The polynomial is strictly increasing\nfor D>3, negative at4 and positive at5, proving (5)'s uniqueness and\nthe claimed upper bound.\n\n## 3. An explicit full certificate attains that bound\n\nPut b=sqrt(2) and let a be the positive root of\n\n    a^2+ba=2+2b,\n    a=(-b+sqrt(10+8b))/2.\n\nThen 1<a<2. Set S=a+b and L_cert=S(2-b)>0. On the two-step schedule\n(a,b), use Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2. The following\nis an identity on the FULL formal three-gradient space:\n\n    V_(a,b) = sum_(i!=j) lambda_ij (-Q_ij),          (6)\n\nwhere\n\n    lambda_01=0,\n    lambda_10=b L_cert,\n    lambda_02=(a-1)L_cert,\n    lambda_20=L_cert,\n    lambda_21=S(S+b),\n    lambda_12=(b-1)lambda_21+b L_cert.\n\nAll coefficients are nonnegative, so accepted interpolation necessity\nQ_ij<=0 proves primitive validity in every finite dimension.\n\nHere is a full coefficient check. Write u=g_0,v=g_1,w=g_2 and use\nx_1=x_0-a u, x_2=x_0-a u-b v. The five residuals used in (6) are\n\n    Q_10=f_1-f_0+(||u||^2+||v||^2)/2+(a-1)<u,v>,\n    Q_02=f_0-f_2+(1/2-a)||u||^2+||w||^2/2\n                                      -b<u,v>-<u,w>,\n    Q_20=f_2-f_0+(||u||^2+||w||^2)/2\n                                      +(a-1)<u,w>+b<v,w>,\n    Q_12=f_1-f_2+(1/2-b)||v||^2+||w||^2/2-<v,w>,\n    Q_21=f_2-f_1+(||v||^2+||w||^2)/2+(b-1)<v,w>.\n\nThe three mixed-gradient coefficients cancel directly from the displayed\nlambdas. The function coefficients are 2a,2b,-2S: use\n(b+2-a)L_cert=2a and (2-b)S(S-b)=2b, both consequences of\naS=2+2b and b^2=2. The squared-gradient coefficients are\n\n    ||u||^2: (a-1)^2 L_cert-a=a(1-a),\n    ||v||^2: (2-3b/2)S(S+b)+b(b-1)L_cert=b(1-b),\n    ||w||^2: -[aL_cert+bS(S+b)+bL_cert]/2=-S(S+1).\n\nFor the first equality, expanding (a-1)^2 S(2-b) using aS=2+2b\ngives 2a-a^2. For the second, divide by S; the constant terms cancel\nand the result is a(2-3b/2), whose product with S is b-2=b(1-b).\nThe third uses b^2=2. This verifies every coefficient in (6).\n\nLet D=1+a+b. Then D(a-1)=1+b and\nD(a-1)(b-1)=b^2-1=1. Also a=2D/(D+1), so equality holds throughout\nthe bound in section2. Finally a>3-b because the defining polynomial\nat 3-b equals 7-5sqrt(2)<0. Thus D>4 and D=D_two. Explicitly,\n\n    D_two=[2+sqrt(2)+sqrt(10+8sqrt(2))]/2\n         =Omega(1,1+sqrt(2)).                        (7)\n\nThe last expression uses the exact primitive join notation from\naccepted fact b98bb807d514e80d. Equations (2)--(7) give an analytic\ncapacity bound and an attaining universal certificate, not an SDP result.\nThis is a two-step calibration, not a new all-length capacity claim.\n\n## 4. Cut tests plus curvature1 admit exponentially large false positives\n\nFix r_geom=3/2. For each integer k>=1 put\n\n    u_i=(r_geom-1)r_geom^i=(1/2)(3/2)^i, 0<=i<k,\n    h^(k)=(u_0,u_1,...,u_(k-1),1,u_(k-1),...,u_1,u_0).\n\nIts length is n=2k+1 and its exact physical capacity is\n\n    D=1+sum h^(k)=2 r_geom^k.                         (8)\n\nEvery cut condition (2) holds STRICTLY. For a left-half step, put\nA=r_geom^i, b=(r_geom-1)A. The other mass is B=D-r_geom A+1.\nSince A<=r_geom^(k-1)=D/(2r_geom),\n\n    AB-D(b-1)\n      =D[(2-r_geom)A+1]-r_geom A^2+A\n      >=D[(3/2-r_geom)A+1]+A=D+A>0.\n\nThe mirror cut has the same two masses interchanged. The center step1\nhas zero left side in (2); steps below1 also satisfy it trivially.\nCondition (3) holds with product ZERO because of that center step.\n\nNevertheless these schedules are not primitive for every k>=15. This\ncan be shown on the ONE fixed scalar quadratic f(x)=x^2/4, starting at1.\nIts curvature is lambda=1/2, and its final iterate is\n\n    x_n=(1/2)[product_(i=0)^(k-1)(1-r_geom^i/4)]^2.    (9)\n\nNone of these factors is zero. For i=0,...,5 the product of their\nabsolute values is exactly\n\n    C_6=(3/4)(5/8)(7/16)(5/32)(17/64)(115/128)\n       =1026375/134217728 > 1/256.\n\nFor i>=6, r_geom^i/4>=729/256>2, so\n\n    |1-r_geom^i/4|>=r_geom^i/8>=729/512>sqrt(2),\n\nwhere the last strict comparison is 729^2>2*512^2. Hence for k>=6,\n\n    x_n > (1/2)(1/256)^2 2^(k-6)=2^(k-23),\n    D |g_n|=r_geom^k x_n > 3^k/2^23.                 (10)\n\nFor k>=15 this exceeds1, since 3^15=14348907>8388608=2^23.\n\nTo check that (10) really forces primitive failure, for any legal\nsmooth-convex data with minimizer0 put\nI_i=<g_i,x_i>-f_i-||g_i||^2/2>=0. A direct telescope gives\n\n    V_h=||x_0||^2-||x_n||^2-2sum h_i I_i\n                         -2S f_n-S(S+1)||g_n||^2.\n\nSmoothness and convexity give ||x_n||>=||g_n|| and\n2f_n>=||g_n||^2. Thus\n\n    V_h<=||x_0||^2-D^2||g_n||^2.                     (11)\n\nEquation (10) makes (11) negative for the explicit quadratic run.\nAll steps and function values are actual; the failure is not a relaxed\ngradient assignment. Capacity (8) grows exponentially with n, so (2)\nand (3) alone cannot establish ANY polynomial capacity upper bound.\n\nThis refutes only the cut-plus-curvature1 filter. The curvature1/2 test\nexplicitly detects the family. It makes no insufficiency claim about\nthe entire quadratic class, and does not propose a primitive schedule\nabove the silver-power threshold.\n\n## 5. These scalar defects also have finite simplex projection witnesses\n\nFor completeness, the negative energy just established can be placed\ninside the requested projection class by finite operations, without\nusing the unverified C27 reduction as a premise. This also explains why\nprojection witnesses can detect a failure not detected by (2) or (3).\n\nGiven this length n schedule and its negative quadratic energy -v_def,\ntake an auxiliary diagonal quadratic with eigenvalues\ntheta_l=(l+1)/(2(n+2)), l=0,...,n, started with all coordinates1.\nIts n+1 gradients are independent because the polynomials\nP_i(z)=product_(t<i)(1-h_t z) have distinct degrees0,...,n; evaluation\nat n+1 distinct theta_l, followed by nonzero row scaling theta_l, is\ninvertible. All distinct auxiliary Q_ij are strictly negative: they\nequal minus one half the A_aux(I-A_aux) squared norm of the sample\nposition difference, and the relevant polynomial difference cannot\nvanish at n+1 distinct nodes.\n\nLet E_aux be its finite energy. Add it in orthogonal coordinates with\nsquared scale epsilon=v_def/[2(v_def+|E_aux|)]. The product function\nis an actual 1-smooth convex function, its new energy is at most\n-v_def/2, all new gradients are independent, and all finite Q_ij<0.\nRename these mixed jets x_i,g_i,f_i and their positive definite gradient\nGram matrix A_gram. Choose\n\n    f_*=min_i(f_i-||g_i||^2/2)-1,\n    r_i=f_*-f_i+<g_i,x_i>-||g_i||^2/2,\n    z_*=sum_j(A_gram^(-1)r)_j g_j.\n\nThen X_i=x_i-z_* and F_i=f_i-f_* satisfy\nF_i=<g_i,X_i>-||g_i||^2/2. Every finite residual is unchanged and\nequals <X_j-g_j,g_i-g_j><0. The comparison with vertex zero is\n<X_i-g_i,-g_i>=-(F_i-||g_i||^2/2)<=-1. These are ALL normal\ninequalities for C=conv{0,g_0,...,g_n}, proving P_C(X_i)=g_i and\nF_C(X_i)=F_i. Translation and the common value shift leave energy\nunchanged. Projection onto the gradient span places this simplex\nand its trajectory in dimension n+1 with exactly n+2 vertices.\n\nThus every k>=15 in (8) has an explicit finite simplex projection\ncounterexample. This construction preserves the negative sign and\nretains every queried vertex, but not normalized objective error.\n\n## Scope and the next missing ingredient\n\nThe exact two-step optimum and the exponential false-positive family\nare quantitative consequences of actual geometric tests. They identify\nprecisely why extrapolating the two-step cut/product proof cannot work\nat arbitrary length. A general capacity argument must use additional\ncurvatures or additional coupled simplex normal inequalities. No\nunproved inequality with silver exponent is used anywhere above.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "workers/max3/research/c28_projection_capacity_tests.md", "target_claim": "All primitive single-cut inequalities plus the unit-curvature quadratic test can imply a polynomial capacity bound.", "status": "Refuted as a proposed necessary-test closure; complete unverified candidate.", "assumptions_satisfied": ["All physical steps are positive.", "Every exact single-cut inequality is strict.", "The full-curvature product is zero."], "failed_conclusion": "The tested capacity is exponential in the number of steps; the actual schedules are nonprimitive.", "impact": "Use additional curvatures or coupled simplex geometry. The whole quadratic class is NOT defeated here; curvature1/2 explicitly rejects the family."}, "glossary": {"r_geom": "3/2.", "u_i": "(1/2)(3/2)^i.", "h^(k)": "The length2k+1 palindromic schedule (u_0,...,u_(k-1),1,u_(k-1),...,u_0).", "D": "Its shifted mass 1+sum h^(k)=2(3/2)^k."}}
{"id": "f533b2a823b0ae32", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T15:43:42.883840+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "The new arbitrary-tail pair policy does NOT close the full-profile gap, even after its exact angle/intercept optimization. On H_m=(H_(m-1),a_rul(13/5)^(m-1),H_(m-1)), a_rul=(13/5)^-17, EVERY pair obeys C_ij<=max(3K_i/4,4K_j); hence the optimized policy E_tail<=16K. A self-contained proof gives F_ray=K<=8/[5a_rul(13/5)^m] and B_n<=1+4n^(4/3). Thus max(F_ray,E_tail)/(1/B_(2^m-1)) tends to zero. This excludes only these explicit policies, not unrestricted W or the target theorem.", "evidence": "# The new two-checkpoint tail policy still misses the full profile\n\nStatus: UNVERIFIED joint proof candidate. No verifier or solver allocation.\nAll new estimates, including the recursive-schedule and benchmark estimates,\nare reproved here. Shared unverified findings are not premises.\n\nThis is a limit of the explicit policy from\n`research/max_profile_height_and_tail_cap.md`, not an upper bound on W_n.\nThe policy itself has a full fixed-function construction in that document.\nThe comparisons here concern its explicit numerical formulas and the\nVERIFIED K and F_ray functionals, defined in facts `b98bb807d514e80d` and\n`1fca3e5dd555cb4a` respectively.\n\n## 1. The exact functionals to be tested\n\nFor any i<j with h_i,h_j>1, write\n\n P=sum_(t<i)h_t, S=sum_(i<t<j)h_t, T=sum_(t>j)h_t,\n A=(h_i-1)/(1+P), epsilon=(h_j-1)/(1+S), sigma=1+2T.\n\nDefine\n\n    C_ij=A^2 min(epsilon^2,epsilon)/(4sigma),\n    C_n^tail=max_(eligible i<j) C_ij,\n\nwith zero if there are no eligible pairs. Also set a=P/(1+P). For\n0<kappa<=A/(1+epsilon), define\n\n V_ij(kappa)=[A kappa epsilon^2\n    -a^2(kappa epsilon-sigma/(1+P))_+^2/\n                 ((A-kappa)(A+2a)+a^2)]/sigma.\n\nLet E_n^tail be the maximum of V_ij(kappa) over those pairs and angles,\nor zero if there are no pairs. These are exactly the lower and optimized\nupper formulas of the constructed family, with the explicit angle optimizer\ngiven in the companion document. Purely algebraically,\n\n    C_n^tail<=E_n^tail<=4 C_n^tail.                        (1)\n\nFor the upper bound discard the nonnegative correction and use\nkappa<=A/(1+epsilon). If epsilon<=1 the result is at most\nA^2 epsilon^2/sigma; if epsilon>=1 it is at most A^2 epsilon/sigma.\nFor the lower bound choose kappa=A/[2max(1,epsilon)]. With\nD=(A-kappa)(A+2a)+a^2, the correction in V is no larger than\na^2 kappa^2 epsilon^2/D<=kappa^2 epsilon^2, so\nV>=epsilon^2 kappa(A-kappa)/sigma>=C_ij.\n\n## 2. The prescribed recursive schedules\n\nPut c=13/5, a_rul=c^(-17), b_u=a_rul c^u and\n\n    H_0=(), H_(m+1)=(H_m,b_m,H_m),\n    n_m=2^m-1,\n    s_m=sum H_m=a_rul(c^m-2^m)/(c-2).\n\nThe last identity follows by induction from s_(m+1)=2s_m+b_m. Define\neta=13/8. For u>=17,\n\n    s_u/b_u=[1-(10/13)^u]/(3/5)>=eta.                    (2)\n\nIndeed (10/13)^3<1/2 and (10/13)^2<4/5 imply\n(10/13)^17<1/40. Every eligible checkpoint b_u>1 has u>=18, so (2)\napplies to every eligible index below.\n\nAt each occurrence of b_u there is a full H_u immediately before it\nand another immediately after it. In particular its complete preceding\nand following masses are each at least s_u>=eta b_u. Its one-based\nposition has 2-adic valuation u; this also follows directly by induction.\n\n## 3. Every new pair is controlled by an old single cut\n\nFix i<j, of levels u and v. Denote the corresponding individual K terms by\n\n    K_i=(h_i-1)^2/[(1+P)^2(1+2(S+h_j+T))],\n    K_j=(h_j-1)^2/[(1+P+h_i+S)^2(1+2T)].\n\nIf epsilon<=1, put x=1+S and d=h_j-1, so d/x<=1. Since\nT>=eta h_j>d, the ratio is\n\n C_ij/K_i=(d/x)^2/4 *[1+(2x+2d)/(1+2T)]\n           <=[2(d/x)^2+d/x]/4<=3/4.                     (3)\n\nNow suppose epsilon>1, so x=1+S<d=h_j-1. Necessarily u<v and i lies\ninside the copy of H_v immediately preceding j. If u>=v, that copy's\nH_v before j lies strictly after i and gives S>=s_v>=eta h_j, a\ncontradiction. If u<v but i precedes that copy, the same contradiction\nholds. Inside the copy, the H_u immediately after i is contained between\ni and j, so S>=s_u>=eta h_i. Also, writing P_loc for the mass in that\ncopy before i, one has\n\n    P>=P_loc=s_v-h_i-S.\n\nBecause u<v, h_i<=h_j/c. Using S<h_j-2 therefore yields\n\n P>(eta-1-1/c)h_j+2=(25/104)h_j+2,\n x/(1+P)<104/25,\n h_i/(1+P)<=1/eta<2/3.                                  (4)\n\nConsequently\n\n (1+P+h_i+S)/(1+P)<1+2/3+104/25<6,\n (h_i-1)^2/(d x)<h_i^2/x^2<=1/eta^2<4/9.\n\nThe exact ratio to the second single cut is thus\n\n C_ij/K_j=[(h_i-1)^2/(4d x)]\n                   *[(1+P+h_i+S)/(1+P)]^2<4.             (5)\n\nEquations (3)--(5) cover EVERY eligible pair, not merely chosen large\ncheckpoints. They prove\n\n    C_(n_m)^tail(H_m)<=4 K_(n_m)(H_m),\n    E_(n_m)^tail(H_m)<=16 K_(n_m)(H_m).                   (6)\n\nThe cases epsilon approaching1, tiny initial levels and zero eligible\npairs cause no exception; the division into (3) and (5) is exhaustive.\n\n## 4. Full ray comparison and a uniform K bound on these schedules\n\nFor completeness the same schedules obey F_ray=K. For proposed first\ntwo ray checkpoints i<j, let their levels be u,v and retain P,S above.\nThe exact accepted ray recurrence would give a second-crossing budget\n\n    [(b_u-1)(b_v-1)/(1+S)-P]/(1+P).\n\nAny chain of at least two checkpoints requires this to be positive.\nWe show the stronger opposite estimate PS>=b_u b_v.\n\nIf u>=v, P>=s_u and S>=s_v. If u<v but i is before the H_v preceding\nj, these same bounds hold. In either case PS>=b_u b_v by (2).\nOtherwise i lies inside that H_v. The local copies of H_u give\n\n    P_loc>=s_u, S>=s_u, P_loc+S=s_v-b_u.\n\nFactoring (P_loc-s_u)(S-s_u)>=0 shows\n\n    PS>=s_u(s_v-b_u-s_u).\n\nFor v=u+1, this is s_u^2>=c b_u^2=b_u b_v since eta^2=169/64>13/5.\nFor larger v, define D_v=s_u(s_v-b_u-s_u)-b_u b_v. Its successive\nincrement is\n\n D_(v+1)-D_v=s_u(s_v+b_v)-(c-1)b_u b_v\n             >=(3-c)b_u b_v>0,\n\nusing s_u>=b_u and s_v>=b_v. Hence PS>=b_u b_v always. The second\ncrossing budget is nonpositive, which prevents both a subsequent positive\ngradient and a terminal return with its positive old-slope constraint.\nOnly zero or one checkpoint remains, so F_ray=K by the accepted fact.\n\nFor m>=18 let s=s_(m-1), the mass of either child of the top checkpoint.\nAn eligible cut at or left of the top checkpoint has T>=s and\n(h_i-1)/(1+P_i)<=1 by its preceding buffer. Its K term is <=1/(1+2s).\nA cut right of the top checkpoint has P_i>=s and, from its own local\nbuffers, T_i>=h_i and P_i>=h_i. Thus its term is at most\n\n    h_i/[2(1+P_i)^2]<=1/[2(1+P_i)]<=1/[2(1+s)].\n\nThe global Huber term is <=1/(1+s). Therefore\n\n    F_ray_(n_m)(H_m)=K_(n_m)(H_m)<=1/(1+s_(m-1))\n                          <=8/(5a_rul c^m).              (7)\n\nIn combination,\n\n    E_(n_m)^tail(H_m)<=128/(5a_rul c^m).                 (8)\n\n## 5. Comparison with the actual B_n sequence\n\nThe following elementary estimate of the VERIFIED benchmark recursions is\nincluded so that no unverified stronger growth estimate is a premise:\n\n    S_circle(n)+1<=(n+1)^(4/3),\n    B_n<=1+4n^(4/3).                                    (9)\n\nWrite p=4/3 just within this section. The primitive join is\n\n Omega(a,b)=(a+b+sqrt((a+b)^2+4ab))/2.\n\nWe claim Omega(x^p,y^p)<=(x+y)^p for x,y>=0. After dividing by\n(x+y)^p and using symmetry, it suffices to show, for 0<=t<=1/2,\n\n    t^p+(1-t)^p+[t(1-t)]^p<=1.                          (10)\n\nThis is the condition that1 lies beyond the positive root of\nz^2-[t^p+(1-t)^p]z-[t(1-t)]^p=0. At t=0 it is equality.\nFor t>0, Taylor's integral remainder and\n\n    [(1-t)^p]''=(4/9)(1-t)^(-2/3)<3/4\n\non [0,1/2] give\n\n    (1-t)^p<=1-4t/3+3t^2/8.\n\nFor the strict derivative bound, 2^(2/3)<27/16 follows on cubing.\nThe left side of (10) is at most\n\n    1+t[-4/3+L(t)],\n    L(t)=t^(1/3)(2-4t/3+3t^2/8)+3t/8.\n\nThe function L is increasing on (0,1/2]. Indeed its derivative times\nt^(2/3) is\n\n    2/3-16t/9+7t^2/8+3t^(2/3)/8.\n\nFor t<=3/8 the first two terms are nonnegative. For 3/8<=t<=1/2,\nthe first three terms are at least their value -1/288 at1/2, since\ntheir derivative -16/9+7t/4 is negative; the last term is at least\n3t/8>=9/64>1/288. Thus the derivative is positive throughout.\nFinally 2^(-1/3)<4/5 gives\n\n    L(1/2)<(4/5)(4/3+3/32)+3/16=319/240<4/3.\n\nThis proves (10), including every t. Induction in the exact primitive\nrecurrence now gives C_n=S_circle(n)+1<=(n+1)^p.\n\nIn the dominant recursion, writing a=C_k, d=B_(n-k-1), the denominator\njoin is\n\n    G(a,d)=2a+d/2+sqrt(d^2+8ad)/2<=4a+d,\n\nsince sqrt(d^2+8ad)<=d+4a. Starting with B_0=1, induction gives\n\n B_n<=1+4[(k+1)^p+(n-k-1)^p]<=1+4n^p\n\nfor every split, proving (9). This is a bound on the full supplied\nsequence, without a phase limit or dyadic-constant substitution.\n\nAs c^3=2197/125>16, one has 2^(4/3)/c<1. From (7)--(9),\n\n max(F_ray_(n_m)(H_m),E_(n_m)^tail(H_m)) B_(n_m)\n       <=[128/(5a_rul c^m)] [1+4(2^m-1)^(4/3)] ->0.       (11)\n\nThus even the exact optimized three-cell policy together with the whole\nverified ray functional fails the required uniform lower comparison.\nAll parameter choices and finite tail budgets were included before this\ncountertest. This does not bound unrestricted W from above and does not\ndecide the project target. In particular it is compatible with the known\nshared, unverified coupled-coordinate construction giving very large true\nerrors on H_m; that construction is not used anywhere in this proof.\n\nThe precise remaining mechanism is repeated changes of direction and\ncreation of positive height across more than two selected checkpoints,\nwith all queried history retained. Optimizing only the final cap, or\nchoosing the two checkpoints more carefully, cannot overcome (11).\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"fact_ids": ["b98bb807d514e80d", "1fca3e5dd555cb4a"], "finding_ids": ["1ed052539a27d4ff", "79f6ce08b41616b5"]}, "glossary": {"C_ij": "A² min(epsilon²,epsilon)/[4(1+2T)] for the specified checkpoint pair.", "C_n^tail": "Maximum C_ij over all eligible pairs, or zero.", "E_n^tail": "Maximum exact constant-tail score V_ij(kappa) over all pairs and 0<kappa<=A/(1+epsilon).", "H_m": "Recursive schedule H_0 empty, H_(m+1)=(H_m,b_m,H_m).", "a_rul": "(13/5)^(-17), the fixed ruler-schedule coefficient.", "b_u": "a_rul(13/5)^u, the step at level u.", "s_m": "Total mass a_rul((13/5)^m-2^m)/(3/5) of H_m.", "eta": "13/8, the exact local buffer lower factor for eligible ruler checkpoints.", "n_m": "Length 2^m-1 of H_m."}}
{"id": "c58273e20409955e", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T15:44:59.620153+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "C29 stronger asymptotic CAPACITY-TEST obstruction: Gsc_m=(Hsc_m,Hsc_m), Hsc_(j+1)=(Hsc_j,a_sc c_sc^j,Hsc_j), c_sc=13/5,a_sc=c_sc^-17, passes EVERY primitive single-cut test strictly AND has V_h>=0 on EVERY convex quadratic in EVERY finite dimension. Yet Dsc_m/(Lsc_m+1)^rho_sil tends to infinity. Thus all quadratics plus all one-cut projection witnesses cannot prove the silver-power primitive capacity bound. Actual general primitivity of Gsc_m is NOT claimed.", "evidence": "# All quadratic primitive tests and all single cuts still miss the capacity scale\n\nStatus: complete self-contained candidate, NOT verifier-accepted. No new\nsolver or fact_submit. Earlier ruler estimates are re-proved here, not used\nas unverified premises. This concerns a necessary-test relaxation of\nprimitive validity; it does NOT prove that the constructed schedules are\nprimitive for general smooth convex functions.\n\n## Statement\n\nPut c_sc=13/5, a_sc=c_sc^(-17), bsc_j=a_sc c_sc^j, and\n\n    Hsc_0=(),  Hsc_(j+1)=(Hsc_j,bsc_j,Hsc_j),\n    ssc_m=sum Hsc_m=a_sc(c_sc^m-2^m)/(c_sc-2).\n\nConsider TWO consecutive copies\n\n    Gsc_m=(Hsc_m,Hsc_m),\n    Lsc_m=2(2^m-1),   Dsc_m=1+2ssc_m.                (1)\n\nFor any finite nonnegative schedule h of sum S define the intrinsic\nprimitive energy on an actual smoothness-one GD trajectory by\n\n    V_h=2sum_(i<n)h_i(f_i-f_n)\n       +sum_(i<n)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2\n       -S(S+1)||g_n||^2.                             (2)\n\nThe following assertions hold for every m:\n\n(a) EVERY single-cut primitive test holds strictly for Gsc_m:\n\n    Dsc_m(h_j-1)_+\n      <(1+sum_(i<j)h_i)(1+sum_(i>j)h_i)              (3)\n\n    for each actual step j.\n\n(b) V_(Gsc_m)>=0 on EVERY finite-dimensional convex quadratic with a\n    minimizer and gradient Lipschitz constant at most1, from every initial\n    point. This tests every curvature, and the actual chronological\n    primitive energy, not only the final polynomial or curvature1.\n\n(c) With rho_sil=log_2(1+sqrt(2)),\n\n    Dsc_m/(Lsc_m+1)^(rho_sil) -> infinity.             (4)\n\nConsequently the conjunction of ALL quadratic primitive-energy tests and\nALL single-cut primitive tests cannot imply the proposed silver-power\ncapacity bound, or a constant multiple of that bound. The remaining\nsimplex/projection geometry is essential to any proof by such testing.\nNo claim is made here that V_(Gsc_m)>=0 for arbitrary nonlinear functions.\n\n## 1. Meaning and necessity of the cut tests\n\nFor a selected step b>1 put A=1+sum before it and B=1+sum after it;\nthe total capacity is D=A+B+b-1. Projection onto C=[-v,u], with\nu>0, v=(b-1)u/B and initial point Au, has gradient u on the entire\nprefix, lands at -Bv after the selected step, then has gradient -v\non the entire suffix and ends at -v. Its fixed legal function is\n\n    F_C(x)=max_(z in C)(xz-z^2/2),  F_C'=P_C.\n\nEvery sample has f_i=x_i g_i-g_i^2/2. Expanding the squared-distance\ntelescope in (2) gives\n\n    V_h=x_0^2-(x_n+Sg_n)^2\n       =A^2u^2-D^2(b-1)^2u^2/B^2.\n\nHence primitive validity requires D(b-1)<=AB. For b<=1 its positive-\npart version is automatic. This proves necessity of the tests in (3),\nincluding all function values, independently of earlier findings.\n\n## 2. All cuts in the two-copy ruler pass\n\nAbbreviate c=c_sc, a=a_sc, s_j=ssc_j, b=bsc_j. For j>=17,\n\n    s_j/b=[1-(2/c)^j]/(c-2)>=eta,  eta=13/8.          (5)\n\nIndeed (2/c)^3<1/2 and (2/c)^2<4/5 imply (2/c)^17<1/40.\nEvery step b>1 has j>=18. A step b<=1 satisfies (3) strictly because\nthe right side is positive.\n\nFor a fixed level j<m, the first occurrence of b in Gsc_m is preceded\nby exactly Hsc_j, and the last occurrence is followed by exactly Hsc_j.\nThe two-copy word is palindromic. Thus the prefix sum at any occurrence\nlies between s_j and 2s_m-s_j-b. For fixed total step sum, the product\nof the two cut masses is concave in the prefix sum, and the two endpoint\nvalues agree. Its minimum is therefore\n\n    (1+s_j)(1+2s_m-s_j-b).\n\nPut s=s_j and L=2s_m. The margin over the cut left side is\n\n    (1+s)(1+L-s-b)-(1+L)(b-1).\n\nIts derivative in L is s+2-b>0 by (5). Since m>=j+1,\nL>=2s_(j+1)=4s+2b. At this minimum L the margin is\n\n    3s^2-3sb-2b^2+8s+2b+2.\n\nThe quadratic part is increasing for s>=eta b and is at least\n\n    (3eta^2-3eta-2)b^2=(67/64)b^2>0.\n\nEvery margin is therefore strictly positive. This proves (a).\n\n## 3. The exact quadratic energy and its chronological moment\n\nFix scalar curvature lambda in(0,1], initial point1, and\nf(x)=lambda x^2/2. For ANY physical schedule h define\n\n    P_i=product_(t<i)(1-lambda h_t),\n    Q=|P_n|, tau=lambda sum h_i,\n    R=lambda sum_(i<n) h_i P_i^2.\n\nThe quantity R includes every actual prefix product; order has not been\ndiscarded. Direct substitution into (2), or the identity\n1-Q^2=sum_i(2lambda h_i-lambda^2 h_i^2)P_i^2, gives\n\n    V_h=1-[1+(1+lambda)tau+tau^2]Q^2-(1-lambda)R.     (6)\n\nFor the single word Hsc_j write Q_j,R_j,tau_j, and put\nz_j=lambda bsc_j. The exact concatenation recurrences are\n\n    Q_(j+1)=|1-z_j| Q_j^2,\n    R_(j+1)=R_j+Q_j^2[(1-z_j)^2 R_j+z_j],\n    tau_(j+1)=2tau_j+z_j.                             (7)\n\nThese formulas include the middle step and both copies in their actual\norder. For two copies of Hsc_m the corresponding quantities are\n\n    Q_double=Q_m^2,\n    R_double=R_m(1+Q_m^2),\n    tau_double=2tau_m.                                (8)\n\n## 4. Any normalized-small-step word has nonnegative quadratic energy\n\nSuppose every z_i=lambda h_i lies in[0,1]. During a chronological\nprefix let tau,R,Q have their analogous partial meanings and set\n\n    B_pot=R+(1+tau+tau^2)Q^2.\n\nOn adding a step z in[0,1], use R'=R+zQ^2, tau'=tau+z,\nand (Q')^2=(1-z)^2Q^2. Exact expansion yields\n\n    B_pot'-B_pot\n      =-Q^2[tau^2 z(2-z)+tau z^2(3-2z)+z^3(1-z)]<=0.\n\nInitially B_pot=1, so it remains at most1. Each partial product has\nmagnitude at least the final product, hence R>=tau Q^2. Equation (6)\ncan now be written\n\n    V_h=1-B_pot+lambda(R-tau Q^2)>=0.                 (9)\n\nThis argument applies to any order and any physical h with lambda h<=1;\nit does not require physical h<=1.\n\n## 5. Controlling the moment before and at first activation\n\nLet I be the first level for which t=z_I>1, and put J=I+1. Then\nI>=18 and 1<t<=c. For all j>=17, (5) gives tau_j>=eta z_j.\n\nFirst bound R_I. Put z=t/c in(1/c,1], so z is the central step of\nHsc_I. All normalized steps inside Hsc_(I-1) are at most z/c. If\nq=Q_(I-1)^2, the squared-distance telescope there gives\n\n    R_(I-1)<=(1-q)/(2-z/c).\n\nAlso q<=exp(-2tau_(I-1))<=exp(-2eta z), since those factors are in[0,1].\nSet C=1/(2-z/c). Substituting into (7) and dropping a nonpositive term,\n\n    R_I<=C+C z^2(1-1/c)q\n        <=[c/(2c-1)] [1+(1-1/c)/(eta^2 exp(2))]\n        <(13/21)[1+512/15379]<2/3.                   (10)\n\nHere z^2 exp(-2eta z)<=1/(eta^2 exp(2)) follows by differentiating,\nand exp(2)>7 follows from its power series. The final rational comparison\nuses 13*512<15379.\n\nThe complete product on Hsc_I satisfies Q_I<=exp(-eta t).\nIts first activated extension therefore has\n\n    z_J Q_J<=c t(t-1)exp(-2eta t)\n             <=3c/(16eta^3)=1248/10985<k_0:=1/8.     (11)\n\nUse exp(2eta t)>(2eta t)^3/6 and (t-1)/t^2<=1/4 for this bound.\nMeanwhile (7) and (10) give\n\n    R_J<2/3+exp(-2eta t)[(2/3)(t-1)^2+t]<35/48.      (12)\n\nTo check the second inequality, put B(t)=(2/3)(t-1)^2+t. The polynomial\n2B-B'=(4t^2-6t+5)/3 is positive, so B(t)exp(-2eta t) decreases\nfor t>=1, as 2eta>2. Its value at1 is exp(-13/4)<1/16.\nThe last bound follows from exp(3)>1+3+9/2+27/6+81/24>16.\n\n## 6. Uniform moment control at every later level\n\nFor j>=J put w_j=z_j Q_j. Here z_j>=c>2. Equation (7) implies\n\n    w_(j+1)=c(1-1/z_j)w_j^2<=c w_j^2.\n\nWith theta_0=c k_0=13/40, (11) therefore gives\n\n    w_j<=k_0 theta_0^(j-J),\n    sum_(j>=J) w_j^2<=k_0^2/(1-theta_0^2)=25/1431.  (13)\n\nThe moment recurrence also gives\n\n    R_(j+1)-R_j<=w_j^2(R_j+1/z_j)\n                         <=w_j^2(R_j+1/c).\n\nThus U_j=R_j+1/c obeys U_(j+1)<=(1+w_j^2)U_j. By (12)--(13),\n\n    R_j+1/c\n      <[35/48+5/13]/[1-25/1431]\n      <3/4+5/13,\n\nso R_j<3/4. We used product(1+w_j^2)<=exp(sum w_j^2)\n<=1/[1-sum w_j^2]. The final exact comparison is\n695*1431=994545<995448=59*12*1406.\n\nAlso tau_j<=z_j/(c-2)=5z_j/3 and Q_j^2<=k_0^2/z_j^2. Consequently\n\n    [1+(1+lambda)tau_j+tau_j^2]Q_j^2\n      <=(1/64)[1/c^2+10/(3c)+25/9]<1/8.             (14)\n\nFor the last simple inequality, the bracket is less than\n1+4/3+3=16/3<8. This bound is uniform in lambda, its activation\nphase t, and the horizon.\n\n## 7. Every quadratic passes for the two-copy schedule\n\nIf m<=I, every normalized step in Gsc_m is at most1, so (9) applies.\nIf m>=J, (13)--(14) give Q_m^2<=1/(64c^2)<1/256 and R_m<3/4.\nUsing (8),\n\n    R_double < (3/4)(1+1/256)=771/1024.\n\nFurther,\n\n    [1+(1+lambda)tau_double+tau_double^2]Q_double^2\n      <=4[1+(1+lambda)tau_m+tau_m^2]Q_m^4\n      <(1/2)Q_m^2<1/512.\n\nEquation (6) therefore yields the explicit positive margin\n\n    V_(Gsc_m)>1-771/1024-1/512=251/1024>0             (15)\n\nfor a unit scalar initial point once m>=J. Curvature lambda=0 gives\nenergy0 and is harmless. Before activation (9) supplies nonnegativity.\n\nFor an arbitrary finite-dimensional convex quadratic with a minimizer,\ntranslate that minimizer to zero, subtract the minimum value, and\northogonally diagonalize its positive semidefinite Hessian. Smoothness\nat most1 places every eigenvalue in[0,1]. The GD trajectory and (2)\nseparate across these eigendirections, with each scalar energy multiplied\nby the squared initial coordinate. The proved scalar nonnegativity\ntherefore proves (b), including singular Hessians and arbitrary starts.\n\n## 8. Capacity comparison and what remains open\n\nFrom (1),\n\n    Dsc_m ~ [2a_sc/(c_sc-2)] c_sc^m,\n    Lsc_m+1=2^(m+1)-1.\n\nSince c_sc=13/5>1+sqrt(2), dividing by\n(Lsc_m+1)^(rho_sil) proves (4). Thus this family passes the ENTIRE\nquadratic primitive-energy class and every one-cut projection test while\nits capacity exceeds any fixed multiple of the proposed silver-power\nbound for all sufficiently large m.\n\nThis is a concrete failure of that necessary-test closure, not a\ncounterexample to the primitive-capacity conjecture. No universal\nprimitive certificate for Gsc_m is supplied. If these schedules fail\nprimitivity, their failure must use geometry beyond all quadratics and\nthe displayed single-cut functions. The strict simplex norm game is an\nappropriate remaining interface because it retains that full geometry;\nan equivalent formulation alone does not settle its capacity bound.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "workers/max3/research/c29_all_quadratic_cut_capacity_obstruction.md", "status": "Complete self-contained unverified candidate; no new solver or verifier call.", "target_claim": "All quadratic primitive-energy tests plus all primitive single-cut inequalities imply a silver-power capacity bound.", "assumptions_satisfied": ["Every cut margin is strictly positive.", "The actual chronological intrinsic energy is nonnegative for every scalar curvature in[0,1].", "Orthogonal diagonalization covers every finite-dimensional convex quadratic."], "failed_conclusion": "The tested capacity divided by (length+1)^rho_sil is unbounded.", "impact": "A general primitive-capacity proof needs coupled nonlinear projection geometry beyond these tests. No universal certificate or actual primitive counterexample is supplied.", "new_mechanism": "The exact quadratic prefix moment R is controlled, not merely the endpoint polynomial. A small-step potential and first-activation ruler recurrence give R_j<3/4 and a uniform positive energy margin after activation.", "confidence": 0.97}, "glossary": {"Gsc_m": "The physical schedule consisting of two consecutive copies of Hsc_m.", "Lsc_m": "Its length 2(2^m-1).", "Dsc_m": "Its physical shifted mass 1+2ssc_m.", "Hsc_m": "Ruler word Hsc_0=(), Hsc_(j+1)=(Hsc_j,a_sc c_sc^j,Hsc_j).", "c_sc": "13/5.", "a_sc": "c_sc^(-17).", "bsc_j": "Physical level-j checkpoint a_sc c_sc^j.", "ssc_m": "Physical sum a_sc(c_sc^m-2^m)/(c_sc-2).", "rho_sil": "The proposed capacity exponent log_2(1+sqrt(2)).", "Q_j": "Absolute complete scalar quadratic product on Hsc_j at curvature lambda.", "R_j": "The chronological moment lambda times sum_(i in Hsc_j) h_i P_i(lambda)^2.", "tau_j": "lambda ssc_j.", "z_j": "lambda bsc_j.", "w_j": "z_j Q_j.", "theta_0": "13/40.", "k_0": "1/8.", "B_pot": "R+(1+tau+tau^2)Q^2 for the normalized-small-step calculation."}}
{"id": "66067b85d1228261", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T15:45:25.113611+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "The four-witness local silver test does NOT itself globalize: the positive schedule (2,2sqrt2-7/5,7/5) has exactly silver capacity and satisfies all four F_Q,F_B,F_C,F_N inequalities strictly, yet an endpoint-cut core witness refutes it. Any global scalar elimination must retain the endpoint cuts.", "evidence": "Definitions and full witnesses are in the self-contained candidate local_memory/terminal_credit_silver_local.md, shared in5b4359baa88bf3e4 and500d209094028aef; here the exact parameter countercheck is explicit. Put z=(8+12sqrt2)/25<1 because288<289. Direct expansion gives F_Q=1-z²>0,F_B=(151-6sqrt2)/18>0,F_C=9-z²>0,F_N=(151-66sqrt2)/9>0. Botheta(u-1)<1 andr(w-1)<1 hold. Nonetheless f'(x)=clip(x,-1/(1+2sqrt2),1), startingx0=1, realizes firststep2 then the prescribed tail on its negative affine ray and ends at its negative threshold. All values obeyfi=gi xi-gi²/2 andx3=g3, hence everyQi3=0 andV=1-[(3+2sqrt2)/(1+2sqrt2)]²<0. This refutes a global sufficiency claim for those four scalar inequalities, not the actual signed-state theorem.", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "local_memory/terminal_credit_silver_local.md", "finding_ids": ["5b4359baa88bf3e4", "500d209094028aef"], "branch_id": "max4_terminal_credit_three_step_global_elimination", "status": "complete_candidate_unverified", "fact_submit_calls": 0}, "glossary": {"F_Q,F_B,F_C,F_N": "The four displayed scalar witness expressions, with all definitions repeated in the evidence.", "eta": "Negative Huber slope ratio(v-1)/(w+1).", "z": "Exact normalized quadratic endpoint factor(8+12sqrt2)/25."}}
{"id": "4a6bed048e4235d9", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T16:05:14.255809+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "Exact phase-resonance counterexample to automatic elliptic metric averaging: for every a>0 and c=13/5, there are arbitrarily small z=rho exp(i pi/3) with Re(z)>|z|² such that a tail product product_(j=J)^(m-1)|cos(arg R_j(z))| stays >9/10 for EVERY m>=J. The radius is uniquely defined by a strictly monotone dyadic weighted-argument equation. These spectral parameters are NOT asserted to have legal prefixes.", "evidence": "# Exact dyadic resonances prevent automatic metric isotropization\n\nStatus: complete unverified geometric counterexample. It does not assert\nthat the resulting smooth-convex prefixes are legal. All phase identities\nand estimates used below are derived here; no earlier unverified finding\nis a premise. This diagnoses a proposed step in the elliptic-prefix program.\n\n## 1. Recursive phases and the failed proposed conclusion\n\nLet c=13/5 and let a>0 be any fixed number. Put b_j=a c^j and\nH_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,b_j,H_j). For a nonreal z in the upper half plane set\n\n    p_j(z)=product_(t=0)^(j-1)(1-b_t z)^(2^(j-t-1)),\n    R_j(z)=p_j(z)(1-b_j z).\n\nIn an elliptic prefix the same2x2 metric M is rotated at each recursive\ncopy. Averaging a cycle over all copies of H_r inside H_m attenuates its\ntraceless metric component by\n\n    Gamma_(r,m)(z)=product_(j=r)^(m-1)|cos(arg R_j(z))|.    (1)\n\nThe proposed generic repair was to assert Gamma_(r,m)(z)->0 for every fixed\nnonreal z. We construct counterexamples with Re(z)/|z|=1/2 and\nRe(z)-|z|^2>0, in the very wide-angle range where circular interpolation\nis obstructed on the recursive family. For these parameters, there is a\nfinite J such that\n\n    Gamma_(J,m)(z)>9/10       for EVERY m>=J.              (2)\n\nThus late copies can preserve almost all metric anisotropy forever. This\ndoes not preclude a different argument using earlier cycles or complete\ninterpolation; it refutes the unconditional phase-decay step itself.\n\n## 2. A uniquely defined sequence of small resonant parameters\n\nFix theta=pi/3. For rho>0 let z=rho exp(i theta) and define the continuous\nprincipal factor arguments\n\n    phi_j(rho)=arg(1-b_j rho exp(i theta)) in(-2pi/3,0),\n    Phi(rho)=sum_(j=0)^infinity 2^(-j-1) phi_j(rho).\n\nThe series converges uniformly in rho because |phi_j|<=2pi/3 and the\nweights sum to one. Each phi_j is strictly decreasing: direct\ndifferentiation of its argument gives\n\n    phi_j'(rho)=-b_j sin(theta)/|1-b_j rho exp(i theta)|^2<0.\n\nConsequently Phi is continuous and strictly decreasing. For rho tending\nto zero each fixed phi_j tends to zero; for rho tending to infinity each\ntends to theta-pi=-2pi/3. In either limit, a finite-head/uniform-tail split\nof the summable series justifies taking the limit. Hence Phi maps\n(0,infinity) onto(-2pi/3,0).\n\nFor every integer k>=1 there is therefore a unique rho_k>0 such that\n\n    Phi(rho_k)=-pi/2^k.                                  (3)\n\nThese radii decrease to zero. To see the last claim, for every epsilon>0\nthe fixed value Phi(epsilon)<0 is eventually smaller than -pi/2^k;\nstrict decrease of Phi then implies rho_k<epsilon.\n\nChoose k sufficiently large that rho_k<1/2, and set z_k=rho_k exp(i pi/3).\nThis is a precise implicit definition with uniqueness, not a numerical\napproximation. Its spectral parameters obey\n\n    Re(z_k)/|z_k|=1/2,\n    Re(z_k)-|z_k|^2=rho_k(1/2-rho_k)>0.                  (4)\n\nThus the counterexample is not obtained by leaving the usual circular\ncocoercivity disk. No claim of full prefix legality follows from(4).\n\n## 3. Exact phase formula at every recursive level\n\nFix one z=z_k and abbreviate phi_j=phi_j(rho_k), Phi=Phi(rho_k). Use an\nunwrapped argument for p_j and R_j obtained by summing factor arguments.\nFrom their exact multiplicities,\n\n    arg_unwrapped(p_j)\n       =sum_(t<j)2^(j-t-1)phi_t\n       =2^j Phi-sum_(ell=0)^infinity2^(-ell-1)phi_(j+ell).\n\nTherefore\n\n    arg_unwrapped(R_j)=2^j Phi+epsilon_j,\n    epsilon_j=phi_j-sum_(ell=0)^infinity2^(-ell-1)phi_(j+ell). (5)\n\nAs j increases, b_j increases and phi_j strictly decreases to\nphi_infinity=-2pi/3. Thus\n\n    0<epsilon_j<=phi_j-phi_infinity.\n\nWhen b_j|z|>=2, write\n\n    1-b_j z=(-b_j z)(1-1/(b_j z)).\n\nThe second factor has positive real part and positive imaginary part. Its\nargument is phi_j-phi_infinity and is at most\n\n    |1/(b_j z)|/[1-|1/(b_j z)|]<=2/(b_j|z|).\n\nHere arg(1+w)<=|w|/(1-|w|) for |w|<1 follows directly by bounding the\ntangent by |Im w|/(1-|w|) and using arctan(t)<=t. The argument sum has\nno branch ambiguity because phi_j lies between phi_infinity and zero.\nHence\n\n    0<epsilon_j<=2/(b_j|z|)      whenever b_j|z|>=2.       (6)\n\nBy(3), for every j>=k the term2^j Phi is an integer multiple of pi.\nEquations(5),(6) give\n\n    |cos(arg R_j)|=|cos(epsilon_j)|.\n\n## 4. A uniform positive lower bound for all later averaging products\n\nChoose a finite J>=k with b_J|z|>=8. Then for j>=J,\n\n    0<epsilon_j<= (1/4)c^(-(j-J))<=1/4.\n\nFor0<=t<=1/4, integration of sin(s)<=s gives cos t>=1-t^2/2. Also\nlog(1-u)>=-2u for0<=u<=1/2, by integrating -1/(1-u)>=-2.\nIt follows that log(cos t)>=-t^2. Consequently, for every m>=J,\n\n    log Gamma_(J,m)\n       =sum_(j=J)^(m-1)log(cos epsilon_j)\n       >=-sum_(j=J)^infinity epsilon_j^2\n       >=-4/[b_J^2 |z|^2(1-c^(-2))]\n       >=-169/2304.\n\nThus\n\n    Gamma_(J,m)>=exp(-169/2304)\n               >=1-169/2304=2135/2304>9/10,\n\nproving(2). The finite products decrease, so they also have a strictly\npositive infinite limit. This proof handles exact resonance; it does not\ninvoke equidistribution, generic parameter behavior, or numerical phases.\n\n## 5. Why(1) is the actual metric attenuation factor\n\nEvery query position of H_m is a product of a subset of R_0,...,R_(m-1):\nthe right copy multiplies all positions of the left-copy pattern by its\nbridge multiplier R_(m-1). Fix a cycle in H_J and average its transported\ncopies in H_m after dividing each inequality by the squared modulus of\nits nonzero starting multiplier. All rotations commute, so this is the\nsuccessive average of identity conjugation and conjugation by the rotation\narg R_j for each j=J,...,m-1.\n\nOn a symmetric2x2 matrix, the scalar part tr(M)I/2 is unchanged. Identify\nits traceless part [[u,v],[v,-u]] with the complex number u+i v. Rotation\nconjugation multiplies that number by exp(+/-2i arg R_j), with the sign\ndepending only on the convention. Averaging multiplies its norm by\n|(1+exp(2i arg R_j))/2|=|cos(arg R_j)|. This proves(1) directly and ties\nthe counterexample to the full common-metric cycle interface.\n\n## 6. Honest remaining question\n\nThe radii in(3) are explicit by a uniquely solvable scalar monotone equation,\nbut no legality of all smooth-convex prefix inequalities has been proved\nat them. A positive long-horizon construction must still choose one M>0,\ncheck every cycle and value, and give an actual terminal payoff. An\nobstruction must use additional geometry or quantitative finite-level\nconstraints; the copies alone do not force the metric to become circular.\n\nMatlas retrieval returned unrelated statements in arXiv:2210.15480,\nmath/0701272 and1303.2019. None is imported. All estimates above are textual\nand symbolic; there was no root-finding computation or numerical search.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"plan_id": "max2_elliptic_phase_resonance_test", "parent_plan": "max2_elliptic_complex_metric", "artifact": "workers/max2/research/elliptic_phase_resonance_obstruction.md", "status": "Complete unverified counterexample to proposed phase-decay lemma; full interpolation remains open", "scope": "Geometric averaging identity only, not a smooth-convex counterexample to the target.", "related_finding": "892ca165436ef59c", "verification_allocation": "No new fact_submit."}, "glossary": {"c": "Geometric step ratio13/5.", "a": "Any positive initial step scale.", "b_j": "a c^j.", "p_j": "Full recursive schedule polynomial at depth j.", "R_j": "p_j(z)(1-b_j z), the multiplier between copies.", "phi_j": "Principal argument of1-b_j rho exp(i pi/3), between-2pi/3 and0.", "Phi": "Weighted phase sum sum_j2^(-j-1)phi_j.", "rho_k": "Unique positive radius satisfying Phi(rho_k)=-pi/2^k.", "epsilon_j": "Difference between phi_j and the dyadic weighted average of subsequent factor phases.", "Gamma_(J,m)": "Product of |cos(arg R_j)| from j=J to m-1."}}
{"id": "4aaee6131a92da0e", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T16:21:36.608711+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "General re-selection cannot keep the supplied full PSD remainder fixed: for every n>=2, the unit-step schedule has an E_(1/n) certificate whose signed V certificate has invariant negative coefficient lambda_(0,n)=-1/n, even though its remainder is positive definite on all gradient differences. A different ordinary certificate exists, so this is NOT strict cone enlargement.", "evidence": "# Signed terminal credit: two exact failures of direct repair\n\nStatus: complete candidate calculations, NOT verifier-accepted. No numerical\nPEP or fact_submit call. These results do not decide equality of the signed\nand ordinary primitive cones. In particular, preservation of the entire PSD\nremainder is stronger than preservation of positive semidefiniteness.\n\n## 1. Exact space of multiplier changes preserving the full remainder\n\nFix a nonnegative list h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)), with n>=1, and formal independent\ngradients g_0,...,g_n. Put s=sum h_i, x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t, and\n\n Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\n V_h=2sum_(i<n)h_i(f_i-f_n)+sum_(i<n)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2\n                                               -s(s+1)||g_n||^2.\n\nAn intrinsic certificate is a multiplier array lambda on distinct finite\npairs such that V_h+sum lambda_ij Q_ij cancels the values and has a PSD\nquadratic remainder on ALL independent formal gradients. A signed certificate\nmay have negative coefficients only on i->n. A certificate for\nE_theta=V_h-theta sum_(i<n)h_i Q_(i,n) has an equivalent signed array with\nlambda_(i,n)>=-theta h_i and all other coefficients nonnegative.\n\nDefine the (n+1)-by-(n+1) lower triangular matrix A by\n\n A_ii=1, A_it=h_t if t<i, A_it=0 if t>i.\n\nLet e be the all-one vector and r=A^(-1)e. Direct forward substitution gives\nr_0=1 and r_(i+1)=(1-h_i)r_i. For a change Delta lambda, with zero diagonal,\nput L=diag(Delta lambda e)-Delta lambda. The following parameterization is\nexact, without any sign assumption:\n\n Delta lambda preserves both value cancellation and the ENTIRE quadratic\n remainder if and only if\n\n   L=B A^(-1), B^T=-B, B e=0, B r=0.                 (1)\n\nTo prove this, preserving value coefficients is exactly equality of incoming\nand outgoing Delta flow at each node, equivalently e^T L=0. The row identity\nL e=0 holds by definition. Write H=A-I. The position terms in sum Delta\nlambda Q are g^T L H g. Because the flow is balanced, its squared-difference\nterms are g^T L g. Thus the full quadratic change is g^T L A g, which is\nidentically zero exactly when B=L A is skew-symmetric. The two flow identities\nbecome B r=0 and e^T B=0; skew-symmetry turns the latter into B e=0.\nConversely, these conditions imply both flow identities and a zero quadratic\nchange, and off-diagonal entries Delta lambda_ij=-L_ij recover the array.\n\nIf some step is positive, e and r are independent. The possible B are precisely\nskew maps on the orthogonal complement of span{e,r}; hence the dimension of\nthe space in (1) is (n-1)(n-2)/2. This dimension is about algebraic changes\npreserving a supplied remainder, not about sign-feasible certificates.\n\nIf h_0=1, then r=(1,0,...,0). Therefore B r=0 forces its first column and,\nby skew-symmetry, first row to vanish. The first row of L also vanishes.\nConsequently EVERY outgoing coefficient lambda_(0,j) is invariant under\nevery change preserving the complete remainder and value cancellation.\nIn particular, a negative lambda_(0,n) cannot be repaired in this way.\n\n## 2. An arbitrarily long, strictly PSD example with a negative invariant\n\nTake any integer n>=2, h_i=1 for all i<n, epsilon=theta=1/n, and\nd_k=g_k-g_(k+1). Begin with the nonnegative adjacent array\n\n lambda^0_(k,k+1)=2(k+1)^2,\n lambda^0_(k+1,k)=2(k+1)(k+2), 0<=k<n,\n\nall other entries zero. Direct expansion of the two adjacent Q terms at each\nstep, and summation of the value coefficients, gives\n\n V_h+sum lambda^0_ij Q_ij=sum_(k=0)^(n-1)(k+1)^2||d_k||^2.       (2)\n\nModify the four coefficients by adding\n\n Delta lambda_01=n epsilon,\n Delta lambda_10=(n-1)epsilon,\n Delta lambda_0n=-epsilon,\n Delta lambda_1n=epsilon.                             (3)\n\nThese are distinct ordered pairs for n>=2. When n=2, the 1n change is simply\nadded to the existing adjacent coefficient. The flow of (3) is zero at every\nnode. Its full quadratic is exactly\n\n epsilon[n Q_01+(n-1)Q_10-Q_0n+Q_1n]\n   =-epsilon <d_0,sum_(k=1)^(n-1)(n-k)d_k>.           (4)\n\nFor a direct check, the value terms cancel. The quadratic left side divided\nby epsilon is (g_0-g_1) dot [sum_(j=1)^n g_j-n g_1]. Writing\ng_j-g_1=-sum_(k=1)^(j-1)d_k yields the right side.\n\nThe resulting signed remainder M is therefore the following sum of squares:\n\n M=sum_(k=1)^(n-1)(k+1)^2\n       ||d_k-epsilon(n-k)d_0/[2(k+1)^2]||^2\n   +[1-epsilon^2/4 sum_(k=1)^(n-1)(n-k)^2/(k+1)^2]||d_0||^2.   (5)\n\nThe last coefficient is strictly greater than 3/4. Indeed,\nepsilon=1/n, (n-k)^2<n^2, and\nsum_(j=2)^n 1/j^2 < sum_(j=2)^n 1/[j(j-1)] <1.\nThus M is positive definite in all the gradient differences and PSD on the\ncomplete original formal gradient space, with exactly the constant-gradient\nkernel. The only negative signed multiplier is lambda_0n=-1/n.\n\nFor the ACTUAL E_theta certificate, add theta h_i=1/n to every incoming\nterminal multiplier. The 0n entry becomes zero, and every other entry is\nnonnegative. These additions cancel the -theta J_h in E_theta, so the\nremainder remains exactly (5). This is a full certificate, including the\nactual function-value cancellation, for 0<theta<1.\n\nNevertheless no ordinary nonnegative certificate for V_h can have this SAME\nremainder M: by Section 1 its 0n multiplier would still be -1/n. The ordinary\ncertificate (2) does exist and has a different PSD remainder. Thus this is\nNOT a strict enlargement example. It disproves a general repair that keeps\nthe given matrix fixed, even for strictly positive difference matrices and\narbitrarily many positive steps. Any successful general repair must genuinely\nchange that matrix or use a different primal argument.\n\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"branch_id": "max4_credit_fixed_remainder_reselection", "target_claim": "Every signed incoming-terminal certificate can be repaired without changing its full quadratic remainder.", "status": "refuted_by_complete_unverified_candidate", "assumptions_satisfied": ["0<theta=1/n<1", "All actual E_theta multipliers are nonnegative", "All function values cancel", "Full PSD matrix has exactly the constant-gradient kernel", "Arbitrarily many strictly positive steps"], "failed_conclusion": "No ordinary V_h certificate has the same remainder, because its outgoing row0 coefficients are invariant.", "impact": "A general PSD-preserving repair must change the matrix; no conclusion about equality of the primitive and signed cones.", "artifact": "local_memory/credit_reselection_repairs.md", "fact_submit_calls": 0, "numerical_pep_calls": 0}, "glossary": {"A": "Lower triangular chronological matrix with diagonal1 and A_it=h_t for t<i, local to the multiplier-change theorem.", "e": "All-one vector in R^(n+1).", "r": "Quadratic gradient vector A^(-1)e, r_0=1 and r_(i+1)=(1-h_i)r_i, local to this theorem.", "L": "Row Laplacian of a signed multiplier change, diag(Delta lambda e)-Delta lambda.", "B": "Skew-symmetric matrix L A in the exact fixed-remainder parameterization.", "d_k": "Independent adjacent formal gradient difference g_k-g_(k+1).", "V_h": "Intrinsic primitive energy explicitly defined in the evidence.", "E_theta": "V_h-theta sum_(i<n)h_i Q_(i,n).", "epsilon": "Signed perturbation size1/n in the unit-step example."}}
{"id": "347ec0d726695de2", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T16:22:00.474965+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "The energy-preserving terminal-core shear of a projection witness is not a legal repair in general. On the explicit Huber run h=(5,10), x=(1,-4,6), g=(1,-1,1), it preserves V_h=-440 and imposes both a core endpoint and zero incoming optimum slacks, but creates the old finite residual Q'_10=5/8>0.", "evidence": "Fix h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1))>=0, s=sum h_i, x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i, f_i=f(x_i), g_i=grad f(x_i). Define Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 and V_h=2sum_(i<n)h_i(f_i-f_n)+sum_(i<n)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-s(s+1)||g_n||^2.\n\n## 3. A negative-energy projection witness does not admit the simple core shear\n\nFor a compact convex K containing 0, put\nF_K(x)=max_(g in K)[<x,g>-||g||^2/2]. Its gradient is P_K(x): the projection\nnormal inequalities imply <P_K(x)-P_K(z),x-z>>=||P_K(x)-P_K(z)||^2, and the\nmaximizing formula then proves differentiability and 1-smoothness. It is\nconvex with minimum F_K(0)=0. Along its GD trajectory define\ny_i=x_i-g_i. Its values satisfy f_i=<g_i,x_i>-||g_i||^2/2, and hence\n\n Q_ij=<g_i-g_j,y_j>,\n V_h=||x_0||^2-||y_n+(1+s)g_n||^2.                  (6)\n\nLet P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, D=1+s. A tempting exact reset sets v=-y_n/D and\n\n X_i=x_i+P_i v, G_i=g_i-v,\n Y_i=X_i-G_i=y_i+(P_i+1)v,\n F_i=<G_i,X_i>-||G_i||^2/2.\n\nThe GD recursion holds, all incoming optimum residuals are zero, Y_n=0, and\nthe algebraic value of V_h is preserved exactly: X_0=x_0 and\nY_n+D G_n=y_n+D g_n. However, the finite residuals become\n\n Q'_ij=Q_ij+(P_j+1)<g_i-g_j,v>,                      (7)\n\nwhich can be positive. Here is a fixed legal negative-energy input.\n\nUse K=[-1,1], so F_K is the unit-threshold symmetric Huber function. Take\nh=(5,10) and x_0=1. The full points, gradients and normals are\n\n x=(1,-4,6), g=(1,-1,1), y=(0,-3,5), f=(1/2,7/2,11/2).\n\nEvery item follows from the explicit Huber function and both GD equations\nhold. Formula (6) gives V_h=1-(6+15)^2=-440. The reset has\nD=16 and v=-5/16, and its first and second gradients are 21/16 and -11/16,\nwhile Y_0=-5/16. Thus\n\n Q'_10=(G_1-G_0)Y_0=(-2)(-5/16)=5/8>0.              (8)\n\nIt is not a legal smooth-convex transcript despite keeping V_h=-440,\nsetting Y_2=0, and setting every incoming optimum residual to zero.\nNo conclusion about other witnesses for this schedule follows. This example\nspecifically prevents inferring simultaneous terminal saturation from this\nenergy-preserving shear.\n\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"branch_id": "max4_credit_core_projection_shear", "target_claim": "Subtracting (P_i+1)y_n/D from every projection normal yields a legal terminal-core witness with the same primitive defect.", "status": "refuted_by_complete_unverified_candidate", "assumptions_satisfied": ["Fixed globally legal convex1-smooth Huber input", "Negative intrinsic energy", "All incoming optimum slacks initially zero", "Same physical GD schedule"], "failed_conclusion": "The transformed finite pair1->0 violates smooth-convex interpolation.", "artifact": "local_memory/credit_reselection_repairs.md", "fact_submit_calls": 0, "numerical_pep_calls": 0}, "glossary": {"F_K": "Projection function max_(g in K)(<x,g>-||g||^2/2).", "K": "The compact convex gradient set, equal to[-1,1] in the counterexample.", "P_i": "Chronological step sum sum_(t<i)h_t, local to the shear.", "D": "Capacity1+sum h_i.", "y_i": "Original projection normal x_i-g_i.", "v": "Core-shear vector -y_n/D.", "X_i,G_i,Y_i,F_i": "Transformed point, gradient, normal and assigned value defined explicitly in the evidence."}}
{"id": "8010ea721f41d0d3", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T16:23:43.815028+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "A positive retained checkpoint height need not yield ANY later error: an explicit two-block scalar Huber prefix has delta_second=A²/(4M1)>0, yet its second checkpoint lands exactly at the true minimizer and every nonnegative tail has zero error. This refutes the positive-height-only outgoing bound as a generally attained payoff.", "evidence": "Fix M0,M1>=1,A>0. Let p=1/M0>0 and q=-A/(2M1)<0. Define f(0)=0 and f'(x)=max(q,min(p,x)). At the second checkpoint x_j=-A(M1+1)/(2M1), so its proximal point x_j-q=-A/2 and delta_second=f(x_j)-q²/2=q(x_j-q)=A²/(4M1)>0. The next step 1+M1 sends x_j-(1+M1)q=0. The simultaneous optimum bound is0 because X_tail=0. Therefore the first upper bound cannot be claimed as attained independently of retained location and optimum constraints. This is a counterexample to that shortcut, not to existence of a different hard family.", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"target_claim": "The second-checkpoint-only positive-height upper bound is always an attainable terminal value.", "status": "refuted", "assumptions_satisfied": ["One fixed convex1-smooth scalar function.", "Every original block query and both checkpoints retained.", "Strictly positive second-checkpoint height.", "True closest minimizer0, initial distance1.", "Arbitrary nonnegative tail."], "failed_conclusion": "Any positive value surviving after the actual second checkpoint.", "impact": "Must couple the checkpoint height to its location and all optimum constraints.", "plan_id": "max_nearcritical_positive_height", "artifact": "research/max_near_critical_ruler_audit.md"}, "glossary": {"M0": "One plus first ordinary block mass.", "M1": "One plus second ordinary block mass.", "p_hub": "Positive Huber slope1/M0, locally p.", "q_hub": "Negative Huber slope-A/(2M1), locally q.", "delta_second": "f(x_j)-q_hub²/2 at the second checkpoint.", "T_tail": "Sum of any subsequent nonnegative tail; locally T."}}
{"id": "0d86939f706d22ab", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T17:13:11.923808+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "Arbitrarily flat padding cannot universally saturate incoming-terminal interpolation while retaining the old queried x_i,g_i coordinates. The original V=-128483/4<0. Thus taking a huge common gradient with differences tending to0 cannot fix this witness.", "evidence": "Let y_i=x_i-g_i, R_i=sum_(t>=i)h_t, w_i=(y_i-y_n)/R_i. All incoming-terminal Q=0 implies Q_ij=<G_i-G_j,Y_j-Y_n>. Append arbitrary GD coordinates z_i,c_i and put e_i=c_i-c_n, rho=max||e_i||, U_j=sum_(t>=j)h_t e_t-e_j. {"id": "cd5a12a623204971", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T17:26:34.299001+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "Scope countertest: the factor31360/38557 below K for fresh ANTIALIGNED relays cannot be extended unchanged to positive adjacent coefficients. {"id": "35d2f3dd8f474b19", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T17:35:19.333539+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "Concrete applicability failure for candidate22347ffeb25f8830: on the actual near-critical word, z_m=1-5/m+2i/sqrt(m) is inside the cocoercivity disk and its angle ratio tends to1, yet the polynomial of the queried prefix with the actual last d_0 step removed satisfies log|p_m^-(z_m)|>=2^m/(3m) for all sufficiently large m. Thus the old safe-sector polynomial damping estimate is false on this family; its joint-mixture theorem cannot be imported. This is a polynomial counterexample, NOT a legal witness or a conclusion about W.", "evidence": "# Applicability audit: the old normal-mixture proof does not transfer\n\nStatus: self-contained UNVERIFIED counterexample to a polynomial estimate,\nnot a counterexample to the target theorem and not a legal GD witness.\nNo verifier or numerical allocation is used.\n\nFix an integer m>1, lambda=1+sqrt(2), c=lambda(1+1/m),\na=(1-1/m)/c, d_j=1+a c^j, and H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,d_j,H_j).\nAll parameters remain fixed within this horizon. Write N=2^m-1,\np_m(z)=product_(j=0)^(m-1)(1-d_j z)^(2^(m-j-1)), and let p_m^-(z)\nbe the polynomial of H_m with its ORIGINAL last step d_0 deleted.\nThus p_m=(1-d_0 z)p_m^- as a polynomial identity.\n\nThe old candidate22347ffeb25f8830 concerned fixed c=13/5, a=c^(-17),\nsteps a c^j (without the added1), the FULL H_m queried prefix, and an\nAPPENDED unit step. Its Section7 really did use a common cycle and allowed\njoint repair of individually illegal modes. The issue is its hypotheses,\nnot separability of that proof.\n\n## An explicit failure of the supposedly safe sector bound\n\nSet z_m=1-5/m+2i/sqrt(m). For m>25 its parameters satisfy\n\n    alpha=1-5/m, sigma=|z_m|^2=1-6/m+25/m^2,\n    kappa=alpha-sigma=1/m-25/m^2>0.\n\nConsequently z_m lies inside the cocoercivity disk sigma<alpha. Also\nalpha/|z_m| tends to1 and is at least2/3 for all sufficiently large m.\nNevertheless\n\n    log|p_m^-(z_m)| >= 2^m/(3m)                     (A)\n\nfor all sufficiently large m. In particular the old purely polynomial\nestimate |z||p_m(z)|^2=O(c^(-m)) on this disk/sector is FALSE for the\nnew word, even with a constant allowed to depend on no other parameter.\n\nHere is a full asymptotic proof with uniform remainder control. For the\nparameters of each horizon extend d_j to all j>=0 and define\n\n    A_j=d_j/(d_j-1)=1+1/(a c^j),\n    L_m(z)=sum_(j>=0)2^(-j-1) log|1-d_j z|.\n\nThe series converges whenever the finitely many factors near1 are nonzero:\nits terms grow at most linearly in j, whereas its weights decay geometrically.\nFor large m, a is bounded away from0 and all A_j<=4. At z=1,\n\n    L_m(1)=log a+log c=log(1-1/m).\n\nPut u_m=z_m-1. Since |u_m|=O(m^(-1/2)), |A_j u_m|<=1/2 for all j\nand sufficiently large m. The power series of log(1+v) has remainder\nafter v-v^2/2 bounded in modulus by 2|v|^3 when |v|<=1/2: bound\nsum_(k>=3)|v|^k/k by |v|^3/(1-|v|). This proves UNIFORMLY in j that\n\n    log|1-d_j z_m|=log(a c^j)\n              +Re(A_j u_m-A_j^2 u_m^2/2)+O(m^(-3/2)).\n\nThe weights sum to1. With D_m=sum 2^(-j-1)A_j and\nJ_m=sum 2^(-j-1)A_j^2, the exact geometric sums are\n\n    D_m=1+1/[a(2-c^(-1))],\n    J_m=1+2/[a(2-c^(-1))]+1/[a^2(2-c^(-2))].\n\nAs Re u_m=-5/m and Re u_m^2=-4/m+25/m^2, it follows that\n\n    m L_m(z_m) -> -1-5D+2J=-4-D1+2D2,\n    D1=lambda^2/(2lambda-1),\n    D2=lambda^4/(2lambda^2-1), D=1+D1, J=1+2D1+D2.\n\nThis limit is strictly greater than3/5. Indeed 12/5<lambda<5/2;\nt^2/(2t-1) increases for t>1, so D1<25/16<8/5. Likewise\nt^4/(2t^2-1) increases for t>1, so\nD2>20736/6575>31/10 (the last inequality is 207360>203825).\nTherefore -4-D1+2D2>-4-8/5+62/10=3/5.\nFor sufficiently large m this gives L_m(z_m)>=1/(2m).\n\nTo pass to the ACTUAL finite product, note that |z_m| stays bounded and\nc stays in a fixed compact interval in (2,infinity), while a is bounded\nabove and away from0. For j>=m and large m,\n\n    log|1-d_j z_m| <= C0+j log c\n\nwith one absolute C0. Hence\n\n    2^m sum_(j>=m)2^(-j-1)log|1-d_j z_m|\n                                  <=C0+(m+1)log c.\n\nIt follows that log|p_m(z_m)|>=2^m/(2m)-O(m). The deleted factor\n|1-d_0 z_m| tends to lambda^(-1)>0; its logarithm stays bounded.\nSince m^2/2^m tends to0, (A) follows.\n\nIn particular kappa |p_m^-(z_m)|^2 also grows without bound. This is\nONLY a scalar polynomial statement. Neither this mode nor a mixture\ncontaining it has been proved legal by this calculation.\n\n## Other nontransferable interfaces\n\nThe old scale argument began at least seventeen small geometric levels\nbefore reaching |z|h~1. Here d_0 tends to sqrt(2), so this assertion fails\nfor fixed nonzero spectral parameters. The old benchmark estimate used\nc^3>16 to obtain a vanishing ratio; now c_m tends to lambda<2^(4/3),\nand even a correctly proved O(c_m^(-m)) bound requires its leading\nconstant to be compared with the exact benchmark.\n\nFinally the retained prefix has length N-1, ending BEFORE the original\nlast d_0 step. The original free displacement is x_(N-1)-d_0 g_(N-1),\nnot a unit step from the end of full H_m. Any new cycle and completion\nestimate must use those actual indices. No whole-normal conclusion on\nthe new family is obtained from the old proof.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"audited_candidate": "22347ffeb25f8830", "guidance": "ea594a814f06f984"}, "glossary": {"lambda": "Silver ratio 1+sqrt(2).", "c_m": "lambda(1+1/m), fixed within horizon m.", "a_m": "(1-1/m)/c_m, fixed within horizon m.", "d_j": "Actual step 1+a_m c_m^j.", "p_m": "Polynomial of H_m, with multiplicities 2^(m-j-1).", "p_m^-": "Polynomial of H_m after deleting the actual last step d_0.", "z_m": "1-5/m+2i/sqrt(m), used only to test the scalar polynomial bound.", "D1": "lambda^2/(2lambda-1).", "D2": "lambda^4/(2lambda^2-1)."}}
{"id": "6973b4cc1da9c16b", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T18:00:00.852880+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "Exact source-interface obstruction at every length n>=2: a valid ordinary full-PSD intrinsic unit-step certificate can have lambda_10=3 but lambda_01=0. Thus no positive diagonal row/column rescaling, similarity, congruence, or destination weighting can turn that supplied actual array into an undirected symmetric Laplacian. The source's noncrossing optimal-vertex theorem is therefore not imported by weighted circulation alone. A different adjacent certificate exists for the same schedule; neither reversal nor re-selection is refuted.", "evidence": "# Source interface audit and an exact directed-certificate obstruction\n\nStatus: complete candidate counterexample to a specified coefficient move,\nNOT verifier-accepted. It does not disprove reversal or certificate re-selection.\nNo numerical or verifier call.\n\n## Source checked and exact applicability limit\n\nYoon--Grimmer, *A Theory of Composition and Duality of Extremal Optimal\nFixed-Point Algorithms*, arXiv:2605.02231v2, Sections2.1--2.4,3.1--3.2,\n4.1--4.2.2, https://arxiv.org/html/2605.02231v2 . The complete HTML was\ndownloaded into this worker's downloads directory and extracted to text.\nThe definitions, inverse-matrix statement, and tree/path proof of Theorem4\nwere read. Matlas title/theorem search returned unrelated results, so no\nMatlas grounding of this exact theorem is claimed.\n\nThe source's Theorem4 characterizes H-dual optimality for optimal VERTEX\nalgorithms for nonexpansive fixed-point residuals. Its arcs form a tree,\nwith one edge from each nonterminal vertex to a larger vertex. Its certificate\nmatrix is symmetric: a negative undirected Laplacian with one diagonal\nanchor. The inverse transform takes order-reversed discrete differences;\nnoncrossing controls their signs. Those hypotheses do not identify an\narbitrary intrinsic GD multiplier array with the source's unique certificate.\n\n## Own exact obstruction to diagonal symmetrization of a supplied array\n\nFor any n>=2, take the physical schedule h_i=1 for i=0,...,n-1. Define\nQ_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 with the actual unit-step GD\nsubstitution, and\n\n V=2sum_(i<n)(f_i-f_n)-n(n+1)||g_n||^2.\n\nEvery gradient g_0,...,g_n is a formal independent vector. Put d_k=g_k-g_(k+1).\nThe ordinary adjacent coefficients\n\n lambda^0_(k,k+1)=2(k+1)^2,\n lambda^0_(k+1,k)=2(k+1)(k+2)\n\ngive the exact full identity\n\n                  V+sum lambda^0_ij Q_ij\n                       =sum_(k<n)(k+1)^2||d_k||^2.       (1)\n\nHere is a direct check. Their net forward flow across edge k is -2(k+1),\nso the values cancel V. On a unit step, the forward Q has gradient part\n-(1/2)||g_k||^2+(1/2)||g_(k+1)||^2-<g_k,g_(k+1)>, while the reverse Q\nhas part (1/2)||g_k||^2+(1/2)||g_(k+1)||^2. Their mixed coefficient is\n-2(k+1)^2. The diagonal g_0 coefficient is1; at an interior index k it\nis k^2+(k+1)^2; at n, after adding V, it is n^2. These are exactly (1).\n\nNow modify just four ORIGINAL ordered coefficients:\n\n Delta lambda_01=-2,\n Delta lambda_10=-1,\n Delta lambda_02=1,\n Delta lambda_12=-1.                                    (2)\n\nAll actual coefficients remain nonnegative: the affected entries become\nlambda_01=0, lambda_10=3, lambda_02=1 and lambda_12=7. The reverse edge\nlambda_21 remains12, and all later adjacent entries retain their positive\nvalues. Every other entry is zero. This is valid for every n>=2, including\narbitrarily many positive steps.\n\nThe added value flow is zero at nodes0,1,2 individually, and elsewhere no\ncoefficient changes. Direct expansion on the first two unit steps gives\n\n          -2Q_01-Q_10+Q_02-Q_12=<d_0,d_1>.               (3)\n\nFor example the corresponding Q_02 gradient part is\n-(1/2)||g_0||^2-<g_0,g_1>+(1/2)||g_2||^2-<g_0,g_2>,\nwhich together with the adjacent formulas verifies every coefficient of\n(3). Thus the FULL repaired ordinary certificate remainder is\n\n M=||d_0||^2+<d_0,d_1>+4||d_1||^2\n                                  +sum_(k=2)^(n-1)(k+1)^2||d_k||^2\n  =||d_0+d_1/2||^2+(15/4)||d_1||^2\n                                  +sum_(k=2)^(n-1)(k+1)^2||d_k||^2.\n                                                                  (4)\n\nThis is PSD on all original independent gradients and strictly positive\nin the difference coordinates, with only the constant-gradient kernel.\n\nNevertheless no positive row/column diagonal rescaling of this ACTUAL\nmultiplier array can make it symmetric. Its support contains the directed\nedge1->0 with weight3, but the reverse edge0->1 has weight0. Multiplication\non the left and right by strictly positive diagonal matrices preserves\nthat asymmetry of zeros, whereas any symmetric matrix must have symmetric\noff-diagonal support. This also excludes diagonal similarity, positive\ncongruence, and destination weighting as ways to turn the given array\ndirectly into an undirected nonnegative Laplacian.\n\nIn particular, the destination weights p_i=i+1 forced by the affine-kernel\nidentity produce mu_10=3 and mu_01=0. They do make a circulation, but do\nnot make the graph reversible or undirected. The distinction is substantive.\n\n## What remains open\n\nThis is a failure of a SPECIFIED map on a supplied valid certificate.\nThe SAME schedule also has the adjacent certificate (1), whose destination-\nweighted array is symmetric. Hence this example explicitly cannot disprove\nexistence of a different well-structured certificate, general reversal, or\nthe same-schedule credit repair conjecture. It shows why weighted circulation\nalone cannot import the source's symmetric anchored-graph theorem.\n\nAn application of that theorem still needs a proved selection or nonlinear\ntransformation that matches the intrinsic V functional, preserves all actual\ncoefficient signs and the full PSD form, and supplies the source's optimal-\nvertex/graph hypotheses. Merely noticing a graph in both settings supplies\nnone of these missing steps. The source is used only for this scope audit;\nthe construction (1)--(4) has a complete independent proof above.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"guidance_id": "66512436ef464e46", "related_findings": ["be38e23574a71815", "5bd01e2b19002a59", "4ad2db6604a59a2c"], "source": "https://arxiv.org/html/2605.02231v2", "local_evidence": "local_memory/source_arc_interface_obstruction.md"}, "glossary": {"lambda_ij": "Actual ordinary interpolation multiplier in the explicit unit-step certificate.", "d_k": "The formal gradient difference g_k-g_(k+1).", "mu_ij": "Destination-weighted array lambda_ij p_j with p_j=j+1 in the unit-step example."}}
{"id": "1219c08ec89c1ac9", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T18:02:52.163976+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "The critical-scalar cancellation theorem cannot drop its reciprocal-core assumption: a fixed Huber derivative with mu=1 has a nonzero trajectory that crosses zero at EVERY actual near-critical step and never hits0. This is a scope countertest, not a counterexample to an objective upper bound at K.", "evidence": "Fix the actual H_m with d_j=1+a_m c_m^j>1. Use the single continuous monotone1-Lipschitz derivative g(x)=max(-1,min(1,x)) and its integral f, with unique minimizer0. Put epsilon=[2 product_(h in H_m)(1+h)]^(-1)>0 and x_0=epsilon. At each prefix, |x_t|<=epsilon product_(s<t)(1+h_s)<1/2, so the whole run is exactly x_(t+1)=(1-h_t)x_t in the quadratic core. Every factor is strictly negative and nonzero. Hence every step strictly crosses0, and none reaches0. The normalized final objective equals product_(h in H_m)(h-1)^2=(a_m c_m)^(2(2^m-1)) c_m^(-2m)>0. The multiplicity of d_j is2^(m-j-1), and sum_j j2^(m-j-1)=2^m-1-m, verified by induction. Its outer radial profiles are min(t,1), satisfying every subpower bound and ray-comparison1; only the reciprocal or small-core-curvature assumptions from e08ee0674ce368d3 fail. Separately, the waiting-budget condition S_j>=7d_j/4 in that candidate holds for j>=3, but is false at j=1,2: S_1/d_1<3/4, S_2/d_2<3/2. Thus neither exact annihilation nor the nonresonant base proof transfers automatically to arbitrary high core curvature.", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"finding_ids": ["e08ee0674ce368d3"], "status": "unverified candidate scope countertest"}, "glossary": {"epsilon": "A positive initial point small enough that the entire finite trajectory stays in the quadratic core.", "d_j": "The actual checkpoint1+a_m c_m^j.", "S_j": "Actual j-level ruler sum with all m-dependent parameters fixed."}}
{"id": "48c6a02c16281f04", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T18:25:07.385190+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "C46 exact scope control: legal nonnegative shared-coordinate flows with ALL adjacent gradient correlations strictly negative can still have positive older-history correlation at a high phase. A four-phase memory-three construction on the actual near-critical word has Delta_02=-s(1-s)<0 and <X_1,p_2><0. Every old/future query, true closest distance, actual objective and primitive defect is explicit. This refutes automatic applicability of C45; it is not a benchmark witness.", "evidence": "# C46. The remaining high-history sign can fail on a legal positive flow\n\nStatus: UNVERIFIED counterexample candidate. This is a scope-control example,\nnot a benchmark-scale witness or a proof of the original target.\n\nThe claim being tested is: nonnegative forward transfers and nonpositive\nadjacent gradient correlations automatically imply the high-phase older\nhistory sign required by C45. The following exact four-phase example\nrefutes that implication.\n\nFix M>=1, q>0, Q>0, A=1+q, B=1+Q, t>=1, and s in(0,1).\nLet w=1-s and choose positive u,x,R_0 by\n\n u^2=(1+qs)(w+A/t)/q,\n x^2=[(2+B/A)u^2+B^2 s/t]/Q,\n R_0^2=[(1+A/B)x^2+s^2+A s/t]/q.                        (1)\n\nTake four ordinary blocks of any nonnegative physical steps of total\nmass M-1 each, with the intervening checkpoints1+Mq,1+MQ,1+Mq.\nOn the assigned ruler these are exactly the four H_(m-2) blocks, with\nM=1+sum H_(m-2), q=a c^(m-2)/M and Q=cq.\n\nIn R^4 define the SINGLE simplex\n\n p_0=(R_0e_0-xe_1-se_3)/(MA),\n p_1=(xe_1-ue_2)/(MB),\n p_2=(ue_2-we_3)/(MA),\n p_3=e_3/(Mt),\n C=conv(0,p_0,p_1,p_2,p_3),\n F_C(y)=max_(p in C)(<y,p>-||p||^2/2).\n\nIts gradient is projection onto C, so it is convex and1-smooth.\nThe positive transfers are exactly\na_01=x, a_03=s, a_12=u, a_23=w. Coordinate3 merges the actual mass\ns sent by phase0 with the later mass w sent by phase2. This is memory\nthree; there is no reset or duplicate direction in place of a shared one.\n\nStarting at R_0e_0, the phase states are\n\n    X_0=R_0e_0, X_1=xe_1+se_3,\n    X_2=ue_2+se_3, X_3=e_3.\n\nSubtracting total phase amounts MA p_0, MB p_1, MA p_2 gives these\nstates exactly. Each original step inside a block uses the displayed\ngradient; we verify that fact against every normal now.\n\nAt the endpoint of the first ordinary block, multiply normal values by M.\nThe current value is (qR_0^2-x^2-s^2)/A^2.\nThe future values are x^2/(AB), -ws/A^2, and s/(At).\nEquation(1) gives\n\n    qR_0^2=(1+A/B)x^2+s^2+A s/t,\n\nwhich dominates every one of these and zero.\n\nAt the endpoint of the next block, the current value is\n(Qx^2-u^2)/B^2. Its future values are\n(u^2/B-ws)/A and s/t.\nEquation(1) implies BOTH required inequalities\n\n Qx^2>=(1+B/A)u^2-(B^2/A)ws,\n Qx^2>=u^2+B^2s/t.\n\nThe old p_0 normal is nonpositive.\n\nAt the endpoint of the third block, the current value is\n(qu^2-Aws-w^2)/A^2. The final normal is (s+w/A)/t.\nThe first equation in(1) is exactly\n\n    qu^2=Aws+w^2+(A^2/t)(s+w/A),\n\nso this comparison holds with equality; zero and both old normals are\nnonpositive or dominated. At the final phase t>=1 makes the p_3 normal\nnonnegative; all older normals are nonpositive.\n\nThese endpoint tests cover EVERY actual query, not just the block ends.\nDuring each block the current normal decreases affinely in the partial\nordinary mass. Every future normal that can be positive increases\naffinely or stays constant. The only negative future normal, the p_2\nnormal in phase0, remains nonpositive throughout. Every old normal\nstays nonpositive because its positive coordinate is exhausted and all\nremaining proximal coordinates are nonnegative. Thus the block endpoint\nis the worst of all actual partial times for each required comparison.\n\nThe simplex is triangular with positive diagonal. Its polar inequalities\nforce every coordinate of a minimizer to be nonpositive, so0 is the true\nclosest minimizer to R_0e_0, at squared distance R_0^2.\nThe original normalized terminal objective is exactly\n\n    2F_C(x_end)/R_0^2=(2Mt-2M+1)/(M^2t^2 R_0^2)>0.       (2)\n\nThe exact total physical mass is\nh_tot=4(M-1)+3+M(2q+Q).\nThe original primitive defect is also explicit:\n\n V/R_0^2=1-[(Mt-(M-1)+h_tot)/(Mt)]^2/R_0^2.
