(3)\n\nNo claim that(2) attains the benchmark is made.\n\nFinally, every adjacent correlation is strictly negative:\n\n <p_0,p_1>=-x^2/(M^2AB),\n <p_1,p_2>=-u^2/(M^2AB),\n <p_2,p_3>=-w/(M^2At).\n\nBut the remote correlation is strictly positive,\n\n    <p_0,p_2>=sw/(M^2 A^2)>0.\n\nAt the HIGH phase1, the older sum contains only T_0p_0, so\n\n <T_0p_0,p_2>=sw/(MA)>0,\n <X_1,p_2>=-sw/(MA)<0.\n\nEquivalently Delta_02=-sw<0. Thus the high-history sign is NOT automatic\nfor positive transfers, even with all adjacent correlations nonpositive\nand all original queries belonging to the same fixed smooth convex function.\n\nThe remaining construction task is to make this genuine older-preload\ncredit survive at many scales with useful normalized payoff. This finite\ncontrol does not accomplish that amplification; its role is to prevent\nthe proved conditional obstruction from being mistaken for a closure of\nall positive shared-coordinate flows.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"fact_ids": [], "finding_ids": ["88b640ae31a56475", "209666ace998cb98"]}, "glossary": {"s": "Fraction in(0,1) of the final coordinate preloaded by phase0.", "w": "1-s, the remaining final mass sent by phase2.", "u": "Positive mass of coordinate2, specified by equation(1).", "x": "Positive mass of coordinate1, specified by equation(1).", "R_0": "Initial coordinate mass in equation(1), which is the actual closest-minimizer distance.", "M": "One plus the ordinary physical block mass.", "q": "Low checkpoint excess divided by M.", "Q": "High checkpoint excess divided by M; Q=cq on the actual four-phase ruler.", "A": "1+q.", "B": "1+Q.", "Delta_02": "Negative of T_0T_2<p_0,p_2>, equal to -s(1-s)."}}
{"id": "37622e6cdcb606e6", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T18:27:32.553459+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "At EVERY n>=4 there is an explicit actual ordinary unit-step intrinsic certificate, with full positive-square remainder and symmetric destination-weighted circulation, whose correctly grounded G,G source certificate fails the Shu-Wang inverse-difference coefficient-sign test. The source G,G performance and q>0 hypotheses are checked, the reduced matrix is invertible, and transformed row/column sums have the correct signs, but one actual off-diagonal is negative. This closes automatic inverse transformation of every supplied certificate; it does NOT disprove reversal or alternate-certificate re-selection.", "evidence": "# An actual inverse-certificate sign obstruction at every length n>=4\n\nStatus: a complete candidate proof, NOT verifier-accepted. No solver or\nfact_submit call. This disproves an automatic transformation of EVERY supplied\ncertificate; it does not disprove existence of a different certificate for a\nreversed schedule. The schedules here have unit steps and no capacity advantage.\n\n## 1. Actual intrinsic input, with a full square certificate\n\nFix n>=4, h_i=1 for 0<=i<n, D=n+1. Use normalized smoothness1,\nx_(i+1)=x_i-g_i, and the project's ORIGINAL inequalities\n\n Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0.\n\nThe intrinsic energy is\n\n V_h=2sum_(i<n)(f_i-f_n)-n(n+1)||g_n||^2.\n\nStart with the adjacent multipliers\n\n lambda^0_(k,k+1)=2(k+1)^2,\n lambda^0_(k+1,k)=2(k+1)(k+2),   0<=k<n.\n\nThey give exactly\n\n V_h+sum lambda^0_ij Q_ij=sum_(k<n)(k+1)^2||g_k-g_(k+1)||^2. (1)\n\nFor a direct check, Q_(k,k+1) has gradient part\n-||g_k||^2/2-<g_k,g_(k+1)>+||g_(k+1)||^2/2 and the reverse Q has\n(||g_k||^2+||g_(k+1)||^2)/2. The flows are -2 at each i<n and\n2n at n; substituting these two quadratic parts gives (1).\n\nMake the following perturbation, with epsilon=1/10, leaving all other\nentries unchanged:\n\n (i,j)       delta lambda_ij\n (0,1)       -2 epsilon/3\n (1,0)       -4 epsilon/3\n (1,2)       -11 epsilon/6\n (2,1)       -11 epsilon/4\n (2,3)       -3 epsilon/4\n (3,2)       -epsilon\n (0,2)       epsilon/3\n (2,0)       epsilon\n (1,3)       epsilon/4\n (3,1)       epsilon/2.\n\nEvery resulting multiplier is nonnegative. The changed entries are\nlambda01=29/15,10=58/15,12=469/60,21=469/40,23=717/40,\n32=239/10,02=1/30,20=1/10,13=1/40,31=1/20.\nThe perturbation has zero value flow at each node. Expanding the four\ndistance-two Q terms and the six adjacent ones gives the full identity\n\n V_h+sum lambda_ij Q_ij=M(g),                            (2)\n\n M(g)=(59/60)||g0-g1||^2+(1/60)||g0-g2||^2\n      +(929/240)||g1-g2||^2+(1/80)||g1-g3||^2\n      +(143/16)||g2-g3||^2\n      +sum_(k=3)^(n-1)(k+1)^2||g_k-g_(k+1)||^2.\n\nFor example Q_(i,i+2) has gradient part\n-||g_i||^2/2-<g_i,g_(i+1)>-<g_i,g_(i+2)>+||g_(i+2)||^2/2;\nits reverse has (||g_i||^2+||g_(i+2)||^2)/2+<g_(i+2),g_(i+1)>.\nThese formulas verify every diagonal and cross coefficient in (2).\nAll weights in M are positive, so it is PSD on ALL n+1 independent\ngradients, with kernel consisting of constant gradient lists.\n\nThere is even a reversible destination-weighted circulation. Put p_i=i+1\nand mu_ij=lambda_ij p_j. Then mu_ij=mu_ji. Indeed the adjacent unperturbed\nweights are 2(k+1)^2(k+2), while the changes in these symmetric weights are\n-4epsilon/3,-11epsilon/2,-3epsilon on the first three adjacent pairs,\nand +epsilon on each of the crossing pairs {0,2} and {1,3}.\n\n## 2. A grounded matrix from an ACTUAL gradient-gradient certificate\n\nLet e be the all-ones vector of dimension n+1 and e_n its last coordinate\nvector. Write hbar=(1,...,1,0), and let L be the row Laplacian of lambda:\nL_ij=-lambda_ij for i!=j, L_ii=sum_(j!=i)lambda_ij. Define\n\n K=L+diag(hbar)-hbar e_n^T+I.                           (3)\n\nThis is not an arbitrary matrix attached by analogy to the source. It is\nthe exact grounding of the following performance certificate. Prepend one\nphysical step D+1 to the unit schedule, index its initial query by a,\nwrite u=g_a, and keep indices 0,...,n for the tail. Thus x0=xa-(D+1)u.\nDefine J=sum_(i<n)Q_(i,n) and G=sum_(i<n)Q_(i,a)+Q_(n,a).\nDirect expansion using x_n=x0-sum_(i<n)g_i gives\n\n V_h-[G+D Q_(a,n)+J]=D^2(||u||^2-||g_n||^2).           (4)\n\nOne way to check (4) is to expand its values, and then use\n2sum_(i<n)<g_i,sum_(j<i)g_j>=||sum_(i<n)g_i||^2-sum_(i<n)||g_i||^2;\nall cross terms cancel. Substitution of (2) into (4) yields\n\n D^2(||u||^2-||g_n||^2)+sum Gamma_ij Q_ij=M(g).         (5)\n\nHere every ACTUAL nonnegative multiplier is specified:\nGamma_(i,j)=lambda_ij+1_(i<n,j=n) inside the tail,\nGamma_(i,a)=1 for all i=0,...,n, and Gamma_(a,n)=D.\nCoincident tail entries are added. There are no other edges.\nIts row and column flows are equal: the extra incoming-terminal tail\nflow is +1 at i<n and -n at n, the edges to a add +1 at every tail node,\nand the edge a->n contributes -D at n and +D at a. Combining with\nthe intrinsic flow (-2,...,-2,2n) cancels every node.\n\nGrounding the negative row Laplacian of Gamma by deleting a gives exactly\n-K. Equivalently, for U=[-e^T;I], its complete balanced matrix is\n\n                         U(-K)U^T.                    (6)\n\nHere K e=e and K^T e=D e_n. Its off-diagonals are nonpositive and each\nrow is strictly diagonally dominant, with surplus1. In particular K is\ninvertible: if K v=0, choose a coordinate of maximal absolute value;\nthe row equation and strict diagonal dominance force that value to be0.\n\n## 3. Precise match to the source matrix and performance setup\n\nPrimary source inspected locally, including the relevant proof:\nShu--Wang, A Unified Theory of H-Duality in First-Order Methods,\narXiv:2609.03281v1, Sections2.2--2.5,4.4,4.5, Theorem3/Corollary3.\nhttps://arxiv.org/html/2609.03281v1\n\nTheir P is cumulative-sum times reversal, hence P^(-1)=Delta, where\n\n Delta_ij=1_(j=n-i)-1_(j=n-i-1), 0<=i,j<=n,            (7)\n\nwith out-of-range terms0. Their reduced G,G embedding in Theorem3 is\nU=[-e^T;I]. (The prose just after the embedding definitions swaps two\nlabels; Theorem3, its proof, and Corollary3 explicitly use U for G,G.)\nTheir signed-matrix convention has nonnegative off-diagonal entries and\nnonpositive row and column sums. The normalization of their slack is\none half of each squared performance term when their rate tau=1.\n\nConsequently the reduced source input supplied by (5) is exactly\n\n                      R=-K/(2D^2),                     (8)\n\nwith the full G,G coefficient matrix U R U^T. Its performance is the\ngradient-norm bound for the physical schedule (D+1,1,...,1), with n+1\nsteps, at rate1. It has the required signs and full PSD slack M/(2D^2).\n\nThe source states its G,G/D,D congruence for strong-convexity parameter\nq>0, so that hypothesis must also be checked. Let y_i denote the physical\nquery, s_i its physical gradient, X_i=y_i-s_i, and z_i=s_i-q X_i.\nFor q=0 the source slack equals M/(2D^2) by (5). For every q>=0 the\nsame matrix has slack\n\n M/(2D^2)+(q/(4D^2))sum_(i,j)Gamma_ij||X_i-X_j||^2>=0. (9)\n\nThis follows by substituting z=s-qX into tr(z^T U R U^T X) and using\nthe balanced flow of Gamma. It is a formal PSD identity, so it proves\nthe source hypothesis for every q>0 (equivalently mu=q/(1+q)>0), not\nmerely for a convex limit. The algorithm matrix has the physical steps\non its first subdiagonal, so its antitranspose reverses their order.\n\nThe source's exact inverse transformation applied to (8) is therefore\n\n Phi_source=(P R P)^(-1)=2D^2 Phi,\n Phi=-Delta K^(-1) Delta.                              (10)\n\nNo source theorem is invoked to claim automatic signs. The congruence\nwould preserve the full unsigned slack, but its separate sign test is\nthe issue tested next.\n\n## 4. A negative off-diagonal entry, exactly and at ALL n>=4\n\nDefine the vector v of dimension n+1 by\n\n v0=-169089/673492, v1=1434/673492,\n v2=-3/673492, v_i=0 for every i>=3.                    (11)\n\nDirect multiplication by (3) gives\n\n                         K v=e1-e0.                   (12)\n\nHere only the first four rows need checking; their nonzero columns\nrelevant to v are\n\n [119/30, -29/15, -1/30],\n [-58/15, 329/24, -469/60],\n [-1/10, -469/40, 127/4],\n [0, -1/20, -239/10].\n\nFor the numerators in (11), these four row products are respectively\n-673492,673492,0,0 before division by673492. Every later row is0\nbecause all its neighbors have index>=3. This works unchanged for every\nn>=4; the terminal column added in (3) multiplies v_n=0.\n\nRow n-3 of Delta is (e3-e2)^T and column n-1 is e1-e0. Equations\n(10)--(12) therefore give the off-diagonal entry\n\n             Phi_(n-3,n-1)=-(v3-v2)=-3/673492<0.        (13)\n\nThe actual source coefficient is -3D^2/336746<0. Invertibility and the\nrow/column sign conditions are NOT the problem. To see the latter\nexplicitly, destination-weighted symmetry and the value flow give\nL p=diag(p)(-2,...,-2,2n)^T. Hence\n\n K p=-D e+2D^2 e_n,\n K^(-1)e_n=(p+D e)/(2D^2),  e_n^T K^(-1)=e^T/D.\n\nSince Delta e=e_n and Delta p=e, it follows that\n\n Phi e=-(e+D e_n)/(2D^2)<=0,\n e^T Phi=-e_n^T/D<=0.                                 (14)\n\nThus precisely an actual off-diagonal interpolation coefficient fails.\nThe n=4 exact rational audit contains all5x5 matrices, but (11)--(14)\nare a uniform symbolic proof and do not rely on that computation.\n\n## 5. Scope\n\nEven an actual intrinsic ordinary certificate with a full positive square\nremainder and a symmetric destination-weighted circulation need not have\nlegal coefficients after the source's canonical inverse-difference map.\nThe example has crossing weighted edges {0,2},{1,3}; no optimal-vertex\nfixed-point assumption is being imported from Yoon--Grimmer.\n\nThe same unit schedule also has the unperturbed certificate (1), and its\nreversal is itself. Accordingly this does NOT disprove general reversal,\ngeneral variable-matrix re-selection, or the existence of a sign-good\ncertificate for the same algorithm. It closes only the proposal that\nsource inverse signs hold for every supplied full intrinsic certificate.\nAny successful use of that route must select a better certificate or\nprove additional sign structure. The unit capacities n+1 are below the\nprimitive benchmark and yield no improving objective family.\n\nRetrieval record: Matlas was queried for this precise H-duality/sign\nclaim; its three returned papers were unrelated and used for no premise.\nThe primary source was already downloaded and its definitions and proof\nwere read directly. Exact Fraction arithmetic checked the displayed\nn=4 matrices and solution in under0.1 second; this is not an SDP call.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"finding_ids": ["6973b4cc1da9c16b", "0b2dfaee171c78eb", "e7b2c6a164a4c24e"], "guidance_id": "66512436ef464e46", "source_urls": ["https://arxiv.org/html/2609.03281v1"], "artifact": "local_memory/inverse_difference_sign_obstruction.md", "exact_audit": "local_memory/inverse_difference_exact_probe.json", "status": "candidate_only"}, "glossary": {"n": "Length of the intrinsic unit-step tail, any integer n>=4.", "D": "Tail capacity n+1.", "lambda": "Actual nonnegative intrinsic interpolation multipliers given explicitly in the proof.", "M": "Full positive sum-of-squares intrinsic remainder.", "p_i": "1+i, prefix capacity at query i for this unit-step schedule.", "mu_ij": "Destination-weighted circulation lambda_ij p_j; symmetric in this construction.", "L": "Row Laplacian of lambda.", "K": "Grounded matrix L+diag(1,...,1,0)-(1,...,1,0)e_n^T+I.", "Gamma": "Balanced nonnegative graph of the actual G,G certificate after a physical prepend step D+1.", "U": "Source reduction embedding [-e^T;I].", "R": "Source reduced certificate -K/(2D^2).", "Delta": "Symmetric inverse cumulative-reversal matrix with entry 1_(j=n-i)-1_(j=n-i-1).", "Phi": "-Delta K^(-1) Delta; source transformed matrix is 2D^2 Phi.", "q": "Only in the source interface, q=mu_strong/(1-mu_strong)>0.", "v": "Exact inverse column difference K^(-1)(e1-e0), displayed in the proof."}}
{"id": "d31c2d50b5a24419", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T18:40:16.329045+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "Exact actual-ruler countertest: a fixed scalar function with a quadratic core and slope1 outer ramps can enter its core and leave it at the very next checkpoint. Full-prefix invariance does NOT imply permanent capture after an arbitrary entry time. This does not refute the new critical-curvature all-start bound or give a benchmark-scale payoff.", "evidence": "Fix m>=128, lambda=1+sqrt2, c=lambda(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c, d_j=1+a c^j and the actual H_m whose first four steps are d_0,d_1,d_0,d_2. Define one fixed derivative g(x)=(3/4)x for |x|<=1 and g(x)=x-(1/4)sign(x) for |x|>=1; put f(x)=integral_0^x g(t)dt. This derivative is continuous nondecreasing with slopes3/4 and1, so f is globally convex differentiable1-smooth and0 is its unique minimizer. Define z=(4/5+d_0/4)/a, y=(z+d_1/4)/(d_1-1), X=(y+d_0/4)/a and start x_0=-X. Since a<1/2 and0<d_1-1<1, X,y,z>1. The SAME function gives exactly x_1=aX-d_0/4=y>1; x_2=-(d_1-1)y+d_1/4=-z<-1; x_3=az-d_0/4=4/5 in(-1,1); and x_4=(4/5)(1-3d_2/4)<-1. Indeed d_2=1+(1-1/m)c>1+(127/128)(12/5)=541/160, so (4/5)(3d_2/4-1)>983/800>1. Every query, spatial knot and objective area comes from this one fixed f. Thus the first entry into the core at time3 is followed immediately by a re-exit at time4. No result about the remaining H_m tail or final benchmark payoff is inferred. The small-curvature ramp theorem in03d773db330bf6cf used a clipped-radius bound over all crossings and never made the false permanence claim.", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"finding_ids": ["03d773db330bf6cf", "f07e30eaf6cc2f95"]}, "glossary": {"g_ramp": "Derivative3x/4 inside[-1,1], x-sign(x)/4 outside.", "X_ramp": "Positive initial magnitude X defined by the three explicit inverse-step formulas.", "y_ramp": "Positive outer iterate y at time1.", "z_ramp": "Magnitude z of the negative outer iterate at time2."}}
{"id": "34bd7ef7b113f62c", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T18:40:38.820944+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "A stronger, existential symmetry obstruction: across ANY positive saturated cut h=(a,1+r,b), r(A+D+r)=AD, with a nonempty tail b, EVERY intrinsic full-PSD certificate with only incoming-terminal signed credit has lambda_(L,j)=0 but lambda_(j,L)=(T^2/D^2-1)b_j>0 at each nonterminal tail query. Thus even arbitrary full-matrix re-selection cannot produce symmetric support or a positively diagonally symmetrizable canonical G,G matrix. An explicit ordinary family at every n>=4 makes the claim nonvacuous. This excludes that symmetric source reduction, not general reversal or nonsymmetric sign-good selection.", "evidence": "# Symmetric re-selection is impossible across a nontrivial saturated cut\n\nStatus: candidate proof, NOT verifier-accepted. This is an existential\nobstruction for a precisely defined certificate class, unlike the earlier\nexample of one badly chosen array. No general reversal claim is refuted.\n\nLet a,b be positive step lists, allowing a to be empty but requiring b to\nhave at least one step. Put A=1+sum a, D=1+sum b, and choose r>0 such that\nrT=AD, where T=A+D+r. Let h=(a,1+r,b), L the bridge query, and z the\nterminal query. The prefix query set P ends at L; the tail query set B\nstarts immediately after the bridge and ends at z. All Q and V are the\noriginal normalized quantities\n\n Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\n V_h=2sum_(i<z)h_i(f_i-f_z)+sum_(i<z)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2\n                                            -(T-1)T||g_z||^2.\n\nSuppose lambda is ANY intrinsic signed certificate: value cancellation is\nexact; the FULL matrix of V_h+sum lambda Q is PSD; and lambda_ij>=0\nexcept that incoming-terminal coefficients lambda_(i,z) may be arbitrary.\nThis includes every ordinary certificate and every actual E_theta\ncertificate after replacing its terminal coefficients by lambda=Lambda-theta h.\n\nThen, with kappa=T^2/D^2, every nonterminal tail query j satisfies\n\n              lambda_(L,j)=0,\n              lambda_(j,L)=(kappa-1)b_j>0.              (1)\n\nIn particular NO choice of intrinsic certificate for this schedule can\nhave symmetric support. No positive diagonal row/column scaling can make\nits multiplier matrix, row Laplacian, destination-weighted circulation,\nor canonical grounded G,G matrix symmetric. This remains true for the\nactual E_theta array Lambda, since neither entry in (1) targets z.\n\n## Self-contained derivation of the forced entries\n\nFirst, use a one-dimensional Huber function and start sufficiently far\nalong its positive affine ray that all gradients are1. All finite Q are0\nand V_h=0, since\n2sum_i h_i sum_(t>=i)h_t=(sum h_i)^2+sum h_i^2.\nThus the all-ones vector belongs to the kernel of the full PSD remainder.\n\nFor a second legal run put eta=r/D=A/T, use the fixed scalar derivative\nf'(x)=min(1,max(x,-eta)), integrate it with f(0)=0, and start at x0=A.\nThis derivative is nondecreasing and1-Lipschitz. For prefix query i let\nR_i^a be its remaining prefix step sum; for tail query j let R_j^b be\nits remaining tail step sum. Its exact data are\n\n x_i=1+R_i^a, g_i=1, f_i=R_i^a+1/2            (i in P),\n x_j=-eta(1+R_j^b), g_j=-eta,\n f_j=eta^2(R_j^b+1/2)                         (j in B).\n\nIndeed the bridge takes1 to-r, and the tail ends at-eta. Substitution\ninto Q gives ALL cross residuals\n\n Q_(i,j)=-(1+eta)eta R_j^b,\n Q_(j,i)=-(1+eta)R_i^a,                        (i in P,j in B). (2)\n\nSame-group residuals are0, and ALL incoming-terminal residuals are0.\nAt every query f_i=<g_i,x_i>-||g_i||^2/2, so telescoping squared\npositions shows V_h=A^2-T^2 eta^2=0. Since each nonterminal-directed\ncoefficient is nonnegative, the full PSD remainder lies between0 and0\non this run. Every strictly negative residual in (2) must have zero\ncoefficient, including lambda_(L,j)=0 for every j in B\\{z}.\n\nThe only cross edges possibly left are nu_i=lambda_(i,z), i in P,\nand mu_j=lambda_(j,L), j in B. The two equality gradient vectors are\ne_P+e_B and e_P-eta e_B. PSD and eta>0 imply M e_P=M e_B=0.\nWrite p=g_L, q=g_z, H_b=sum_(j<z,j in B)b_j g_j.\nThe entire mixed part of the quadratic remainder is\n\n       -sum_(i in P)nu_i<g_i,H_b+q>\n                          +r sum_(j in B)mu_j<p,g_j>.  (3)\n\nThis follows directly from the position term of Q and its squared\ngradient difference; within-group terms have no mixed contributions.\nFor i<L, row i of M e_B is -D nu_i/2, so nu_i=0. The tail rows of\nM e_P now yield mu_j=(nu_L/r)b_j for j<z and mu_z=nu_L/r.\n\nValue flow is out_i-in_i=-2h_i for each i<z. Summing through P gives\nsum_(j in B)mu_j-nu_L=2(A+r). Consequently\n\n nu_L=2r(A+r)/(D-r)=r(kappa-1),\n mu_j=(kappa-1)b_j,    mu_z=kappa-1.                   (4)\n\nThe equality uses rT=AD and kappa=T^2/D^2; it also implies r<D and\nkappa>1. This proves (1) with every sign and full-kernel condition retained.\n\nFor any positive diagonal matrices C,E, entries (C lambda E)_(L,j)\nand (C lambda E)_(j,L) are respectively0 and positive, hence cannot be\nequal. Adding a diagonal to form a Laplacian does not change this pair.\nThe canonical grounded matrix K=L_lambda+diag(hbar)-hbar e_z^T+diag(w)\nalso leaves this NONTERMINAL pair unchanged, up to the negative sign of\nthe row Laplacian. Thus the same obstruction holds there and in its full\nbalanced G,G embedding. A simultaneous relabeling cannot fix support\nasymmetry. This argument concerns all intrinsic certificate choices,\nincluding a changed full PSD remainder.\n\n## Nonvacuity at unbounded lengths\n\nTake a to be k unit steps, k>=1, and b=(1,1). Then A=k+1, D=3,\nr=(-A-3+sqrt((A+3)^2+12A))/2 and n=k+3 can be arbitrarily large.\nEvery such schedule HAS an ordinary full certificate. Here are complete\ncoefficients and the identity, independent of any unverified result.\n\nFor a unit list of length m, use adjacent weights\nsigma_(i,i+1)=2(i+1)^2 and sigma_(i+1,i)=2(i+1)(i+2).\nDirect expansion gives V+sum sigma Q=sum_(i<m)(i+1)^2||g_i-g_(i+1)||^2.\nEmbed these arrays separately as sigma^a,sigma^b. Keep sigma^a on P;\non B use kappa sigma^b plus (kappa-1)b_j on each j->z;\nadd (kappa-1)b_j on j->L for j<z, (kappa-1) on z->L,\nand r(kappa-1) on L->z. Every coefficient is nonnegative.\n\nTo check the full identity, define\nC=sum b_j Q_(j,L)+Q_(z,L)+r Q_(L,z)+sum b_j Q_(j,z).\nUsing the physical bridge1+r, its value part is\n2sum b_j f_j+(r-D)f_L+(2-D-r)f_z, and its gradient part is\nsum b_j(1-b_j)||g_j||^2+[(D-r)/2-r^2]||g_L||^2\n                                             +(D+r)||g_z||^2/2.\nCross terms cancel on expansion. Since\n(kappa-1)(D-r)=2(A+r), kappa r^2=A^2, kappa D^2=T^2,\none obtains V_h=V_a+kappa V_b-(kappa-1)C.\nAdding exactly the displayed coefficients leaves the full PSD remainder\nM_a+kappa M_b. Thus the all-certificate asymmetry obstruction is nonvacuous\nfor this positive-step family at every n>=4.\n\nAt k=1 this is h=(1,2,1,1); the forced pairs include\nlambda12=0,lambda21=3 and lambda13=0,lambda31=3 for EVERY intrinsic\ncertificate of the stated type. The ordinary full certificate previously\ndisplayed for this h illustrates existence but is not needed as a premise.\n\n## Exact scope of the closure\n\nThis excludes the proposal to re-select an intrinsic certificate and then\ndiagonally symmetrize it in order to apply a symmetric fixed-point arc\ntheorem. It does not exclude a nonsymmetric noncrossing theorem, another\nperformance conversion, arbitrary changes of coordinates that also change\nthe interpolation inequalities, or a sign-good inverse certificate. The\nsubcritical/basic reversal class can still contain all the examples above.\nNo improved objective capacity or general impossibility claim is made.\n\nRetrieval: Matlas query for the saturated-cut support claim returned two unrelated results (2102.07922 Lemma6 and2312.05551 Theorem2), neither used as a premise.", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"finding_ids": ["2d75b4696641736c", "6973b4cc1da9c16b", "37622e6cdcb606e6", "4a064f93111ac5a2"], "artifact": "local_memory/forced_asymmetric_saturated_cut.md", "guidance_id": "66512436ef464e46", "status": "candidate_only"}, "glossary": {"A": "Capacity 1+sum of the positive prefix list a.", "D": "Capacity 1+sum of the nonempty positive tail list b.", "r": "Bridge excess over1, satisfying rT=AD.", "T": "Joined capacity A+D+r.", "L": "Query before the physical bridge1+r.", "z": "Terminal query.", "kappa": "T^2/D^2.", "P": "Prefix query set through L.", "B": "Tail query set after the bridge through z.", "eta": "r/D=A/T.", "lambda": "Any signed intrinsic array, with possible negative entries only when the target is z.", "Lambda": "Actual nonnegative E_theta array before incoming-terminal credit is subtracted."}}
{"id": "cc59ae14a26f3e78", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T18:47:41.971261+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "Stronger than fixed-remainder failure: for ANY schedule with h0=1, every value-cancelling intrinsic full matrix satisfies M00=1 and M0n=-lambda0n/2. Hence if the supplied lambda0n<0, EVERY ordinary repair has an INDEFINITE difference M_new-M, even if both endpoint matrices are PSD. Explicit E_(1/n) unit-step certificates at every n>=2 make this nonvacuous, with ordinary repair known to exist. No universal repair using only PSD additions, only PSD subtractions, or a one-direction Loewner path is possible.", "evidence": "# Any ordinary repair in this family has an indefinite matrix change\n\nStatus: candidate, NOT verifier-accepted. Strengthens the fixed-remainder\nobstruction4aaee6131a92da0e; it does not disprove general PSD-preserving repair.\n\n## The boundary invariant\n\nFix ANY nonnegative schedule h of length n>=1 whose first step is h0=1.\nLet lambda be any real intrinsic multiplier array cancelling all formal\nfunction values, and let M be the FULL formal gradient matrix of\n\n V_h+sum lambda_ij Q_ij,\n Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\n x_i=x0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t,\n V_h=2sum_(i<n)h_i(f_i-f_n)+sum_(i<n)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2\n                                        -s(s+1)||g_n||^2,\n s=sum h_i.\n\nWrite L for the row Laplacian of lambda and A for the lower triangular\nmatrix with diagonal1 and entries A_it=h_t for t<i. Direct expansion\nof Q and the value flows gives\n\n M=diag(h_i(2-h_i),-s(s+2))+sym(L A).\n\nSince h0=1, column0 of A is the all-ones vector, so column0 of L A\nis zero. Consequently\n\n                         M00=1,\n                         M0n=-lambda_0n/2.             (1)\n\nMore generally, twice the off-diagonal portion of row0 of M equals\nthe corresponding portion of row0 of L A. Let M_new come from any other\nvalue-cancelling intrinsic array on the SAME h. If M_new-M is positive\nOR negative semidefinite, its00 entry is0 by (1), hence its entire\nrow0 is0. To see this without an external lemma, restrict its quadratic\nform to e0+t e_j; a nonzero linear term in t contradicts either sign\nnear t=0. Invertibility of A then implies that row0 of L_new-L is0.\nThus EVERY outgoing coefficient lambda_(0,j) is unchanged by any\nsemidefinite-ordered matrix change.\n\nIn particular, if lambda_0n<0 and lambda_new is ordinary, then\nlambda_new,0n>=0 implies (M_new-M)0n!=0 while (M_new-M)00=0.\nThe corresponding2x2 principal determinant is strictly negative.\nTherefore M_new-M has both a positive and a negative eigenvalue.\nThis holds for EVERY ordinary repair, regardless of its other coefficients\nand regardless of how far its full PSD matrix is allowed to move.\n\n## An explicit credited input at every n>=2\n\nFor completeness the input family is rederived here, rather than using an\nunverified finding as a premise. Take h=(1,...,1), epsilon=theta=1/n,\nd_k=g_k-g_(k+1). The ordinary adjacent array\n\n lambda^0_(k,k+1)=2(k+1)^2,\n lambda^0_(k+1,k)=2(k+1)(k+2)\n\nhas remainder M0=sum_(k<n)(k+1)^2||d_k||^2 by expanding the adjacent Q.\nModify it by\n\n delta lambda01=n epsilon, delta lambda10=(n-1)epsilon,\n delta lambda0n=-epsilon, delta lambda1n=epsilon.\n\nWhen n=2 the last change is added to its existing adjacent edge. The\nchanges have zero value flow. Their full quadratic sum is\n\n -epsilon<d0,sum_(k=1)^(n-1)(n-k)d_k>.\n\nFor a direct expansion, the left side before taking differences is\nepsilon<g0-g1,sum_(j=1)^n g_j-n g1>. Thus the remainder is exactly\n\n M=sum_(k=1)^(n-1)(k+1)^2\n       ||d_k-epsilon(n-k)d0/[2(k+1)^2]||^2\n   +[1-epsilon^2/4 sum_(k=1)^(n-1)(n-k)^2/(k+1)^2]||d0||^2. (2)\n\nThe last coefficient is greater than3/4: use epsilon=1/n,\n(n-k)^2<n^2, and\nsum_(j=2)^n 1/j^2<sum_(j=2)^n 1/[j(j-1)]<1.\nSo the full matrix is PSD and is positive definite on gradient differences.\nThe sole negative intrinsic coefficient is lambda0n=-1/n.\n\nFor the ACTUAL E_theta=V_h-theta sum_(i<n)h_i Q_(i,n) certificate, use\nLambda_(i,n)=lambda_(i,n)+theta h_i and keep every other entry. All\nactual coefficients are nonnegative, including Lambda0n=0; their added\nterminal terms cancel the credit and leave (2). The ordinary certificate\nlambda^0 already displayed proves that ordinary repair DOES exist.\nNevertheless its difference from this input, and the difference of EVERY\nother ordinary repair from it, is indefinite by (1).\n\n## Which routes this closes\n\nA general repair cannot insist on keeping the matrix fixed, adding only\nPSD remainders, subtracting only PSD remainders, or taking a sequence of\nmatrix changes all ordered in the same Loewner direction. The obstruction\nalready holds for arbitrarily long unit schedules and theta=1/n tending0.\nIt does NOT prohibit a path whose endpoint matrices are PSD and whose\nindividual differences are indefinite. Such a genuine matrix change is\nexactly what a viable general repair must allow.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"finding_ids": ["4aaee6131a92da0e", "5bd01e2b19002a59", "4a064f93111ac5a2"], "artifact": "local_memory/loewner_monotone_repair_obstruction.md", "status": "candidate_only"}, "glossary": {"h0": "The physical first step, assumed to equal1.", "M": "Full independent-gradient remainder of a value-cancelling intrinsic array.", "M_new": "Full remainder of any ordinary replacement array for the same physical schedule.", "lambda0n": "Signed multiplier on the original initial-to-terminal inequality Q_(0,n).", "L": "Row Laplacian of the signed multiplier array.", "A": "Lower triangular trajectory matrix with diagonal1 and A_it=h_t for t<i.", "epsilon": "1/n in the explicit unit-step construction.", "theta": "Credit parameter epsilon=1/n.", "d_k": "g_k-g_(k+1)."}}
{"id": "a1661dacd3b561f7", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T19:27:15.453747+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "Complete DIRECT full-objective-array obstruction on the ACTUAL critical words: a nonnegative recursively specified AP-style multiplier family cancels every value and calibrates exactly at Silver, but its FULL remainder has negative level2 midpoint coefficient for all m>=128 (also m3). Its negative subspace has dimension2^(m-3)-1 with x0=x_n=g_n=g_(n-1)=0, so no initial-distance inflation, endpoint-only correction, final-step replacement using the same array, or PSD patch of smaller rank can repair it. For every fixed tau>0 and every sigma, a fixed bad level exists asymptotically. This is an ansatz obstruction, NOT a legal lower witness or a rejection of the critical schedules.", "evidence": "# Direct objective gluing on the actual critical words\n\nStatus: UNVERIFIED joint calculation and certificate-space obstruction.\nNo solver and no fact_submit. The negative formal vectors below are NOT legal\nsmooth-convex trajectories. They refute a specified full multiplier array and\nits stated repairs, not the schedule or the original profile conjecture.\n\nThe only mathematical predecessor used is accepted fact\n`1252b8823bb5a290` for interpolation, and `b98bb807d514e80d` for the\nHuber/cut witnesses and the exact benchmark recursions. The recursive pattern\nwas suggested by Altschuler--Parrilo, arXiv:2309.16530, Theorem 3.4; all\nidentities needed here are derived below rather than imported from it.\n\n## 1. Normalization and a fully specified multiplier array\n\nNormalize L=1 and x_star=g_star=f_star=0. For every sample let\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 <=0,\n    C_ij=-2 Q_ji\n        =2(f_i-f_j)+2<g_j,x_j-x_i>-||g_i-g_j||^2 >=0.\n\nEvery position is substituted using the actual GD steps. In the full formal\nquadratic, x_0,g_0,...,g_n are independent; they are not restricted to any\nchosen primal family.\n\nFix positive numbers a,c, with c>2. Write\n\n    d_j=a c^j,  H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+d_j,H_j),\n    n_j=2^j-1, S_j=sum H_j, M_j=1+S_j.\n\nThroughout this calculation require d_j<M_j at the levels being used.\nDefine the following exact scalar recurrence:\n\n    Z_0=1, t_0=1,\n    z_j=(M_j+d_j)/(M_j-d_j),\n    r_j=1+2z_j=(3M_j+d_j)/(M_j-d_j),\n    Z_(j+1)=r_j Z_j,\n    t_(j+1)=(1+sqrt(4 Z_(j+1)-3))/2,\n    delta_j=Z_j-r_j d_j^2.                              (1)\n\nThus Z_j=t_j^2-t_j+1. All r_j>1, so the square roots are real and\nt_j>=1. This fixes every parameter; there is no numerical optimization.\n\nHere is the actual nonnegative array lambda^(j) on C_ij. Its base has only\nlambda^0_(star,0)=1. Suppose its block has length n=n_j, sum S=S_j,\nand put p=n, q=2n+1, b=1+d_j, z=z_j, r=r_j, t=t_j, t'=t_(j+1).\n\n1. Embed the old array on the left sample indices {0,...,p,star}.\n2. Add r times the old array on {p+1,...,q,star}.\n3. For each right queried index i=p+1,...,q-1, whose physical step is h_i,\n   add z h_i to entries (p,i) and (q,i), and subtract 2z h_i from (star,i).\n4. Add the following six sparse corrections:\n\n    (p,q): z,                 (q,p): z d_j,\n    (p,star): -S,             (q,star): 2S+b-rS,\n    (star,p): b-t,            (star,q): t'-rt.           (2)\n\nThe corrections individually may be signed, but the FINAL array is\nnonnegative. In fact its optimum entries are exactly\n\n    lambda_(star,i)=h_i for i<q,\n    lambda_(star,q)=t',\n    lambda_(i,star)=0 for i<q,\n    lambda_(q,star)=2S+b.                                (3)\n\nTo verify this inductively, left interior entries are unchanged, the old\nleft terminal entry t becomes b, right interior entries become\n(r-2z)h_i=h_i, and the right terminal entry becomes t'. Incoming optimum\nentries at the left terminal cancel, and the right terminal entry becomes\n2S+b. Every other update either embeds a nonnegative old coefficient or\nadds z h_i, z, or z d_j. This proves actual coefficient signs, including\nall optimum edges, rather than just signs of an auxiliary matrix.\n\n## 2. Complete identity and calibration\n\nThere is an exact gradient-only remainder E_j, defined on all the formal\ngradients of the block, such that\n\n    ||x_start||^2-||x_end-t_j g_end||^2-2t_j f_end\n       =sum lambda^(j)_ik C_ik + E_j.                  (4)\n\nIts recursion is\n\n    E_0=0,\n    E_(j+1)=E_j(left)+r_j E_j(right)\n                         +delta_j ||g_p||^2.            (5)\n\nHere each child is evaluated at its actual chronological sample indices.\nIn particular E_j contains no terminal-gradient term of its own: its\nvariables are only the midpoint gradients of all internal recursion nodes.\n\nWe verify (4)--(5) without omitting any function coefficient. Denote the\nleft endpoint and gradient by x,g and the right endpoint and gradient by\ny,v. Their values are f,F. The weighted right displacement is\n\n    w=sum_(p<i<q) h_i g_i=x-bg-y.\n\nThe rank-one correction in step 3 sums to\n\n    2zS(f+F)-zS(||g||^2+||v||^2)\n                         -2z<w,x+y-g-v>.               (6)\n\nCombine the two child identities, the correction (6), and (2). The\ncoefficient of f is\n\n    2[(z-1)S-b+z-zd_j]=0,\n\nbecause z(S+2-b)=S+b. The coefficient of F is -2t', and every\ninterior value has already canceled within its child and (6). The\noptimum value is zero under normalization; before normalization the\ntotal value flow cancels it against the terminal coefficient.\n\nThe x^2, <x,g>, <x,v>, <y,g>, and <g,v> coefficients all cancel.\nThe remaining expression is\n\n    ||x_start||^2-||y-t'v||^2-2t'F\n       +(r d_j^2-Z_j)||g||^2\n       +(Z_(j+1)-r Z_j)||v||^2\n       -E_j(left)-r E_j(right).\n\nThe last gradient coefficient is zero by (1). This is exactly (4)--(5).\nThis calculation also follows by direct expansion of (6); the cancellations\nuse only z(S+2-b)=S+b and r=1+2z.\n\nThe calibration is exact. Set lambda=1+sqrt(2), a=1/lambda, c=lambda.\nThen M_j=lambda^j, d_j=lambda^(j-1), z_j=lambda, r_j=lambda^2,\nZ_j=M_j^2, and delta_j=0 at every level. Thus E_j=0 and (4) is a\nvalid full initial-distance certificate at the undeformed silver word.\nIt is the canonical endpoint-square guarantee, not a claim that this\nparticular certificate attains the sharper Huber constant.\n\nFor an arbitrary real q_cert, the SAME nonnegative array supplies the\nfollowing fully value-canceled candidate objective matrix:\n\n    q_cert ||x_0||^2-2 f_n\n       +sum_(i!=k) [2 lambda^(m)_ki/t_m] Q_ik\n     =(q_cert-1/t_m)||x_0||^2\n       +[||x_n-t_m g_n||^2+E_m]/t_m.                 (7)\n\nThis explicitly includes the true initial vector and the optimum index.\nIts validity as an upper bound depends on FULL PSD of the right side.\n\n## 3. An exact negative midpoint for the assigned parameters\n\nNow set m>=128, epsilon=1/m, and use the assigned family\n\n    c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=(1-epsilon)/c.\n\nFor these parameters 2<c<3 and 0<a<c-2<1. The exact mass formula is\n\n    M_j=A c^j+(1-A)2^j,  A=a/(c-2),                 (8)\n\nso 0<A<1 and M_j-d_j=A(3-c)c^j+(1-A)2^j>0.\nThus every coefficient in the proposed array is defined and nonnegative.\n\nNevertheless delta_2<0. Here is a finite exact proof with an explicit\nparameter interval, not a limiting differential equation. Put s=sqrt(2).\nFor j=0,1,2, write r_j=N_j/D_j, where\n\n    N_0=(2+s)+(4-s)epsilon,\n    D_0=(2-s)+s epsilon,\n    N_1=(4+3s)+(9-3s)epsilon-epsilon^2,\n    D_1=s+(3-s)epsilon+epsilon^2,\n    N_2=(10+7s)+(19-5s)epsilon-(4+s)epsilon^2\n                                                 -(1+s)epsilon^3,\n    D_2=(2+s)+(5-3s)epsilon+s epsilon^2\n                                                 +(1+s)epsilon^3.\n\nAll denominator coefficients are positive. Since d_2=lambda(1-epsilon^2),\n\n    delta_2=P(epsilon)/(D_0 D_1 D_2),\n    P=N_0 N_1 D_2-lambda^2(1-epsilon^2)^2 N_2 D_0 D_1.\n\nThe coefficients of P, in increasing degree 0 through 10, are\n\n    0,\n    -112-96s,\n    -96-84s,\n    640+12s,\n    528+368s,\n    88+92s,\n    -320-167s,\n    -202-158s,\n    -2-4s,\n    42+30s,\n    10+7s.                                           (9)\n\nThese coefficients follow by multiplying the displayed degree-at-most-three\npolynomials. A tiny exact Q(sqrt(2)) polynomial multiplication independently\nchecked the list; the displayed identity is the mathematical evidence.\nUsing s<3/2, the sum of absolute upper bounds for coefficients of degree\nat least two is 3311. Consequently, for 0<epsilon<=1/128,\n\n    P(epsilon)/epsilon\n       <=-112-96s+3311 epsilon\n       <-112+3311/128<0.                             (10)\n\nFor comparison, the first derivative is\n\n    delta_2=-(48+28sqrt(2))epsilon+O(epsilon^2).\n\nThe same negative coefficient occurs at the finite diagnostic m=3:\n\n    P(1/3)=(-1059632-2141552sqrt(2))/59049<0.\n\nThe three relevant denominators are positive there as well. This agrees\nwith failure of the ARRAY even though max3's unrestricted numerical H_3\ndiagnostic reported a Huber match. That diagnostic is not used as a premise.\n\n## 4. Negative FULL formal directions, and limits of repairs\n\nEvery occurrence of a level-two midpoint contributes\n\n    omega_i delta_2 ||g_i||^2\n\nto E_m, with omega_i>0, a product of right-child weights r_j from its\nancestors. There are K=2^(m-3) such indices. Their actual step is the same\nnumber b_2=1+d_2. No other term of E_m uses any of those gradient\ncoordinates, by the diagonal recursion (5).\n\nFirst choose one such index i, set x_0=0, g_i=v!=0, all other queried\ngradients zero, and g_n=-(b_2/t_m)v. Then\n\n    x_n=-b_2 v, x_n-t_m g_n=0, E_m=omega_i delta_2||v||^2<0.\n\nThus (7) is not PSD for ANY q_cert. Merely increasing the initial-distance\ncoefficient does not repair the proposed array.\n\nThere is a larger obstruction when m>=4. Work first with scalar formal\ngradients, put x_0=g_n=0, allow arbitrary g_i at the K selected indices\nsubject to sum_i g_i=0, and set every other gradient to zero. This is a\nsubspace D of dimension K-1 on which\n\n    x_n=0, g_(n-1)=0,\n    E_m=delta_2 sum_i omega_i g_i^2<0\n\nfor every nonzero vector. The weights need not coincide. It follows that:\n\n* Any added quadratic depending only on x_0,x_n,g_n,g_(n-1) vanishes on D.\n* Replacing only the final physical step leaves every position unchanged\n  on D, because g_(n-1)=0. With the same array, the negative evaluation\n  persists for every prescribed final-step replacement and every q_cert.\n* Any PSD matrix addition of rank less than K-1 has a nonzero kernel vector\n  in D, by dimension counting, and therefore cannot repair the full matrix.\n\nThe last statement is about additions to this fixed matrix. Changing\nrelative interpolation multipliers may have an indefinite difference and\nis NOT excluded. Likewise a correction of growing rank is not excluded.\nThe selected vectors deliberately do not satisfy the interpolation\nconstraints; no legal lower witness, no W upper bound, and no impossibility\ntheorem for H_m is inferred.\n\n## 5. Fixed-constant deformations: the same ansatz always fails for tau>0\n\nConsider the allowed neighboring family\n\n    c=lambda(1+tau/m), a=(1-sigma/m)/c,                (11)\n\nwith fixed real tau,sigma, for all sufficiently large m. Positivity and\nd_j<M_j hold uniformly over the word once 0<a<1 and 2<c<3: indeed\n\n    M_j/d_j=1/(c-2)\n               +[1/a-1/(c-2)](2/c)^j\n\nlies between 1/a and 1/(c-2), both greater than one.\n\nIf tau>0, the specified multiplier recursion always develops a negative\ncoefficient at a FIXED level as m tends to infinity. If sigma+tau<0,\nthen d_0>1/lambda and delta_0=1-r_0 d_0^2<0, since\nd maps to d^2(3+d)/(1-d) is strictly increasing for 0<d<1 and equals\none at d=1/lambda.\n\nFor sigma+tau>=0 the fixed level j=4 suffices. To show this, temporarily\nregard epsilon as a real variable tending to zero and set\n\n    u=2/lambda, D=sigma+(1+lambda^2)tau,\n    T_j=lambda^2 tau-D u^j.\n\nDifferentiation at epsilon=0 of the explicit finite formulas gives\n\n    M_j(0)=lambda^j,\n    (d/depsilon)log M_j |0=j tau-D(1-u^j),\n    (d/depsilon)(d_j/M_j)|0=(1/lambda)T_j.\n\nFor r(x)=(3+x)/(1-x), (d/dx)log r at x=1/lambda equals 2.\nUsing 1-u=1/lambda^2 and\n2(1/lambda-1/lambda^2)/(1-u)=2sqrt(2), equation (1) therefore gives\n\n    (d/depsilon)[delta_j/M_j^2]|0\n       =2sqrt(2)[tau(j-lambda^2)-D(1-2u^j)].          (12)\n\nOne can also verify (12) by summing the finite geometric series for T_j.\nAt j=4, 4-lambda^2<0 and 1-2u^4>0. The latter follows from\nu^4=272-192sqrt(2)<1/2; squaring 543<384sqrt(2) verifies it with\npositive sides. Moreover D>=lambda^2 tau>0. Thus (12) is strictly\nnegative. The expression is an analytic finite rational function near\nzero, so delta_4<0 for every sufficiently large m. This uses a derivative\nat one fixed finite depth, not an unproved continuum recurrence.\n\nThe same full-vector argument as in Section 4 then excludes the array and\nits stated endpoint or fixed-rank patches. This generalization does not\nexclude a different full certificate for any of the deformed schedules.\n\n## 6. Rechecking the exact comparison and an actual deformation filter\n\nSet rho=log_2(lambda). From the accepted exact recursions, primitive shifted\nmass C_n=S_circle(n)+1 has join\n\n    Omega(A,B)=(A+B+sqrt((A+B)^2+4AB))/2,\n\nand the benchmark B_n has join\n\n    G(A,D)=2A+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8AD)/2.\n\nHere is a proof of C_n<=(n+1)^rho and B_n<=1+4n^rho, retained rather\nthan assuming the earlier draft comparison. Define F(t)=(1+t)^rho-1.\nFor t=exp(z), direct differentiation gives\n\n    d^2/dz^2 log F(exp(z))\n      =rho t(1+t)^(rho-2)\n        [(1+t)^rho-1-rho t]/F(t)^2>0.\n\nThe numerator bracket is positive since rho>1 and its derivative is\nrho[(1+t)^(rho-1)-1]>0. Convexity at z,-z gives\nF(t)F(1/t)>=F(1)^2=(lambda-1)^2=2. With A=t^rho B and\nE=(1+t)^rho B, this implies (E-A)(E-B)>=2AB, so\n\n    Omega(A,B)<=(A^(1/rho)+B^(1/rho))^rho.\n\nInduction in the primitive recursion gives its claimed bound. Also\nG(A,D)<=4A+D. If k+l+1=n, the dominant induction now gives\n\n    B_n<=1+4[(k+1)^rho+l^rho]<=1+4n^rho.\n\nThe last inequality follows from superadditivity of t^rho on nonnegative\narguments, which follows by integrating its increasing derivative.\n\nFor (11), the exact mass formula (8) gives\n\n    M_m/lambda^m\n      =(1+tau/m)^m [A+(1-A)(2/c)^m] -> exp(tau).       (13)\n\nThus the actual benchmark satisfies\n\n    limsup B_(2^m-1)/(2M_m-1)<=2 exp(-tau).             (14)\n\nIn particular tau=1 recovers 2/e. This comparison alone is not an\nobjective upper bound; the failed matrix in Section 4 cannot supply one.\n\nThere is also a useful LOWER bound for the exact benchmark. Balanced\nprimitive joins give C_(2^j-1)>=lambda^j. Put b_0=1 and\n\n    b_(j+1)=G(1,b_j)/lambda.\n\nBalanced dominant joins imply B_(2^j-1)>=b_j lambda^j. The map in this\nrecursion is strictly increasing. Its fixed point above one is\n\n    b_infty=sqrt(2)(1+lambda^(-1/2))>2.                (15)\n\nTo verify the fixed point, squaring the equation G(1,b)=lambda b\ngives lambda(lambda-1)b^2-4lambda b+4=0; the positive root (15)\nsatisfies the original equation, whereas the smaller algebraic root does\nnot. Alternatively uniqueness above one follows because\nG(1,b)/b=2/b+1/2+sqrt(1+8/b)/2 is strictly decreasing. Since\n1<b_1=4/lambda<b_infty, monotonicity shows b_j increases to b_infty.\n\nThis yields an ACTUAL legal-witness filter even when the final step is\nreplaced arbitrarily. Let P_m=S_m-(1+d_0) be the sum of the queried prefix\nbefore that final step, and prescribe any b_m>=0. For P_m>=1, the two\naccepted Huber/cut witnesses give\n\n    W_(n_m)(H_m with final step b_m)\n       >=max{1/(2P_m+2b_m+1),\n                    (b_m-1)_+^2/(P_m+1)^2}\n       >=1/(2P_m+3+2sqrt(P_m)).                       (16)\n\nFor b_m<=1+sqrt(P_m), the first term proves (16). For larger b_m, the\nsecond is at least P_m/(P_m+1)^2, which is at least its right side\nbecause P_m^2+P_m+2P_m sqrt(P_m)-1>=0 for P_m>=1.\nEquations (13), (15), and (16) prove the uniform statement\n\n    liminf_m B_(n_m) W_(n_m)(modified word)\n                     >=b_infty/(2 exp(tau)).           (17)\n\nConsequently no fixed asymptotic separation below the benchmark is possible\nwithin these fixed-constant deformations, even with an arbitrary final step,\nwhen tau<=log(b_infty/2). This is a legal lower-bound conclusion for that\nparameter region. It is distinct from the formal-matrix obstruction for\ntau>0, and does not settle the assigned tau=1 family.\n\n## 7. A richer exact join, with the unresolved full-matrix issue exposed\n\nThe following calculation records the next concrete repair interface. It\ndoes not assert that a scalar local condition closes the target.\n\nReplace the canonical child storage by\n\n    I=||x_start||^2-||x_end-u0 g_end||^2-2v0 f_end\n       =sum lambda C+E,\n    Z=(u0-1)^2+v0,\n    k=S+u0-v0>=0.\n\nAssume its optimum entries are h_i on (star,i), u0 on the terminal\n(star,end), zero on other (i,star), and k on (end,star); retain the\nENTIRE gradient remainder E. These properties hold for the calibrated\ncanonical family. They are assumptions of this interface, not all full\ncertificates.\n\nUse two nonnegative correction weights A0,B0. Scale the right child by\nr=1+A0+B0. For each right queried i add A0 h_i on (p,i), B0 h_i on\n(q,i), and -(A0+B0)h_i on (star,i). Choose cross coefficients p0,q0>=0\nwith\n\n    p0-q0=b+(1-A0)S.\n\nAdjust optimum entries to physical h_i, terminal u1, and\nk1=2S+b+u1-v1>=0 exactly as in (2), with child k in place of S.\nThus the actual final array is nonnegative provided the named scalar\ncoefficients and u1,k1 are nonnegative. Put\n\n    d=b-1, M=S+1, t=A0-B0,\n    H=M+d-B0(M-d)-t M,\n    ell=p0-B0-t,\n    Z1=(u1-1)^2+v1,\n    V=x_p-x_q-g_p+g_q.\n\nFull coefficient expansion, including all function values, gives\n\n    E_new=E_left+r E_right\n       +t||V||^2+2<V,(H-ell)g_p+ell g_q>\n       +(Z-rd^2)||g_p||^2+(rZ-Z1)||g_q||^2.           (18)\n\nFor example the canonical construction sets A0=B0=z, p0=z,\nq0=zd, H=ell=t=0 and Z1=rZ, recovering (5).\nTo check (18), first use D=x_p-x_q. The local coefficients of\nD^2, D g_p, D g_q are t,-2(ell+t-H),2(ell+t).\nSubstituting D=V+g_p-g_q makes the g_p g_q coefficient zero and gives\nexactly the displayed diagonal and cross terms. The value coefficient\nat p is 2[(A0-1)S-b+p0-q0]=0 and the terminal coefficient is -2v1.\n\nDemanding the isolated three-vector matrix in (18) be PSD is only a\nSUFFICIENT restriction. It forces Z>=rd^2 and Z1<=rZ, and risks\nrepeating the already recorded scalar-deficit failure. The useful open\nquestion is whether the FULL child remainders can pay for an indefinite\nlocal term.\n\nThere is a finite exact way to retain that information for this array\nclass. Define the gradient moment z_block=sum h_i g_i+g_end. If a child\nremainder is decomposed into nonnegative internal squares plus the sharp\ntwo-variable form\n\n    a0||z_block||^2+2b0<z_block,g_end>+c0||g_end||^2,\n\nthen the parent remainder before elimination is the explicit four-variable\nform, in L=z_left, g=g_p, R=z_right, e=g_q,\n\n    a0||L||^2+2b0<L,g>+c0||g||^2\n      +r[a0||R||^2+2b0<R,e>+c0||e||^2]\n      +t||d g+R||^2+2<d g+R,(H-ell)g+ell e>\n      +(Z-rd^2)||g||^2+(rZ-Z1)||e||^2.                (19)\n\nThe parent moment is exactly z_parent=L+d g+R. Substituting\nL=z_parent-dg-R and completing squares in g,R gives its next\ntwo-variable form when the corresponding eliminated block is PSD and\nthe cross columns lie in its range. Negative eliminated directions are\nfull-matrix obstructions and must not be dropped. This is an explicit\nrecursively generated multiplier family and exact remainder interface;\nno choice of its free parameters or uniform constant establishing the\nrequested asymptotic separation has yet been proved.\n\nThe original target remains open. The finished result of this attempt is\nthe fully exhibited obstruction to (1)--(7), the exact parameter filter\n(17), and the identified need to change the full recursive array rather\nthan inflate its endpoint bound.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"plan_id": "8bfae58e9df83020", "branch_id": "max_direct_deformed_gluing", "artifact": "workers/max/research/max_direct_full_matrix_gluing.md", "fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290", "b98bb807d514e80d"], "status": "complete_unverified_counterexample_to_explicit_array", "source_pattern": "arXiv:2309.16530 Theorem3.4; all needed algebra re-derived", "target_claim": "Full PSD of the explicitly specified recursively glued array and its stated small repairs.", "assumptions_satisfied": ["Actual physical schedule and nonnegative final multipliers", "Every function-value coefficient canceled", "Original full formal vectors and true initial distance retained"], "failed_conclusion": "Full PSD", "scope": "Certificate family only; the formal negative vectors are not interpolable trajectories."}, "glossary": {"Z_j": "Canonical endpoint storage parameter t_j^2-t_j+1 in this proposed recursive full certificate.", "delta_j": "Actual midpoint coefficient Z_j-r_j d_j^2 in the full remainder; it may be negative.", "r_j": "Right-child multiplier weight (3M_j+d_j)/(M_j-d_j) in this certificate family.", "d_j": "Excess over one of the level-j ruler checkpoint: a c^j.", "M_j": "One plus the actual sum of the ruler word H_j in this finding.", "E_j": "Entire gradient remainder recursively specified in equation (5), before any PSD assertion.", "C_ij": "Opposite-sign, transposed interpolation expression -2Q_ji; nonnegative on legal trajectories."}}
{"id": "b20150129d0b0403", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T19:40:47.845934+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "C52: in the pending all-horizon bipartite rank-two flow program, retaining the terminal normal yields an exact positive 2x2 affine tail-cost recursion. A proposed one-tail monotonicity shortcut is REFUTED at critical low ratio and level-four high ratio, even with negative older history. This is a relaxation counterexample, not a legal full adversary or class closure.", "evidence": "# C52: the two tail energies in the pending bipartite history branch\n\nStatus: unverified necessary-condition derivation and a counterexample to\none proposed simplification. No new solver or verifier call. This does not\nclose the bipartite class and does not assert that the relaxed example is\nan interpolable full trajectory.\n\nThe C50 numerical batch is closed. C51 supplies its exact finite candidate.\nThe following is an analytic continuation of C48 with arbitrarily many\nphases, rather than another fixed graph enumeration.\n\n## 1. Real vectors and necessary terminal-normal constraints\n\nThere are K low/odd phase pairs, with indices 2j,2j+1 for 0<=j<K.\nThe last odd phase is terminal. Every ordinary block has total step mass\nM-1, M>=1. Low phases have total phase length MA, A=1+q, q>0;\nnonterminal odd phases have total length MB_j, B_j=1+Q_j, Q_j>0.\nThese total lengths include the following checkpoint. The last odd phase\nhas artificial gradient scale Mt with t>=1 and has no checkpoint.\n\nUse orthonormal coordinate vectors e_i and nonnegative target coefficients\nbeta_l and gamma_l. Low sources have weights u_j>=0,\nwith u_0>0, and send u_j beta_l to every odd coordinate 2l+1 with l>=j.\nNonterminal odd sources have weights v_j>=0 and send v_j gamma_l to\nevery even coordinate 2l with l>j. Set\n\n    U_j=sum_(i<j)u_i, U'_j=U_j+u_j,\n    V_j=sum_(i<j)v_i,\n    Y_j=sum_(l>=j)beta_l^2,\n    X_j=sum_(l>=j)gamma_l^2,        j>=1,\n    U_fin=U'_(K-1).\n\nFor j>=1 the mass at even coordinate 2j is V_j gamma_j. At odd\ncoordinate 2j+1 it is U'_j beta_j. The actual phase gradients are\n\n    p_(2j)=[V_j gamma_j e_(2j)-u_j sum_(l>=j)beta_l e_(2l+1)]/(MA),\n    p_(2j+1)=[U'_j beta_j e_(2j+1)-v_j sum_(l>j)gamma_l e_(2l)]/(MB_j).\n\nAt the root replace V_0 gamma_0 by the independent initial mass R0>0.\nAt the terminal phase use p_last=U_fin beta_last e_last/(Mt).\nSubtracting each phase length times its gradient removes its current\ncoordinate and creates the stated inherited masses at the next phase.\n\nIf all the projection normals hold for these actual vectors, they are\ngradients of the fixed function\n\n    F_C(x)=max_(p in C)(<x,p>-||p||^2/2),\n    C=conv(0,p_0,...,p_last).\n\nProjection nonexpansiveness proves that this is a convex differentiable\n1-smooth function with minimum at zero. All future normals, not just\nthose retained below, are required for this realization.\n\nAssume beta_last>0 and put D_tail=U_fin beta_last^2/t>0. At the last\nquery of a low ordinary block, the comparison with the terminal vertex\ngives\n\n    q V_j^2 gamma_j^2\n       >=u_j(AU_j+u_j)Y_j+A(AU_j+u_j)D_tail, j>=1.          (1)\n\nAt a nonterminal odd block, its corresponding comparison gives\n\n    Q_j (U'_j)^2 beta_j^2\n       >=v_j(B_j V_j+v_j)X_(j+1)+B_j^2 U'_j D_tail.         (2)\n\nAt the root it gives\n\n               q R0^2>=u_0^2 Y_0+A u_0 D_tail.             (3)\n\nFor clarity these are direct normal calculations, not consequences of a\nprevious unverified claim. At a low endpoint, after multiplying the\nnormal height by M, the own height is\n[q V_j^2 gamma_j^2-u_j(AU_j+u_j)Y_j]/A^2.\nIts inner product with the terminal gradient, with the same scaling, is\n(U_j+u_j/A)D_tail. This proves (1). At an odd endpoint the own height is\n[Q_j(U'_j)^2 beta_j^2-v_j(B_jV_j+v_j)X_(j+1)]/B_j^2;\nthe terminal height is U'_j D_tail. This proves (2). The same root\ncalculation, with U_0=0 and initial mass R0, gives (3).\n\nFor a legal member with D_tail>0, (1) forces V_j gamma_j>0 for j>=1,\nbecause U_j>=u_0>0. Likewise (2) forces every nonterminal odd mass\nU'_j beta_j>0. All diagonal coordinate masses are therefore positive.\nThe polar inequalities <p_i,z><=0 imply z_i<=0 by backward induction,\nstarting with the terminal coordinate. Hence zero is the actual closest\nminimizer to R0 e_0, with squared distance R0^2.\n\n## 2. An explicit all-horizon positive transfer\n\nFor positive denominators set\n\n    a_j=v_j(B_jV_j+v_j)/(Q_j(U'_j)^2),\n    b_j=B_j^2/(Q_j U'_j),\n    c_j=u_j(AU_j+u_j)/(q V_j^2),\n    d_j=A(AU_j+u_j)/(q V_j^2).\n\nEquations (1)--(2) imply the ordered backward inequalities\n\n    Y_j>=Y_(j+1)+a_j X_(j+1)+b_j D_tail,\n    X_j>=X_(j+1)+c_j Y_j+d_j D_tail.                       (4)\n\nThus the tail vector (X_j,Y_j) is bounded below by\n\n    [[1+c_j a_j,c_j],[a_j,1]] (X_(j+1),Y_(j+1))\n                       +(c_j b_j+d_j,b_j)D_tail.          (5)\n\nAll entries are nonnegative and the matrix determinant is one. The\nterminal data are X_K=0, Y_(K-1)=beta_last^2=D_tail t/U_fin;\nthere is no odd transfer equation (2) at the terminal phase. Equation\n(1) still applies to its preceding low phase. Apply the corresponding\nlow step first, then (4) for preceding pairs. Equations (3)--(5) give\na fully explicit lower bound on the actual initial squared norm for\nevery source-weight sequence and arbitrary number of phases.\n\nIf Yhat_0 is the lower bound on Y_0/D_tail produced by this ordered\naffine recursion, the original prescribed-terminal objective obeys\n\n  2F_C(x_end)/R0^2\n    <=q(2Mt-2M+1)U_fin/[M^2 t(u_0^2 Yhat_0+A u_0)].        (6)\n\nIndeed the terminal coordinate starts at U_fin beta_last, moves for\nordinary mass M-1 with gradient U_fin beta_last/(Mt), and hence its\nobjective is (2Mt-2M+1)(U_fin beta_last)^2/(2M^2t^2).\nDivide by (3). This bound is only for the stated prescribed terminal;\nno claim about all free terminal completions is made.\n\nThe full nonterminal normal inequalities have not been eliminated. A\nsmall value of the right side of (6) is a valid exclusion for this class;\na large value supplies no actual hard function by itself.\n\n## 3. Why source growth does not telescope through one tail alone\n\nA tempting simplification was to make U'_j Y_j nondecreasing in the\nbackward direction at every odd phase with negative older history.\nThe following exact local example refutes that inference from (4).\n\nPut s=sqrt(2), lambda_s=1+s,\n\n    q=s-1, A=s, Q=lambda_s^3=7+5s, B=1+Q,\n    U'_j=1, V_j=1, v_j=9, u_(j+1)=1,\n    U'_(j+1)=2, V_(j+1)=10,\n    D_tail=1, Y_(j+1)=1000, X_(j+2)=0.\n\nChoose the next low coordinate and current odd coordinate at equality:\n\n    x=gamma_(j+1)^2\n      =[(lambda_s^2)/100]*1000+(4+3s)/100,\n    X_(j+1)=x,\n    Y_j=1000+[9(Q+10)/Q]x+(Q+1)^2/Q.                     (7)\n\nThese satisfy the two retained terminal-normal equations exactly.\nThe coefficient of 1000 in Y_j is\n\n                 (37+108sqrt(2))/100 < 1339/700 < 2.       (8)\n\nThe additive term is less than 19: Q>14 gives\n9(Q+10)/Q<108/7, (4+3s)/100<9/100, and\n(Q+1)^2/Q=2+10s<17. Thus Y_j<2000 and\n\n                  U'_j Y_j<U'_(j+1)Y_(j+1).              (9)\n\nThe older-history sign is nevertheless negative. Its numerator is\n\n    V_j V_(j+1) gamma_(j+1)^2\n                           -U'_j u_(j+1)Y_(j+1)=10x-1000<0,\n\nbecause lambda_s^2<6 gives x<60+9/100. The parameters q and Q are\nthe critical low ratio and the level-four high ratio; the strict\ninequalities persist in a neighborhood of them.\n\nThis is a counterexample to the proposed scalar monotonicity inside the\nnecessary-condition relaxation. It is not advertised as a full legal\nprojection transcript. For consistent terminal-anchor data one may set\nt=2000, so D_tail=U_fin beta_last^2/t=1 when the next odd phase is\nterminal with U_fin=2 and beta_last^2=1000. Other earlier-query normals\nwould still have to be checked for any global realization.\n\nConsequently the C48 continuation must retain both tails in (5), or\nexploit the still-retained future nonterminal normals. Negative-history\nsource growth by itself has not provided the uniform normalization\ncharge needed for an asymptotic witness-class bound. The full-target\nupper and lower questions remain open.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"branch_id": "C48_bipartite_full_history", "artifact": "workers/max3/research/c52_bipartite_tail_transfer.md", "target_claim": "U'_j Y_j>=U'_(j+1)Y_(j+1) at every negative-history high phase, using only retained terminal-normal constraints.", "status": "refuted_in_the_stated_relaxation", "full_class_status": "open; all remaining future nonterminal normals and free completion must still be retained"}, "glossary": {"D_tail": "Positive terminal anchor U_fin beta_last^2/t.", "U_j": "Cumulative earlier low source weight in this bipartite construction.", "U_fin": "Total low source weight through the terminal pair.", "V_j": "Cumulative earlier high source weight in this bipartite construction.", "X_j": "Sum of squared future even target coefficients gamma_l^2 for l>=j.", "Y_j": "Sum of squared future odd target coefficients beta_l^2 for l>=j.", "Yhat_0": "Lower bound on Y_0/D_tail produced by the exact ordered affine tail recursion."}}
{"id": "ef2a9e6fbbaf7db5", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T19:43:57.616606+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "Scope audit of51463365645476f0: its equal two-anchor coefficient is NOT negative semidefinite on every added coordinate starting at zero. The exact row chi=Re(P1-P2)/5+(3/20)Im P1+Im P2 has limiting coefficient>1/2000. This is an algebraic certificate counterexample, NOT a legal GD adversary; the SAME row is itself bounded below benchmark by the pre-bridge anchor alone (limsup<=12/(5e)<.9).", "evidence": "# Two bridge-adjacent anchors suppress arbitrary PSD difference coupling\n\nStatus: complete candidate proof, NOT verifier-accepted. No routine fact_submit\nis allocated. The only accepted inputs are the interpolation convention and\nsmoothness proof in fact1252b8823bb5a290 and the benchmark recursions in\nfactb98bb807d514e80d. No earlier unverified covariance or spectral obstruction\nis used. All new estimates and the exact asymptotic comparison are proved here.\n\n## 1. The actual family and the scope of the claim\n\nFor integers m>=128 put lambda=1+sqrt(2), c_m=lambda(1+1/m),\na_m=(1-1/m)/c_m, d_j=1+a_m c_m^j, H_0=(), and\nH_(j+1)=(H_j,d_j,H_j). Parameters stay fixed within the horizon.\nLet n=2^m-1, q=n-1, and retain H_m^- by deleting ONLY the original\nlast step d_0. Write S_m=sum H_m^->0 and\nP_i(z)=product_(t<i)(1-h_t z) for the retained chronology, 0<=i<=q.\n\nFix zeta_1=(1+i)/2 and zeta_2=(4+i)/2, and z_l=zeta_l/S_m.\nChoose ANY w_m in [0,1] and ANY real matrix T_add,m with two columns,\nwithout an upper bound on its entries or number of rows. Set\nR(v)=(Re v,-Im v)^T and define actual Euclidean vectors\n\n x_i=(sqrt(w_m) Re P_i(z_1),sqrt(w_m) Im P_i(z_1),\n      sqrt(1-w_m) Re P_i(z_2),sqrt(1-w_m) Im P_i(z_2),\n      T_add,m R(P_i(z_1)-P_i(z_2))),\n g_i=(sqrt(w_m) Re[z_1 P_i(z_1)],sqrt(w_m) Im[z_1 P_i(z_1)],\n      sqrt(1-w_m) Re[z_2 P_i(z_2)],sqrt(1-w_m) Im[z_2 P_i(z_2)],\n      T_add,m R(z_1 P_i(z_1)-z_2 P_i(z_2))).                    (1)\n\nThen ||x_0||=1 and x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i exactly. The added coordinates\nstart at zero. The one-row choice T_add,m=sqrt(rho)(Re omega,Im omega)\nrecovers the whole proposed modela0f6a1f128f59b70, with every rho>=0 and\n|omega|=1. More generally M_add,m=T_add,m^T T_add,m is an arbitrary PSD2x2 matrix.\nSince only M_add,m affects the data, two added coordinates suffice.\n\nAssume arbitrary JOINT queried values, including the optimum(0,0,0),\nare interpolated by a convex differentiable1-smooth function F with\nF(0)=0=min F. No modewise legality or separable values are imposed.\nFor all sufficiently large m, uniformly over w_m,T_add,m,F, and b in[0,2],\n\n       2F(x_q-b g_q) <= 21/(50 S_m).                     (2)\n\nThus at the exact project benchmark U_n^ZJ=1/B_n,\n\n limsup_(m->infinity) sup_(w_m,T_add,m,F,b) B_n 2F(x_q-b g_q)\n       <=42/(25e)<63/100<1.                             (3)\n\nThe original last step b=d_0 belongs to[0,2]. This is a scoped\nobstruction to the stated two-mode PSD-difference class, including\nunbounded coupling strength and arbitrary joint values. It is not an\nupper bound on unrestricted W_n(H_m), or on every zero-initial\ncross-mode metric, or on arbitrary finite normal spectra.\n\n## 2. A single shared two-anchor interpolation inequality\n\nFor any queried index r, the sum of the actual inequalities\nQ_(q,r)<=0 and Q_(r,star)<=0 gives\n\n F(x_q) <= <g_q,x_q>+<g_r-g_q,x_r>\n                 -||g_r||^2/2-||g_q-g_r||^2/2.           (4)\n\nThis follows by expanding Q as in fact1252b8823bb5a290; the joint\nfunction value at r cancels. Drop the two nonnegative squares.\nTake r_-=2^(m-1)-1 and r_+=r_-+1, the actual queries immediately\nbefore and after the unique largest bridge d_(m-1). Average (4)\nwith coefficient1/2 on each. These are shared indices for every mode.\n\nFor l=1,2 define\n\n p_l,m=P_(r_-)(z_l), v_l,m=P_(r_+)(z_l), t_l,m=P_q(z_l),\n D_l,m=Re(zeta_l t_l,m conj(t_l,m))\n       +(1/2)Re[zeta_l(p_l,m-t_l,m)conj(p_l,m)\n                       +zeta_l(v_l,m-t_l,m)conj(v_l,m)].  (5)\n\nFor k in{-,+,q} let Y_k,m be respectively\np_1,m-p_2,m, v_1,m-v_2,m, t_1,m-t_2,m; let U_k,m be the same\ndifferences with the l-th term multiplied by zeta_l. For real column\nvectors a,b let sym(a,b)=(ab^T+ba^T)/2. Define the real symmetric matrix\n\n K_m=sym(R(U_q,m),R(Y_q,m))\n       +(1/2)sym(R(U_-,m-U_q,m),R(Y_-,m))\n       +(1/2)sym(R(U_+,m-U_q,m),R(Y_+,m)).                (6)\n\nThe averaged inequality is the EXACT finite-m bound\n\n S_m F(x_q) <= w_m D_1,m+(1-w_m)D_2,m+trace(M_add,m K_m).     (7)\n\nNo approximation, deletion of a prefix constraint, or scaling of\nthe initial norm occurs in (7). It suffices to show D_l,m<21/100\nand K_m negative semidefinite for every sufficiently large m.\n\n## 3. Exact scaled product limits for the actual truncated word\n\nLet delta=1-2/lambda=3-2sqrt(2). Direct summation gives\n\n S_m=2^m-2-a_m+a_m(c_m^m-2^m)/(c_m-2),\n S_m/lambda^m -> e/[lambda(lambda-2)]=e.                 (8)\n\nConsequently, for each fixed integer j>=0,\n\n d_(m-1-j)/S_m -> delta lambda^(-j).                     (9)\n\nIn the left H_(m-1) copy, d_(m-1-j) occurs2^(j-1) times for j>=1.\nHence, for either zeta=zeta_l,\n\n L(zeta)=product_(j>=1)(1-delta lambda^(-j)zeta)^(2^(j-1)),\n p_l,m -> p_l=L(zeta_l),\n v_l,m -> v_l=(1-delta zeta_l)p_l,\n t_l,m -> t_l=(1-delta zeta_l)p_l^2=p_l v_l.              (10)\n\nHere are convergence details, including the many small steps. Write\ne_j,m=d_(m-1-j)/S_m=1/S_m+A_m c_m^(-j), where\nA_m=a_m c_m^(m-1)/S_m ->delta and is bounded. The summed contribution\nof the1/S_m terms in the left copy is at most2^m/S_m ->0. The tail\nsum of the remaining terms over j>=J is bounded by a constant times\nsum_(j>=J)(2/lambda)^j, tending to zero uniformly as J->infinity.\nFor all large m, max_(j>=1)|zeta_l e_j,m|<1/2. On that disk the\nabsolutely convergent series for log(1-u) obeys\n|log(1-u)|<=2|u|. Fixed-j convergence from(9), followed by the tail\nbound, proves convergence of the log products and their exponentials.\nThe identities for v and t use the finite identities\n\n v_l,m=(1-d_(m-1)z_l)p_l,m,\n t_l,m=(1-d_(m-1)z_l)p_l,m^2/(1-d_0 z_l).\n\nThe latter denominator tends to1, since d_0 is bounded and S_m->infinity.\nThis explicitly retains the removed original d_0 and never replaces it\nby an appended unit step. Therefore D_l,m and K_m converge to D_l and K\nobtained by inserting p_l,v_l,t_l into(5)--(6).\n\n## 4. Rational enclosures for the limiting two-by-two matrix\n\nThe needed numerical-looking bounds below are rational inequalities,\nwith a finite power-series derivation. They do not rely on a solver.\nSince the left factors have power sums\n\n mu_r=sum_(j>=1)2^(j-1)(delta lambda^(-j))^r\n       =delta^r/(lambda^r-2),\n\nthe absolutely convergent logarithm is\n\n       log L(zeta)=-sum_(r>=1)mu_r zeta^r/r.              (11)\n\nPut A_4(zeta)=-sum_(r=1)^4 mu_r zeta^r/r. The maximum left coefficient\nis delta/lambda and eta=|zeta|delta/lambda<3/20 for both zeta values.\nAlso mu_2<1/125 and |zeta|^2<=17/4. Bounding mu_r by\nmu_2(delta/lambda)^(r-2), the tail obeys\n\n |log L-A_4| <= mu_2 |zeta|^2 eta^3/[5(1-eta)]\n             <27/10^6.                                 (12)\n\nThe inequality eta<3/20 follows from |zeta|<=sqrt(17)/2<21/10\nand delta/lambda<1/14. The inequality mu_2<1/125 follows by substituting\nlambda=1+sqrt(2), or the square-root enclosure immediately below.\n\nFor complete finite arithmetic details, enclose sqrt(2) by\n\n 14142135623/10^10 < sqrt(2) < 14142135624/10^10,\n\nwhose squared endpoints straddle2. Form lambda,delta,mu_1,...,mu_4\nusing interval addition, multiplication, and reciprocal of positive\nintervals; form A_4. Its real part is negative and its modulus is<1.\nEvaluate E_12(A)=sum_(j=0)^12 A^j/j! by the recurrence t_0=1,\nt_j=t_(j-1) A/j and E_12=sum t_j. Ordinary rational interval arithmetic\ngives the following outward enclosures (every finite decimal is exact):\n\n| zeta | Re E_12(A_4) | Im E_12(A_4) |\n|---|---|---|\n| (1+i)/2 | [.79526080,.79526082] | [-.16872576,-.16872574] |\n| (4+i)/2 | [.42009743,.42009746] | [-.09205447,-.09205445] |\n\nThe exponential-series tail is bounded by e/13!<3/13!. Because\nRe A_4<0, changing A_4 by the log tail changes its exponential by\nat most (27/10^6)/(1-27/10^6). The sum of these two errors is<31/10^6.\nThus the following coarser intervals safely enclose the exact p_l:\n\n| l | Re p_l | Im p_l |\n|---|---|---|\n| 1 | [.7952,.7953] | [-.1688,-.1686] |\n| 2 | [.4200,.4202] | [-.0921,-.0920] |\n\nFor clarity, the arithmetic in the last substitution can be reproduced\nwithout complex transcendental operations. For intervals a,b,c,d use\n(a+ib)(c+id)=(ac-bd)+i(ad+bc), with conjugation negating the imaginary\ninterval. Use delta in[.17157,.17158], set v_l=(1-delta zeta_l)p_l\nand t_l=p_l v_l, then evaluate(5)--(6) by these operations. The resulting\noutward rational bounds are\n\n 0<D_1<209/1000,        0<D_2<209/1000,\n -101/1000<K_11<-97/1000,\n -57/1000<K_22<-54/1000,\n -64/1000<K_12=K_21<-61/1000.                            (13)\n\nThese deliberately loose bounds avoid relying on printed floating digits.\nThey imply K_11<0 and\n\n det K >[(97)(54)-64^2]/10^6=1142/10^6>0.\n\nCompleting the square in a real two-variable quadratic now proves K is\nnegative definite. Since all its entries and D_l,m converge, for all\nsufficiently large m, K_m is negative definite and D_l,m<21/100.\nThis is uniform in w_m,M_add,m because these parameters do not enter the\ncoefficient limits. In particular M_add,m is allowed to diverge arbitrarily\nfast: trace(M_add,m K_m)<=0 holds at the EXACT finite m, before any limit.\nEquation(7) gives F(x_q)<=21/(100S_m).\n\nFor audit convenience only, two_anchor_interval_check.py repeats the\ndisplayed finite rational operations in a few milliseconds. Its output\nis not a replacement for(11)--(13) and their explicit remainder bounds.\nThe first script attempt had a Python Interval/ComplexInterval dispatch\nTypeError; reversing a scalar-complex multiplication repaired it. The\ncorrected script exits0 and checks all the displayed enclosures. No\noptimization call was made in this arithmetic check.\n\n## 5. Free terminal step and the exact original benchmark\n\nFor any actual interpolating F, integrating its1-Lipschitz gradient on\nthe segment from x_q to x_q-b g_q gives\n\n F(x_q-b g_q)<=F(x_q)-b(1-b/2)||g_q||^2<=F(x_q),\n\nfor every b in[0,2]. The terminal gradient is unrestricted. This proves(2).\n\nWe include the benchmark estimate rather than importing an unverified\ncomparison. Put r=log_2 lambda, C_k=S_circle(k)+1, and B_k=2S_bullet(k)+1.\nThe accepted recursions in factb98bb807d514e80d give\n\n C_0=B_0=1,\n C_n=max_(0<=j<n)Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),\n B_n=max_(0<=j<n)G(C_j,B_(n-j-1)),\n Omega(A,B)=(A+B+sqrt((A+B)^2+4AB))/2,\n G(A,B)=2A+B/2+sqrt(B^2+8AB)/2.\n\nFor V(t)=(1+t)^r-1, direct differentiation gives\n\n d^2/du^2 log V(exp u)\n =r t(1+t)^(r-2)[(1+t)^r-1-r t]/V(t)^2>0,  t=exp u.\n\nThe final strict positivity follows by differentiating\n(1+t)^r-1-r t, which vanishes at0 and has positive derivative for t>0.\nMidpoint convexity at u,-u implies\nV(t)V(1/t)>=(2^r-1)^2=(lambda-1)^2=2.\nFor A,B>0 set t=(A/B)^(1/r) and M=(A^(1/r)+B^(1/r))^r.\nThen (M-A)(M-B)>=2AB, so M>=Omega(A,B), by the positive root of\nthe quadratic and M>max(A,B). Induction yields C_k<=(k+1)^r.\nAlso sqrt(B^2+8AB)<=B+4A gives G(A,B)<=4A+B. Using\nu^r+v^r<=(u+v)^r for nonnegative u,v, induction yields B_n<=1+4n^r.\nThat elementary inequality follows by differentiating (1+t)^r-t^r.\n\nAt n=2^m-1, n^r/lambda^m->1, while(8) gives S_m/lambda^m->e.\nCombining this with(2) proves the first bound in(3). Finally\ne>1+1+1/2+1/6=8/3 gives42/(25e)<63/100.\n\n## 6. What remains open\n\nThis closes the concrete order-one two-block proposal, more broadly for\nevery PSD coupling of the real and imaginary mode differences. It does\nnot close all PSD additions annihilating the initial vector. That larger\nzero-initial subspace has dimension3: one may also independently combine\nIm P_i(z_1) and Im P_i(z_2), instead of requiring opposite coefficients.\nThe same two-anchor matrix on that three-dimensional space is not negative\nsemidefinite; a specific positive direction is screened separately.\nNeither such a positive certificate coefficient nor the finite solver\ndiagnostics supplies a legal trajectory. The next actual object must still\npass every joint prefix/optimum constraint and all terminal anchors, with\nthe original normalized profile constants.\n\n## 7. A precise failed extension, and why its positive row is not a witness\n\nDefine a real linear functional on two complex numbers by\n\n chi(v_1,v_2)=Re(v_1-v_2)/5+(3/20)Im v_1+Im v_2.         (14)\n\nIt vanishes at(1,1) and hence is a legitimate extra coordinate with\nzero initial value. It is outside the two-dimensional difference\nsubspace because its two imaginary coefficients are not opposites.\nLet y_-,y_+,y_q be chi applied to the pairs(p_1,p_2),(v_1,v_2),(t_1,t_2),\nand u_-,u_+,u_q be chi applied to those pairs after multiplying their\nl-th component by zeta_l. The averaged coefficient for this single\nextra coordinate is\n\n k_chi=u_q y_q+[(u_--u_q)y_-+(u_+-u_q)y_+]/2.            (15)\n\nUsing the same exact enclosures and interval rules of Section4 gives\n\n          1/2000 < k_chi < 1/1000.                     (16)\n\nFor reproducibility the direct interval substitution produces an\ninterval contained in[.000624,.000679]. Thus the matrix in the larger\nthree-dimensional zero-initial coordinate space\n(Re(P_1-P_2),Im P_1,Im P_2) has a positive quadratic direction.\nThis REFUTES the proposed algebraic extension that this particular\naveraged matrix is negative semidefinite on that entire space.\nIt does not refute any legal-trajectory upper bound: no interpolating\nvalues or function have been supplied for(14).\n\nIndeed, this specific direction is also suppressed by a different\nactual inequality. The pre-bridge anchor alone has coefficient\n\n k_chi,-=u_q y_q+(u_--u_q)y_-,\n          -1/5000 < k_chi,- < -1/10000.                 (17)\n\nThe associated two normal coefficients from(4) have rational bounds\n\n 0<D_1,-<207/1000,          0<D_2,-<293/1000.             (18)\n\nThe direct interval results lie in[-.000162,-.000104] for(17),\nand respectively[.20518,.20603],[.29155,.29216] for(18).\nAll bounds follow by substituting exactly the same p_l intervals\ninto(14)--(15) and the single-anchor version of(5). By finite-word\nconvergence, eventually the actual coefficient of the rank-one row\nsqrt(rho)chi is negative and both normal coefficients are<3/10.\nThe same proof as(7) and the free terminal descent give\n\n        2F(x_q-bg_q)<=3/(5S_m),\n        limsup B_n 2F(x_q-bg_q)<=12/(5e)<9/10,           (19)\n\nfor every legal member of this PARTICULAR additional-row family,\narbitrary rho>=0,w_m in[0,1], and b in[0,2]. The final strict bound\nuses e>8/3. Thus a positive coefficient for one tested certificate\nis not a legal payoff gain; changing the anchor rules out this row.\n\nWhat remains is the general3x3 PSD zero-initial coupling, with every\nactual interpolation cycle retained, or a genuinely different spectrum.\nNeither(16) nor(19) decides that larger class. Any later proof must derive\nits own full joint legality or shared coefficient inequalities, not treat\nthe positive direction of one certificate as an adversarial construction.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"target_claim": "The equal two-bridge-anchor coefficient remains negative semidefinite on the entire three-dimensional subspace of linear added coordinates vanishing initially.", "status": "refuted_algebraic_extension_only", "finding_ids": ["51463365645476f0", "1d4c55ac6a66b6f6"], "fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290", "b98bb807d514e80d"], "artifact": "workers/max2/research/two_bridge_anchor_coupling_obstruction.md", "impact": "Do not promote a positive coefficient as a legal witness or retune the chi row. {"id": "c5a096d73740bf81", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T20:22:04.929353+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "Actual LEGAL small-radius/full-metric counterexample: schedule(10,1), retainedS=10, real nodes(.01,.02,.03), explicit injective map with SPD metric and||x0||=1, explicit diagonal quadratic F, and ALL queried jets interpolated give2F(x2)>1/2 despiteS|z|<=.3<1/3.
