This is a finite scope audit, not an asymptotic resolution.", "evidence": "# Small modal radius does not control unrestricted common metrics\n\nStatus: complete UNVERIFIED construction and scope audit. No verifier or solver\ncall. This does not claim the original exact-profile target. The interpolation\nand terminal conventions are those of accepted fact 1252b8823bb5a290.\n\n## 1. An actual legal counterexample to a radius-only metric extension\n\nTake the schedule (10,1). Its retained queried prefix is the single step10,\nso its retained mass is S=10, with final free step b=1 in [0,2]. Use three\npositive real modal parameters\n\n        z_1=1/100, z_2=2/100, z_3=3/100.\n\nEvery S z_j is at most3/10<1/3. Let\n\n        alpha=(1128,-2303,1176).\n\nDirect integer arithmetic gives\n\n sum alpha_j=1,  sum alpha_j z_j=1/2,\n                         sum alpha_j z_j^2=1/4.        (1)\n\nDefine an injective real linear map A:R^3->R^4 by\n\n A(v_1,v_2,v_3)=(alpha_1 v_1+alpha_2 v_2+alpha_3 v_3,\n                                  v_1,v_2,v_3)/2.     (2)\n\nIts metric is the strictly positive definite matrix\nA^T A=(alpha alpha^T+I_3)/4; it includes nonzero initial correlations.\nAlso ||A(1,1,1)||=1 exactly. Define, on R^4,\n\n F(X)=X_0^2/4+X_1^2/200+X_2^2/100+3X_3^2/200.          (3)\n\nThis function is convex and differentiable, has gradient Lipschitz\nconstant1/2<=1, and has its unique minimizer at0. Thus it is admissible\nfor normalized smoothness1. Start at x_0=(1,1,1,1)/2. Its gradient is\ng_0=(1/2,1/100,2/100,3/100)/2. The actual first step gives\n\n x_1=(-4,9/10,8/10,7/10)/2,\n g_1=(-2,9/1000,16/1000,21/1000)/2.                     (4)\n\nFor P_0(z)=1 and P_1(z)=1-10z, identities (1) show exactly that\n\n x_i=A(P_i(z_1),P_i(z_2),P_i(z_3)),\n g_i=A(z_1 P_i(z_1),z_2 P_i(z_2),z_3 P_i(z_3)), i=0,1. (5)\n\nThe last gradient g_2 is unrestricted by the modal ansatz. The actual\nlast step x_2=x_1-g_1 has first coordinate -1, so\n\n                        2F(x_2) > 1/2.                (6)\n\nEvery prefix/optimum interpolation inequality holds, since these are\nactual jets of the explicit global function (3). The initial distance\nis exactly one and the minimizer is genuinely0. Thus this is an actual\nlegal polynomial-image trajectory, with a FULL SPD common metric,\nthree small positive REAL modes, and an allowed final step.\n\nIn particular it refutes the proposed upgrade that arbitrary common\nmetrics at S|z|<=1/3 must obey the normal-mixture Huber cap\n2F(x_1-b g_1)<=1/[2S+1+b(2-b)]. That right side is1/22 at b=1, whereas\n(6) is larger than1/2. The earlier normal and bounded-coupling theorems\ndid not assert such an upgrade, and this example does not contradict\nthem. It also does not contradict the specific two-complex-mode\nnear-critical obstruction in finding cebf601250c82dc2: the spectra,\nchronology, and metric assumptions are different.\n\n## 2. Why the scope changes when the number of modes grows\n\nThis is a warning about extrapolation, not a proposed arbitrary-Gram\nresearch program. For a positive schedule of length n>=1, select ANY\nn+1 distinct positive real nodes theta_j, even inside an arbitrarily\nsmall interval. Let P_i(z)=product_(t<i)(1-h_t z). The polynomials\n\n                  1, zP_0(z), ..., zP_(n-1)(z)          (7)\n\nhave successive degrees0,1,...,n and nonzero leading coefficients.\nThey form a basis of polynomials of degree at most n. Their evaluations\nat the n+1 distinct nodes are an invertible square matrix: a linear\ncombination vanishing at all nodes would be a polynomial of degree at\nmost n with n+1 roots, and hence zero; its basis coefficients must vanish.\nThis is the same polynomial-degree argument used in the strict-primal\nproof of accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290.\n\nGiven ANY actual normalized legal prefix vectors x_0,g_0,...,g_(n-1),\ndefine a real linear map A on the basis evaluation columns in (7) by\n\n A(1,...,1)=x_0,\n A((theta_j P_i(theta_j))_j)=g_i.\n\nInvertibility defines A uniquely. The polynomial identity\nP_i(z)=1-sum_(t<i)h_t zP_t(z) then gives\n\n A((P_i(theta_j))_j)=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t=x_i\n\nfor all0<=i<=n, including the unqueried final position. Thus every legal\nprefix has this modal representation at arbitrarily small positive nodes\nwhen the full PSD metric A^T A is unrestricted. The prefix values are\nunchanged, the initial norm is unchanged, and the terminal gradient stays\nfree, exactly as required in the reduced formulation of fact1252.\n\nZero steps cause identical queried polynomials and identical actual\njets at those positions. Retain one representative of each distinct\nqueried polynomial. Their degrees are distinct. They can be extended\nto a basis of all polynomials of degree at most n, and the unused basis\nvectors can be assigned arbitrary images, giving the same representation\nwith n+1 nodes. This also directly covers a last queried zero step,\nwithout assuming an unqueried endpoint gradient identity.\n\nConsequently the unrestricted full-metric, n+1-node modal formulation\nwith every joint interpolation constraint and a free terminal gradient\nis exactly as strong as the full original fixed-schedule problem. It\ndoes not furnish a new lower bound by itself. A radius argument alone\ncannot make this enlarged formulation simpler. A scalable proof must\nidentify additional genuine structure, or solve the full interpolation\nproblem it encodes.\n\n## 3. Implication for the next model\n\nThe completed two-mode bound controls a fixed four-dimensional temporal\npolynomial span, not merely the ambient Euclidean dimension or spectral\nradius. Changing only the metric within that precise span is now closed\nby the candidate proof. A new direction must change the temporal span or\nthe trajectory mechanism and still prove all joint legality and the\ncorrect benchmark payoff. Neither the finite example (6) nor the exact\nrepresentation (7) is an asymptotic answer to the original problem.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"status": "complete_candidate_unverified", "target_claim": "Radius-only extension of normal-mixture Huber cap to arbitrary common PSD metrics.", "assumptions_satisfied": ["Actual finite-dimensional convex smooth function with minimizer0", "All queried and optimum jets genuinely legal", "Exact GD schedule(10,1)", "Initial norm1", "Three positive real modes withretainedS times radius<=.3", "Strictly positive definite common metric", "Free terminal gradient andb=1"], "failed_conclusion": "2F(x2)<=1/[2S+1+b(2-b)]=1/22", "impact": "Initial correlations and temporal-span dimension are load-bearing; fixed two-mode closure cannot be extrapolated to all modes.", "fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290"], "finding_ids": ["cebf601250c82dc2", "f42b4c43d2ea0c3a"], "artifact": "workers/max2/research/full_metric_small_spectrum_scope_audit.md", "new_optimization_evaluations": 0, "new_verifier_calls": 0}, "glossary": {"alpha_row": "Real row(1128,-2303,1176), with moments sum alpha=1, sum alpha*z=1/2, sum alpha*z²=1/4.", "A_map": "Injective map R3->R4 takingv to(alpha_row dot v,v1,v2,v3)/2; metric(alpha_row alpha_row^T+I3)/4 is SPD.", "F_quad": "Explicit global quadraticX0²/4+X1²/200+X2²/100+3X3²/200, convex and1/2-smooth.", "theta_j": "Distinct positive real spectral nodes used in the separate scope-representation argument."}}

Define\n\n A_tail=(d-1)/(2T_tail+1)>0,\n f'(x)=max(-A_tail,min(1,x)), f(0)=0, x_0=1.           (39)\n\nThis derivative is monotone, continuous and1-Lipschitz, so its integral is\none convex1-smooth function with unique minimizer0. The first query has\ngradient1; after the actual first step d the point is\n\n x_1=1-d=-A_tail(2T_tail+1).\n\nEvery later query stays at most -A_tail(T_tail+1)<-A_tail, since the remaining\ntotal step mass is T_tail. Thus every later gradient is the constant -A_tail.\nAt the actual end of H_m the full normalized payoff is exactly\n\n             2f(x_n)=(d-1)^2/(2T_tail+1)>0.            (40)\n\nIn fact this also REFUTES extension of the super-small bound (28) to a double\nzero root, for every m>=128. Count the levels to obtain\n\n 1+S_total=A_mass c^m+(1-A_mass)2^m,\n A_mass=a/(c-2) in(0,1).\n\nHere A_mass<1 follows from ac<1=lambda(lambda-2)<c(c-2). Hence S_total<c^m,\nand (10) implies\n\n                  2f(x_n)>(3/64)c^(-m).               (40a)\n\nThis exceeds100000 exp(-m^3/4): logc<1 and\nlog(100000*64/3)<15, while m^3/4-m>15 for m>=128.\nThus the zero-root multiplicity in Section7 is quantitatively load-bearing,\nnot only needed for the exact-zero statement in Section3.\n\nAll ordered constraints, including every queried value and the optimum, hold\nbecause these are actual jets of the single explicitly defined function (39).\n\nThis transcript belongs to the growing class\n\n              chi=z^2 product_(j=0)^(m-1)(z-mu_j),     (41)\n\nof degree m+2. To specify the real map T on the quotient, define it directly\non a polynomial p by\n\n T[p]=u p(0)+v p'(0)+d w p(mu_0),\n v=-A_tail, w=1+A_tail, u=1-dw.                       (42)\n\nEvaluation at0, the derivative at0 and evaluation atmu_0 all vanish on\nmultiples of chi, so this is a well-defined quotient map. The remaining\nlevel coordinates are allowed but have zero image. For every i,\n\n x_i=u-s_i v+d w P_i(mu_0),\n g_i=v+w P_i(mu_0),  s_i=sum_(t<i)h_t.\n\nP_0(mu_0)=1 and P_i(mu_0)=0 for every i>=1, because the first step is d_0.\nThus these formulas reproduce exactly (39), and T[1]=u+dw=1. The common\nmetric T^T T is real PSD; no nonlinear or time-dependent map has been used.\nIn particular v*(dw)=-A_tail d(1+A_tail)<0. Hence the tempting cross-term\ninequality <v,eliminated_component>>=0 is already false on legal data.\n\nThe function is genuinely nonquadratic on the transcript: a quadratic with\nminimum at0 and g_0=1 atx_0=1 would have curvature1, while its gradient atx_1\nwould be1-d, not -A_tail. For large positive T_tail these differ. Its exact\npayoff is the known weak first-cut bound, so (40) is NOT an improved\nadversary or a profile resolution. It proves only that a double-zero\nresidual and its negative cross terms must be handled in any extension of\nSections3 or7. Arbitrary shifted upper-level nodes, double-zero blocks and\nchanging generators remain open after these scoped obstructions.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290", "b98bb807d514e80d"], "target_claim": "Extending the simple-zero-root annihilation or exp(-m³/4) obstruction to double-zero roots; or assuming nonnegative terminal-gradient correlation with the eliminated component.", "assumptions_satisfied": ["Actual H_m and every queried jet of one explicit legal function", "Unique minimizer and actual initial distance1", "Real common PSD metric in a growing m+2-dimensional temporal quotient"], "failed_conclusion": "Zero or super-small terminal payoff; nonnegative eliminated-component correlation.", "impact": "Must retain the polynomial suffix and signed common-metric cross terms. {"id": "456e36fd0eb02833", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T21:42:25.226267+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "Two ACTUAL legal scope counterexamples for joint positive-height draft28db18b9be162b29. (1) On the actual H_m, scalar A=3/2, w0=-3/4, all other w=0, and one positive common height array satisfy EVERY original Q and have a unique minimizer, but N_(0,q)>0. Thus even well-conditioned positive quadratic heights plus Q do not imply the metric-normal policy. (2) An actual nonquadratic A=diag(d_top,1) object has highest-mode Rayleigh quotient exactly d_top but the full queried gradient at the central bridge has Rayleigh quotient<2<d_top/2. Thus top-mode cost alone cannot justify the whole-gradient norm budget without additional control. Neither example violates the stated payoff bound or provides a new sharp adversary.", "evidence": "## 8. Actual counterexamples to two tempting extensions of the proof\n\n### 8.1 Positive quadratic heights and all Q do NOT imply the normals\n\nThis example is on the SAME actual word, not an unrelated matrix example.\nKeep m>=128, d=d_0, S=S_m-d_0, and set\n\n A=3/2 on R, t=3/4, k=(d-5/4)/4, v=kt/S,\n w_0=-t, w_j=0 for j>=1, u=dt-1.\n\nThen x_0=-1, x_i=u-s_i v for i>=1, g_0=v-t, and g_i=v for i>=1.\nSet the ONE common value array\n\n f_0=v x_0-3v^2/4+3t^2/4,\n f_i=v x_i-3v^2/4 for i>=1, f_star=0.\n\nThese are exactly (6), with positive heights delta_0=t^2/4=9/64,\ndelta_i=0 for i>=1, and delta_star=v^2/4>0. A obeys the spectral condition\n(3), with condition number one. Yet the normal condition (4) fails.\n\nHere are EVERY original interpolation comparisons. For suffix i,j>=1,\nQ_ij=0. For every i>=1,\n\n Q_(0,i)=(5/4-d)t^2+s_i t v <=-3kt^2<0,\n Q_(i,0)=-t^2/4<0,\n Q_(i,star)=-v^2/4<0,\n Q_(star,i)=5v^2/4-v x_i<0.\n\nAt the initial query,\n\n Q_(0,star)=5t^2/4-t-tv-v^2/4=-3/64-tv-v^2/4<0,\n Q_(star,0)=-t^2/4+(1-t)v+5v^2/4<0.\n\nTo check the displayed strict signs without a numerical check, use\n11/8<d<3/2, which follows from m>=128 and\na>(127/128)/(5/2)>3/8. Thus 1/32<k<1/16, S>=16,\nand 0<v<3/1024<1/64. Moreover\n\n x_q=t(d-k)-1> (3/4)(43/32)-1=1/128\n                                      >5v/4.\n\nAll suffix x_i>=x_q. The last initial-star expression is at most\n-9/64+1/256+5/16384<0. The bound S>=16 follows from\nS>=S_4-d_0=7d_0+4d_1+2d_2+d_3>7(11/8)+4+2+1=133/8>16.\n\nAccepted fact1252 therefore supplies one actual convex1-smooth maximal\ninterpolant of ALL these jets. It has UNIQUE minimizer zero: in that fact's\nconjugate maximum of quadratics, Q_(star,i)<0 says the star quadratic is the\nunique active term at gradient zero; its derivative there is zero. Hence the\nsubdifferential of the conjugate at zero is {0}, exactly the minimizer set\nof the interpolant. The true initial distance is consequently1.\n\nBut its proposed metric normal satisfies\n\n              N_(0,q)=(k+3/2-d)t^2>0.\n\nThus all ordinary interpolation inequalities and one positive quadratic\nheight matrix do NOT imply (4), even with A perfectly conditioned. This\nexample is a scope counterexample, not a counterexample to the payoff bound\n(20); its payoff remains of the known weak scale. No claim about the entire\nQ-only quadratic-height policy follows from it.\n\n### 8.2 Highest-mode Rayleigh cost does NOT transfer to the full gradient\n\nDrop only the condition-number cap. Let d=d_(m-1)>4, retain S_m as the full\nmass, and put\n\n A=diag(d,1),\n U=(1+[10(2S_m+1)]^(-2))^(-1/2),\n p=U/(2S_m+1), epsilon=p/(10d),\n u=(0,U), v=(0,p), w_(m-1)=(epsilon,0), other w_j=0.\n\nThe actual initial point is (p/10,U), of norm1. For every query,\nz_i=(0,U-s_i p), while z_star=(0,p). Since\nU-S_m p=U(S_m+1)/(2S_m+1)>=p, their convex hull is\nC={0} x [p,U]. Every queried residual is horizontal, so its dot product\nwith every difference in C is zero. The star residual (0,-p) gives a\nnonpositive normal comparison with each point in C. Thus every inequality\n(4), including the optimum comparisons, holds. Its single function is\n\n F_A(x,y)=x^2/(2d)+dist(y,[p,U])^2/2+p y-p^2/2,\n\nwith unique minimizer zero. It has strictly positive queried heights before\nthe central annihilation and normalized final payoff U^2/(2S_m+1).\nThe highest residual direction satisfies <w,A w>=d||w||^2 exactly.\n\nNevertheless, at the actual central query before the d step, its gradient\nis g=(epsilon p_(i,m-1),p), and 0<p_(i,m-1)<=1 implies\n\n <g,A g>/||g||^2\n   =1+(d-1)epsilon^2 p_(i,m-1)^2/(p^2+epsilon^2 p_(i,m-1)^2)\n   <1+1/(100d)<2<d/2.\n\nHence the proposed extension 'the highest-mode Rayleigh identity implies\n<g_i,A g_i>>=h_i||g_i||^2/2 for every earlier gradient' is FALSE even for an\nactual legal object in this positive-height family. The example does not\nshow a distance increase or violate (20); it invalidates that attempted\nreplacement of the condition-number argument. A different common budget\nwould be needed to treat unbounded metric conditioning.\n\n## 9. The common initial-height budget without any metric policy\n\nThere is an exact necessary budget for the GENERAL double-zero data (1),\nwith arbitrary jointly legal values. Define C_min=f_q-<v,x_q>,\ndelta_i=f_i-<v,x_i>-C_min-||R_i||^2/2, and\neta=delta_star=-C_min-||v||^2/2. The tilted function F-<v,.> is minimized\nat every constant-gradient suffix point. Smooth convex interpolation with\none of these minima proves delta_i>=0 and eta>=0.\n\nPut R_0=sum_j w_j and Z_0=sum_j d_j w_j. The single actual optimum inequality\nQ_(0,star)<=0 is EXACTLY\n\n       eta >= delta_0+||R_0||^2+<v,R_0>-<R_0,x_0>.    (25)\n\nIndeed substitute f_0=<v,x_0>-||v||^2/2-eta+||R_0||^2/2+delta_0\nand g_0=v+R_0 into that inequality. The original unqueried last step has\n\n 2F(x_q-bv)<=2<v,x_0>-[2S+1+b(2-b)]||v||^2\n                                      -2<v,Z_0>-2eta.       (26)\n\nThus for EVERY theta in[0,1], combining (25), eta>=0, and ||x_0||=1 gives\n\n 2F(x_q-bv)<=2||v+theta R_0||-[2S+1+b(2-b)]||v||^2\n     -2<v,Z_0>-2theta[delta_0+||R_0||^2+<v,R_0>].     (27)\n\nNo mode-specific values or signs were used. This is a necessary shared\ninitial budget, not a sufficient legality test or a target-scale bound.\nWhat is still missing in the arbitrary-height model is a simultaneous lower\nbound on delta_0 strong enough to offset -<v,Z_0> in (27), while retaining\nall the other ordered interpolation inequalities. The isolated level charge\nfrom the earlier draft cannot simply be summed to supply that bound.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290"], "finding_ids": ["28db18b9be162b29", "10bd4d79197b0379"], "artifact": "research/joint_positive_height_metric_obstruction.md", "status": "refuted", "tested_extensions": ["all Q with positive quadratic heights imply all metric normals", "highest-mode Rayleigh identity gives the same lower Rayleigh bound on every full gradient"]}, "glossary": {"A": "Common positive height operator; first example3/2, second diag(d_top,1).", "N_ij": "Metric normal <R_i,z_j-z_i> with z_i=x_i-A R_i.", "R_i": "Residual gradient g_i-v in the exact double-zero model.", "d_top": "Largest actual ruler step d_(m-1).", "k": "Positive scalar (d_0-5/4)/4 in the first construction.", "t": "Scalar residual magnitude3/4 in the first construction.", "S": "Actual queried-prefix sum S_m-d_0.", "eta": "Shared star height -C_min-||v||²/2.", "delta_0": "Shared initial positive height in the general double-zero model."}}
{"id": "690761f5e926551d", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T21:57:11.459114+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "The O(M_m^-2) bound in signed-interval draft e09fd8046ee1daf2 cannot be extended to arbitrary endpoint heights, even within the identical coordinate-history rule: an exact Huber member has payoff1/(2M_m-1)>3/M_m² for M_m>=6 and satisfies the shared height-charge inequalities with equality.", "evidence": "Normalize L=1 and take the actual nonnegative word H_m of sumS=M_m-1. Let tau=1/(2S+1) and F(x)=x²/2 for|x|<=tau, F(x)=tau|x|-tau²/2 otherwise. Its derivative is continuous, monotone and1-Lipschitz; its unique minimizer is0. Fromx0=1, every original sample isx_i=1-tau sum_(t<i)h_t>=tau, withg_i=tau andf_i=tau x_i-tau²/2. Thus its true initial distance is1 and2f_n=tau=1/(2M_m-1). SinceM²>6M-3 forM>=6, this exceeds3/M². For ANY dyadic ordinary-cell cutoff withC=1+sumH_ell and checkpoints1+r_j, implement the signed interval rule by Aamp_k=tau and all other Aamp_j andBamp_j zero, so everyp_j=tau e_k. At the endpointZ_j, delta_j=f(Z_j)-||p_j||²/2=tau Z_j-tau²>0. The actual endpoint displacement is(C+r_j)tau; hence delta_j-delta_(j+1)=(C+r_j)tau². The exact interpolation charge (C+r_j)Eref_(j+1)+C Bamp_j²-r_j Aamp_j Bamp_j<=delta_j-delta_(j+1) is therefore equality, withEref_j=tau². Also delta0=tau-C tau² makes the radius bound (C||p0||²+delta0)²/||p0||² exactly1. This refutes ONLY removal of the zero-height hypothesis from the scoped draft bound. It supplies no improvement over Huber and does not settle the positive-height construction problem.", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"target_claim": "Extend V_ref<3/M_m² from zero endpoint heights to arbitrary endpoint heights without changing the coordinate rule.", "status": "refuted", "finding_ids": ["e09fd8046ee1daf2"], "branch_id": "signed_interval_joint_primal"}, "glossary": {}}
{"id": "a6a07e7cfa5a843c", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T22:08:05.154185+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "At EVERY exact ZJ horizon n>=2 a fixed finite root-dispersion transfer lowers both canonical Huber and unit-quadratic payoffs but forces actual B_n W_n>25/16. Proved nonempty maximizing root prefixes by a strict DP rotation, chose an actual sqrt2 primitive leaf, and gave the explicit legal root-cut instance. This rules out every full-array continuation on the specified transfer family, including arbitrary support, positive optimum-source weights, and higher-rank PSD.", "evidence": "# A finite all-horizon obstruction to a root dispersion transfer\n\nStatus: **unverified candidate**, cumulative round 14. No solver or verifier\ncall. This is a class obstruction and a counterexample to the adequacy of two\nprimal tests, not a resolution of the unrestricted asymptotic problem.\n\nThe only accepted premises are the exact P/B recurrences and the explicit\nsingle-cut lower bound in `b98bb807d514e80d`, and the actual canonical Huber\nconstruction in `0be8b54e0a33381c`. All further algebra is proved here. The\nsource words coincide with arXiv:2410.12395v2 Definitions 4.1/4.3, but no\nexternal closure theorem or earlier unverified finding is a proof premise.\nMatlas was queried for related rotation results and returned unrelated hits.\n\n## Statement\n\nPut rho=1+sqrt(2), a=sqrt(2)-1. Let p^(n),d^(n) be the exact primitive and\ndominant words selected by always choosing the SMALLEST maximizing split in\nthe accepted recurrences, and then using the actual bridges phi and psi.\nIn detail P_n=1+S_circle(n), B_n=1+2S_bullet(n), P_0=B_0=1,\n\n    P_n=max_k Omega(P_k,P_(n-k-1)),\n    B_n=max_k G(P_k,B_(n-k-1)),\n    Omega(A,E)=(A+E+sqrt((A+E)^2+4AE))/2,\n    G(A,D)=2A+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8AD)/2.\n\nAn empty word has mass 1. A primitive parent of masses A,E has word\n(left,phi(A-1,E-1),right). A dominant parent of masses A,D has word\n(primitive left,psi(A-1,(D-1)/2),dominant right). This is a finite exact\nrecursive algorithm, with all choices made before querying a function.\n\nFor EVERY n>=2, its dominant root has a nonempty primitive prefix. Write\nthat root as\n\n    d^(n)=(p^(k),1+d,d^(l)),   k>=1, l=n-k-1,\n    A=P_k, D=B_l, B_n=G(A,D).\n\nSelect the first occurrence of sqrt(2) in that prefix. There is at least\none. For any real t in (0,1], define the actual new word h^(n,t) by changing\nonly this selected entry and the root bridge:\n\n    sqrt(2)  ->  1+a(1-t),\n    1+d      ->  1+(1+t/2)d.                         (1)\n\nEvery new step is nonnegative, in fact at least one. Both the canonical\nHuber payoff and the payoff of f(x)=x^2/2 decrease strictly below 1/B_n,\nyet the actual worst-case value obeys\n\n    B_n W_n(h^(n,t))\n      >= (1+t/2)^2 [A/(A-a t)]^2\n      >  (1+t/2)^2.                                (2)\n\nIn particular, the fixed deformation t=1/2 is defined at every horizon\nn>=2 and satisfies\n\n    B_n W_n(h^(n,1/2)) > 25/16.                    (3)\n\nThis is an exact obstruction with a uniform finite interval of allowed\ndeformations. It applies to ANY full multiplier/remainder array at the new\nword, including new positive optimum-source coefficients, new interpolation\nedges, and higher-rank remainders. It is a legal primal obstruction, not a\nrestriction of certificate support.\n\n## 1. No empty maximizing dominant prefix for n>=2\n\nFor A>0,z>=0 define\n\n    T_A(z)=(z+sqrt(z^2+8A))/2.\n\nThen sqrt(G(A,D))=T_A(sqrt(D)), and T_A is increasing in A and z, as follows\ndirectly from the formula. We prove the strict rotation inequality\n\n    T_(Omega(1,A))(z) > T_1(T_A(z))   for A>=1,z>=1. (4)\n\nPut C=Omega(1,A), u=T_A(z), v=T_1(u). The defining quadratics give\n\n    C^2=(A+1)C+A,\n    u^2=z u+2A,  u>=2,\n    v(v-u)=2.\n\nConsequently\n\n    u^2(C+1)-2C^2\n       =z u(C+1)-2C >=2(C+1)-2C=2>0.\n\nLet r=v/u>1. Then r(r-1)=2/u^2, while\n(1+1/C)(1/C)=(C+1)/C^2. Since r(r-1) is strictly increasing for r>=1,\nthe preceding strict inequality implies r<1+1/C. Therefore\n\n    v(v-z)/2 = A v/u+1 < A(1+1/C)+1=C.\n\nThe positive root of w(w-z)/2=C is T_C(z); the polynomial is strictly\nincreasing for w>=z. Since v>z, the last inequality proves (4).\n\nSuppose k=0 were a maximizing split for a horizon n>=2. Choose an actual\nmaximizing split j of its nonempty right child at horizon n-1, and let\nA=P_j, D=B_(n-j-2). Its root value would be G(1,G(A,D)). But\nP_(j+1)>=Omega(1,A), because that is one allowed primitive split. The\nalternative root split j+1 at horizon n has value\n\n    G(P_(j+1),D) >= G(Omega(1,A),D) > G(1,G(A,D)),\n\nby (4). This contradicts maximality. Thus k>=1 for every maximizing tree,\nindependently of the specified tie rule. No eventually fixed or dyadic\nmaximizing pattern has been assumed.\n\nEvery nonempty finite primitive binary construction has an internal node\nwhose two children are empty: choose an internal node of largest depth.\nIts bridge is phi(0,0)=sqrt(2), so the selected occurrence exists. Since\nall other steps are nonnegative, A>=1+sqrt(2)=rho.\n\n## 2. Exact product and root identities\n\nFor a primitive parent put C=Omega(A,E), d_P=phi(A-1,E-1)-1. Substitution\ngives C=A+E+d_P and d_P C=AE. Inducting from the empty word yields\n\n    product_(i in p^(n)) |1-h_i|=1/P_n.             (5)\n\nAt a dominant join let r=(1+sqrt(1+8A/D))/2. Direct substitution gives\n\n    d=A/r,\n    r^2-r=2A/D,\n    G(A,D)=D r^2,\n    2d^2+Dd=AD.                                    (6)\n\nInducting from its empty word, the dominant product is\nd/(A sqrt(D))=1/sqrt(G(A,D)). Thus\n\n    product_(i in d^(n)) (1-h_i)^2=1/B_n,\n    d^2/(A^2 D)=1/B_n.                              (7)\n\nAll source steps exceed one. In particular the product contribution of the\nselected prefix entry is a>0, and that of the root bridge is d>0.\nWe need the strict inequality d>2a. For the polynomial\nF(z)=2z^2+Dz-AD, which is strictly increasing at z>=0 and vanishes at d,\nuse A>=rho, D>=1 to obtain\n\n    F(2a)=8a^2-D(A-2a)\n          <=8a^2-(rho-2a)=21-15sqrt(2)<0.\n\nThe last inequality follows from sqrt(2)>7/5, proved by squaring. Hence\nd>2a, as required.\n\n## 3. The two decreasing tests\n\nThe change (1) increases the physical step sum by\n\n    Delta s=t(d/2-a)>0.                             (8)\n\nThe exact canonical Huber instance for the NEW word consequently has\nnormalized payoff\n\n    1/[B_n+2t(d/2-a)] < 1/B_n.\n\nThe unit quadratic has x_0=1 and produces exactly the product of squared\nstep gaps. Using (7), its normalized payoff after (1) is\n\n    [(1-t)(1+t/2)]^2/B_n < 1/B_n,                  (9)\n\nbecause (1-t)(1+t/2)=1-t/2-t^2/2 belongs to [0,1) for t in (0,1].\nThese are actual, normalized legal instances. Their smaller values do not\ngive an upper bound on W_n of the changed word.\n\n## 4. The legal cut that rules out every full-array continuation\n\nUse the exact single-cut instance of accepted b98bb807d514e80d at the\nROOT bridge of the altered word. Its prefix mass is A-a t, the bridge\ngap is (1+t/2)d, and its suffix mass remains D. That accepted construction\nis valid for arbitrary nonnegative prefix and suffix steps, so it applies\nto every chronological sample of (1). Its true initial distance is one,\nits minimizer is zero, and its exact normalized objective payoff is\n\n    ((1+t/2)d)^2 / [(A-a t)^2 D].                   (10)\n\nFor clarity, its derivative at smoothness one is\nmax(-c_minus,min(c_plus,x)), with\n\n    c_plus=1/(A-a t),\n    c_minus=c_plus(1+t/2)d/D,\n    x_0=1.\n\nThe prefix has constant positive gradient c_plus, reaches x_p=c_plus,\nand the bridge sends it to -D c_minus. All suffix gradients remain\n-c_minus, so its final value is D c_minus^2/2. Thus (10) includes the\nactual initial distance and does not reset at an intermediate point.\n\nMultiplying (10) by B_n and using the second identity in (7) gives (2).\nAt t=1/2, (1+t/2)^2=25/16, proving (3). A valid full PSD upper\ncertificate q must satisfy q>=W_n, so no choice of its coefficients,\nsupport, optimum incidences, or rank can yield q B_n<1 on this family.\n\n## Scope and next interface\n\nThis eliminates a concrete gap-dispersion mechanism which escapes global\ndoubly stochastic averaging and the unchanged ConPD rule. Both source\nchildren are genuine exact-benchmark words. The bridge and a primitive\nleaf change, with a uniform relative bridge change at all horizons, and\nthe complete certificate may be reselected arbitrarily. The obstruction\nuses one legal hard function and a fixed multiplicative gap.\n\nIt does not eliminate joint changes which also increase the root prefix\nmass or sufficiently increase the suffix mass, nor arbitrary distributions\nof changes across an unbounded set of scales. Such a candidate must pass\nthe root cut as well as the Huber/quadratic tests and must then establish\nthe full PSD continuation with a quantitatively useful radius.
