{"id": "501aba74c8acfcdf", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T23:07:19.181350+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "max2 exact CHRONOLOGY counterexample to the proposed shortcut: even ACTUAL nearcritical ruler levels, the same physical step multiset and total mass, ordered first occurrences, totally nonnegative stochastic Newton jump columns, and exact occupation moments do not force elapsed-time storage PSD. Moving only the intervening d0 in (d0,d1,d0,d2) to after d2 admits a formal rank-one direction with J<-19/10 for every m>=128. This is a permuted-word certificate test, explicitly ILLEGAL as interpolation data, and does NOT refute the original-word T claim or bound W.", "evidence": "# Actual Newton chronology: a birth chain and a joint covariance charge\n\nStatus: UNVERIFIED proof candidate and partial storage argument. The exact\ncoordinate identities and ordered-kernel result below are complete candidates.\nThe covariance domination in Section 5 is OPEN. In particular there is no\nwhole-interface error bound, no legal benchmark-scale construction, and no\nresolution of the original exact-profile problem in this file. No optimization,\nnumerical experiment, installation, or fact submission was used in this round.\n\nAccepted mathematical inputs: ordinary smooth interpolation and scaling in\nfact 1252b8823bb5a290, and the benchmark definitions in b98bb807d514e80d.\nThe total-nonnegativity assertion also uses the explicitly identified and\naudited published theorem in Section 3. No earlier unverified max2 result is\na premise: the identities needed for this argument are derived here.\n\n## 1. The actual interface and notation\n\nFor an integer m>=128, put\n\n lambda=1+sqrt(2), c=lambda(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c,\n d_j=1+a c^j, H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,d_j,H_j).\n\nSet n=2^m-1 and q=n-1. The queried steps h_0,...,h_(q-1) are the word\nH_m with its final d_0 deleted. The actual final step is b in [0,2]. Define\n\n s_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, S=s_q,\n p_ij=product_(t<i)(1-h_t/d_j),\n R_i=sum_(j<m)p_ij w_j, Z_i=sum_(j<m)d_j p_ij w_j,\n x_i=u-s_i v+Z_i, g_i=v+R_i, 0<=i<=q.\n\nAll these vectors use ONE arbitrary real PSD Gram matrix. The values f_i\nform ONE array; the optimum jet is x_star=g_star=f_star=0; ||x_0||=1 is\nthe distance normalization relative to the chosen actual minimizer. Every\noriginal queried inequality is retained:\n\n Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0.       (1)\n\nThe first d_j occurs at step 2^j-1. Thus p_ij is positive before query\n2^j and zero thereafter, and R_q=Z_q=0. The recursion gives\n\n Z_i-Z_(i+1)=h_i R_i, sum_i h_i p_ij=d_j.             (2)\n\nHere and below unqualified time sums range over 0<=i<q.\n\nFor clarity about the original value budget, define\n\n C_min=f_q-<v,x_q>, eta=-C_min-||v||^2/2,\n delta_i=f_i-<v,x_i>-C_min-||R_i||^2/2.\n\nOn an actual legal function, tilting by -<v,x> makes x_q a minimizer;\nthe smooth optimum inequalities give delta_i>=0. Also eta>=0 follows\nfrom Q_(q,star). Expanding Q_(0,star) gives the exact common budget\n\n eta>=delta_0+||R_0||^2+<v,R_0>-<R_0,x_0>.          (3)\n\nNo height is set to zero and neither eta nor the norm of x_0 is discarded.\n\n## 2. A lossless stochastic change of coordinates\n\nDefine polynomials\n\n phi_k(z)=product_(r<k)(1-d_r z), 0<=k<=m.\n\nWork modulo z phi_m(z), which has the m+1 distinct nodes\n0,1/d_0,...,1/d_(m-1). The double zero in the position quotient leaves\nexactly this simple-zero gradient quotient. Put\n\n V_k=v+sum_(j<m)phi_k(1/d_j) w_j, 0<=k<=m.\n\nThen V_0=g_0 and V_m=v. This change from (w_0,...,w_(m-1),v) to\n(V_0,...,V_m) is invertible: the evaluation matrix\nphi_k(1/d_j), with columns ordered j=0,...,m-1, then the node0, is\nupper triangular with strictly positive diagonal.\n\nThere are coefficients B_ik determined by B_00=1, B_0k=0 for k>0 and\n\n B_(i+1),0=(1-h_i/d_0)B_i0,\n B_(i+1),k=(1-h_i/d_k)B_ik+(h_i/d_(k-1))B_i,(k-1), 0<k<m,\n B_(i+1),m=B_im+(h_i/d_(m-1))B_i,(m-1).             (4)\n\nAlthough an unused factor 1-h_i/d_k can be negative for a dead state,\nit always multiplies zero. More precisely, if r_i distinct ruler levels\nhave occurred before query i, induction gives B_ik=0 for k<r_i.\nThe next step has level at most r_i, so h_i<=d_k on every occupied\nstate k<m. If it is the first d_(r_i), the mass at r_i moves entirely\nto r_i+1. Consequently all B_ik are nonnegative, every row sums to one,\nand after the first d_(m-1) the row is the point mass at state m.\n\nThe polynomial identity\n\n (1-hz)phi_k=(1-h/d_k)phi_k+(h/d_k)phi_(k+1)\n\nfor k<m, together with (1-hz)phi_m=phi_m modulo z phi_m, proves\n\n g_i=sum_(k=0)^m B_ik V_k.                           (5)\n\nThus B is exactly the marginal law of a time-inhomogeneous pure-birth\nchain: from occupied state k<m it stays with probability 1-h_i/d_k\nand advances one state with probability h_i/d_k; m is absorbing.\nThis probability space is auxiliary. No state vector V_k is assumed\nto be a gradient at an additional sample of the legal function.\n\nThe positions are equally explicit. Let U_k=V_k-v and\n\n Y_k=sum_(r=k)^(m-1)d_r U_r, Y_m=0.\n\nThe conditional expected change of Y at step i is -h_i U_k when the\nstate is k. Hence sum_k B_ik Y_k changes by -h_i R_i. Initially\n\n Y_0=sum_k d_k U_k=sum_j d_j w_j=Z_0,\n\nwhere the middle equality follows by telescoping\nd_k phi_k(z)=(phi_k(z)-phi_(k+1)(z))/z at z=1/d_j.\nTherefore\n\n Z_i=sum_k B_ik Y_k, x_i=u-s_i v+sum_k B_ik Y_k.      (6)\n\nIn particular the troublesome coupling remains, explicitly,\n\n <v,Z_0>=sum_(k<m)d_k<v,U_k>.                        (7)\n\nEquations (5)-(6) preserve the original vectors, not just their norms.\nSubstitution in (1) gives EXACTLY\n\n Q_ij=f_i-f_j\n   +<sum_k B_ik V_k, (s_i-s_j)v+sum_k(B_jk-B_ik)Y_k>\n   +(1/2)||sum_k(B_ik-B_jk)V_k||^2.                  (8)\n\nAll earlier/earlier, suffix, and optimum conditions remain in this one\narray. In particular (5) does not authorize independent state heights,\northogonality of state vectors, or projected legality.\n\nTwo occupation identities retain quantitative physical costs:\n\n sum_i h_i B_ik=d_k,                                (9)\n sum_i s_(i+1)h_i B_ik=d_k sum_(r<=k)d_r, 0<=k<m.    (10)\n\nTo prove (9), sum the flow equation (4). State0 has initial mass1 and\nfinal mass0, so its total outflow is1. Successive transient states have\nequal total inflow and outflow, so their total outflows are also1.\nFor (10), write M_k=sum_i s_(i+1)h_i B_ik and M_(-1)=0.\nSummation by parts gives\n\n sum_i s_(i+1)(B_(i+1),k-B_ik)=-sum_i h_i B_ik=-d_k.\n\nUsing (4), M_k/d_k-M_(k-1)/d_(k-1)=d_k, with the second term zero\nat k=0. Induction proves (10). The terminal occupation is\nsum_i h_i B_im=S-sum_(k<m)d_k; it is nonnegative and keeps the full\nconstant-gradient suffix in the budget.\n\n## 3. Total nonnegativity for the ACTUAL chronological evaluations\n\nThis is a theorem about temporal coefficient matrices, not yet the\nweighted energy matrix. It holds for arbitrary strictly increasing positive\nlevels d_0,...,d_(m-1) with the same ordered first-occurrence rule.\n\nLet P be the matrix with chronological rows i=0,...,q and columns\np_i0,...,p_i,(m-1),1, in that order. Then P is totally nonnegative:\nevery square minor, with its inherited row and column order, is >=0.\nThe same is true of B, and of the jump matrix\n\n A_ij=(h_i/d_j)p_ij, 0<=i<q, 0<=j<m.                (11)\n\nEvery column of A sums to1 by (2).\n\nExternal theorem used, with its local definitions made explicit:\nDavid Galvin and Adrian Pacurar, *Total non-negativity of some combinatorial\nmatrices*, arXiv:1807.08658v3 (2019), Theorem1.2; published JCTA172 (2020),\n105179. For a nondecreasing real sequence a_l, define C_ik by\nproduct_(t<=i)(X-e_t)=sum_k C_ik product_(l<=k)(X-a_l).\nThen C is totally nonnegative iff e is restricted growth relative to a:\nthe cap initially is a_1, an e_t below the cap leaves it unchanged,\nan equality advances it to the next a_l, and exceeding it is forbidden.\nThe proof constructs a planar path matrix; zero pivots at cap hits move\nall negative edge weights out of the network without changing its path\nmatrix. Disjoint-path determinant cancellation then proves the minor signs.\n\nThe exact PDF and extracted text were read, including Definition1.1,\nTheorem1.2, the pivot lemmas3.7/3.9, and the complete proof of1.2.\nFiles: downloads/1807.08658v3.pdf and downloads/1807.08658.txt.\nMatlas was queried repeatedly for this statement and title but did not\nreturn the paper; the primary arXiv text, not a fabricated Matlas hit,\ngrounds this citation. No verifier acceptance is claimed for this application.\n\nHere is the complete applicability check. Set\n\n a_1=-1/d_0<...<a_m=-1/d_(m-1)<a_(m+1)=0,\n\nand continue the a sequence with zeros as far as necessary. Set\ne_(i+1)=-1/h_i. A first occurrence of d_j is exactly a hit of the\ncurrent cap a_(j+1). Between first occurrences, every level is smaller\nand hence its e is strictly below the next cap. After all levels have\nappeared, every e is negative and the cap remains0. Thus the theorem's\nrestricted-growth condition is satisfied, with actual repetitions and\nthe actual deleted last d_0, not an ordered surrogate schedule.\n\nLet N_k(X)=product_(r<k)(X+1/d_r), so\nphi_k(z)=(product_(r<k)d_r)N_k(-z). The exact Newton expansion gives\n\n B_ik=(product_(t<i)h_t) C_ik/(product_(r<k)d_r),\n 0<=k<=m.                                          (12)\n\nThe coefficients with k>m vanish upon evaluation at the m+1 nodes\n-1/d_0,...,-1/d_(m-1),0. Positive row and column scaling and truncation\npreserve all minor signs, proving that B is totally nonnegative.\n\nFor completeness the node-evaluation matrix V_kj=phi_k(1/d_j), with\nthe node0 last, is also totally nonnegative. After positive row scaling,\nits transpose is L_jk=product_(r<k)(a_(j+1)-a_(r+1)). It is lower\ntriangular. In Neville elimination its pivot in row i,column k is\n\n product_(r=i-k)^(i-1)(a_(i+1)-a_(r+1))>0, i>=k.\n\nIndeed the pivot is the ratio of determinants on consecutive rows\ni-k,...,i and initial columns0,...,k, and on rows i-k,...,i-1 and\ncolumns0,...,k-1. These determinants are Vandermonde determinants:\nthe monic Newton polynomials differ from monomials by a triangular\ncolumn change with diagonal1. Cancelling gives the displayed pivot.\nAll elimination multipliers are positive. Reversing the elimination\nexpresses L as a product of elementary lower bidiagonal matrices with\nnonnegative off-diagonal entries and a positive diagonal matrix.\nEach factor is totally nonnegative, as follows directly by expanding\nits minors, and products preserve this property by Cauchy--Binet.\nThus V is totally nonnegative. Now P=BV, proving the assertion for P.\nFinally A is a row and column positive scaling of P's residual submatrix,\nso A has the asserted minor signs and stochastic column sums.\n\nThe chronological matrix has full column rank: the queries immediately\nbefore first d_j, followed by any query after first d_(m-1), give a square\nupper triangular evaluation submatrix with strictly positive diagonal.\nThus none of these representations has silently removed a modal direction.\n\n## 4. Exact cancellation of ALL shared heights\n\nDefine the elapsed-time quadratic form\n\n J=sum_i [2s_(i+1)h_i<g_(i+1),g_i>\n          +s_(i+1)||g_(i+1)-g_i||^2+h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2].\n\nThe original interpolation inequalities have the algebraic identity\n\n ||x_0||^2-2S f_q+2sum_i h_i Q_(i,star)\n       +2sum_i s_(i+1)Q_(i+1,i)=||x_q||^2+J.        (13)\n\nTo check it, GD gives\n2sum_i h_i Q_(i,star)=2sum_i h_i f_i-||x_0||^2\n +||x_q||^2+sum_i h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2.\nAlso Q_(i+1,i)=f_(i+1)-f_i+h_i<g_(i+1),g_i>\n +||g_(i+1)-g_i||^2/2. Summation by parts with s_0=0 and s_q=S\ncancels the displayed common values and proves (13). All multipliers\nare nonnegative. This is a sufficient certificate combination and does\nnot claim that its selected constraints are sufficient for legal data.\n\n## 5. A Brownian-bridge covariance with the full diagonal cost\n\nPut D_i=g_i-g_(i+1)=sum_j A_ij w_j, where A is (11). Define increasing\nclock knots and nonnegative penalties\n\n beta_i=s_(i+1)(s_i+1), 0<=i<q,\n beta_q=S(S+1), beta_(-1)=0,\n c_i=(h_i-1)s_(i+1).\n\nAll h_i>1, so c_i>=0, and beta_0<...<beta_q. Expanding the squared\ndifferences in J gives\n\n J=sum_(i=0)^q(beta_i-beta_(i-1))||g_i||^2\n                          -sum_(i<q)c_i||D_i||^2. (14)\n\nFor example the coefficient of ||g_i||^2 at an interior node is\ns_i(h_(i-1)+h_i)+h_i; that of ||g_q||^2 is S h_(q-1).\nThese are exactly the differences of beta in (14).\n\nSince g_i=v+sum_(r>=i)D_r, changing the order of finite sums yields\n\n J=beta_q||v||^2+2<v,sum_i beta_i D_i>\n        +sum_(i,l<q)beta_min(i,l)<D_i,D_l>\n        -sum_i c_i||D_i||^2.\n\nDefine\n\n Kbr_(i,l)=beta_min(i,l)-beta_i beta_l/beta_q.\n\nCompleting the ONE constant-mode square proves the exact joint identity\n\n J=beta_q||v+(sum_i beta_i D_i)/beta_q||^2\n       +sum_(i,l<q)Kbr_(i,l)<D_i,D_l>\n       -sum_i c_i||D_i||^2.                         (15)\n\nThe kernel Kbr is a real covariance kernel. For a direct elementary\nconstruction, take independent centered random variables xi_r of variances\nbeta_r-beta_(r-1), 0<=r<=q, let Z_i=sum_(r<=i)xi_r, and set\nZbr_i=Z_i-(beta_i/beta_q)Z_q. Direct expansion gives\nE[Zbr_i Zbr_l]=Kbr_(i,l). Consequently Kbr is PSD. This use of\nprobability creates no randomness in the legal instance or its shared Gram.\n\nThe remaining sufficient claim is the EXPLICIT known-matrix domination\n\n A^T Kbr A >= A^T diag(c_i) A                        (16)\n\nin the real PSD order, for the actual nearcritical ruler words. It is\nequivalent to J>=0 on this temporal quotient, because the constant square\nin (15) can be minimized by a freely chosen formal vector v. Every cost\nis present, including all repeated small steps, the large-step penalties,\nand the final constant-gradient suffix through beta_q.\n\nThus (16), IF PROVED, combines with (13) and ||x_0||=1 to give\n2f_q<=1/S, and the ordinary smooth descent inequality then gives\n2F(x_q-bv)<=1/S for every b in [0,2]. The previous draft's exact-B\ncomparison would have to be included in a complete eventual submission;\nits conditional target constant is lambda/exp(1)<1. Neither (16) nor\nthat resulting whole-interface bound is asserted established here.\n\nEquation (16) is a fixed, concrete covariance inequality on stochastic\nNewton columns. It has no unknown interpolation multipliers or heights.\nIt remains a hard restricted weighted Hardy inequality, not a routine\ncheck and not an equivalent restatement of the original all-h minimax goal.\n\n## 6. Why total nonnegativity does NOT settle the energy sign\n\nHere is an exact counterexample to that GENERIC implication. It is not a\ncounterexample to (16) for the actual m-dependent parameters.\n\nTake levels t=3/2 and u_step=4 and the ruler word (t,u_step,t). On its first two\nsteps, choose the single residual column at rate1/u_step, namely\n\n g_0=1, g_1=5/8, g_2=0, v=0.\n\nThe post-annihilation third step contributes zero. The ordered Newton\nmatrix and birth matrix are totally nonnegative by the same construction\nas above. Nevertheless the first step contributes\n\n 2t^2(5/8)+t(3/8)^2+t(1-t)=291/128,\n\nand the second contributes\n\n (t+u_step)(5/8)^2+u_step(1-u_step)(5/8)^2=-325/128.\n\nTherefore J=-17/64<0. The hypotheses used to prove the temporal minor\nsigns all hold, but weighted-energy positivity fails. These scalar data\nare a formal certificate test, not an asserted legal interpolation array.\n\nAccordingly, a planar network for A cannot by itself be called a proof of\n(16). A successful path argument must ALSO absorb the explicit costs c_i\nusing the actual relation between ruler levels and chronological mass.\n\n### 6.1 Stronger test: keep the ACTUAL levels, step multiset and total mass\n\nThe insufficiency persists even when those scalar data are preserved.\nLet t=d_0, u_step=d_1, d=d_2 be the ACTUAL parameters of Section1. The queried\nword starts (t,u_step,t,d,...). Move that one intervening t just after d,\nobtaining the permuted queried word\n\n                 (t,u_step,d,t,...).\n\nEvery subsequent step is in its original order. The multiset, q, S, all\nd_j, ordered first occurrences, and the final deleted d_0 are unchanged.\nIn particular the total-nonnegativity argument and occupation identities\n(9)-(10) apply to this permuted word, too. It is deliberately NOT the\noriginal chronological word in (16).\n\nUse scalar modal vectors with v=0, w_j=0 for j>=3, and\n\n w_2=-1/[(1-t/d)(1-u_step/d)],\n w_1=[1-(1-t/d)w_2]/(1-t/u_step), w_0=-w_1-w_2.\n\nAll denominators are positive because t<u_step<d. On the permuted word the\ngradients are exactly (0,1,-1,0,0,...). The common modal Gram is real PSD\nof rank1. Direct substitution in J gives\n\n J=6t+6u_step-3u_step^2-2t u_step+2d-d^2.             (17)\n\nHere the first step contributes t, the second contributes\n4(t+u_step)-2u_step(t+u_step)+u_step(1-u_step), and the third contributes\nt+u_step+2d-d^2; their sum is (17).\n\nFor a uniform exact sign, write epsilon=1/m and tau=sqrt(2). Then\n\n t=tau-2epsilon/[lambda(1+epsilon)]<=tau,\n u_step=2-epsilon, d=tau+2-lambda epsilon^2.\n\nThe expression (17) is increasing in t, since its t coefficient is\n6-2u_step>0. Substituting tau for t, and comparing d with tau+2, gives\n\n J<=-2+(6+2tau)epsilon-3epsilon^2\n                    +2lambda^2 epsilon^2-lambda^2 epsilon^4\n   <-2+9epsilon+12epsilon^2<-19/10, m>=128.           (18)\n\nThe last strict inequality follows from\n9/128+12/128^2=1164/16384<1/10. Thus actual levels, the original total\nmass, stochastic column sums, total nonnegativity, and the exact first\noccupation moments do NOT by themselves force the energy sign.\n\nThe example is explicitly illegal as interpolation data: g_0=0 and the\nfirst GD step gives x_1=x_0, while g_1=1. Thus\nQ_01+Q_10=||g_0-g_1||^2=1>0 for every choice of shared values. One may\nset x_0=1 by choosing the free position offset u; that does not repair\nthe violation. No claim about W_n follows from this formal example.\n\nAs an algebraic illustration of the missing repeated-step storage, at\nthe limiting parameters t=tau, u_step=2, d=tau+2 the SAME modal vectors on\nthe original chronology have gradients\n\n                 (0,1,-1,-(2-tau),0,...).\n\nTheir J is 2+4tau>0, whereas the permuted chronology gives J=-2.\nIndeed the four original steps contribute respectively tau, -2,\n4+3tau, and0. This comparison is only a fixed exact limiting identity;\nit is not used as a proof at finite m or for arbitrary modal vectors.\n\n## 7. Current proof state\n\nComplete draft structure: lossless finite birth chain; invariant occupation\nbudgets (9)-(10); audited total nonnegativity of the actual temporal and\njump matrices; common-height cancellation (13); and the exact conditioned\ncovariance formula (15), with all cross terms and all penalties retained.\n\nStill open: the uniform quantitative domination (16), or an actual legal\npositive-height object escaping its intended bound. Two direct attempts\ndid not prove it: local scalar square completion discards required past\ncovariance; comparison to a continuous positive Laplace kernel has no\nproved uniform error bound on signed modal combinations. The new ordered\nkernel result does not fill this quantitative gap, as Section6 demonstrates.\n\nNo whole-interface charge or benchmark-scale legal construction has been\ncompleted. The original problem remains unresolved, and no new fact entered\nthe graph. The fourth launch's second round does not authorize another round,\nnumerical budget, or verifier submission.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"target_claim": "Temporal total nonnegativity plus actual levels, mass, and occupation moments alone implies weighted-energy PSD.", "status": "refuted", "assumptions_satisfied": ["Actual m-dependent d_j", "Same queried step multiset and S", "Ordered first occurrences", "Totally nonnegative chronological evaluations and stochastic jump columns", "One real rank-one modal Gram", "All exact birth-chain occupation identities"], "failed_conclusion": "J>=0 on every formal scalar modal output", "not_claimed": ["Original chronological covariance inequality refuted", "Existence of legal high-payoff trajectory", "Any asymptotic conclusion about W"], "finding_ids": ["83a03447bbd73458"], "fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290"], "artifact": "research/newton_birth_chain_joint_storage.md Section6.1", "branch_id": "max2_newton_birth_covariance"}, "glossary": {"t": "Actual d_0 in Section6.1.", "u_step": "Actual d_1 in Section6.1; distinct from the free position offset u.", "d": "Actual d_2 in Section6.1.", "J": "Elapsed-time storage quadratic defined in Section4.", "epsilon": "1/m in the uniform exact sign calculation.", "B_ik": "Exact auxiliary birth-chain marginal; equation4.", "A_ij": "Stochastic Newton jump coefficient h_i p_ij/d_j.", "Kbr": "Clock-conditioned covariance beta_min(i,l)-beta_i beta_l/beta_q."}}
{"id": "06fc37ec32b366f7", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T23:16:13.909284+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "max2 ACTUAL all-Q counterexample to higher-curvature sign transfer: on every original nearcritical H_m (m>=128), a one-dimensional explicit convex1-smooth function with UNIQUE minimizer0, true initial distance1, one shared value array, positive queried heights, and only resonant modes d4,d5 has <L2,g-L1><0 at query2 before the ACTUAL adjacent d0,d2 pair, although ordinary adjacent cocoercivity <L1,g-L1>>=0 holds. The exact swap formula is ΔJ=2αβ(β-α)(t+α+β)<L2,g-L1>. Thus even full legality cannot make each swap a positive local charge. This is a weak-payoff scope counterexample, not a refutation of whole T PSD or the original goal.", "evidence": "# Actual legal negative mixed curvature in the full double-zero quotient\n\nStatus: complete UNVERIFIED candidate construction and adjacent-swap identity.\nNo fact submission is allocated, and this is not a fact-graph entry.\nThe only accepted mathematical input is ordinary smooth interpolation in\nfact1252b8823bb5a290. Everything else needed here is derived below.\nThis refutes a local sign inference; it neither closes the whole quotient\nclass nor resolves the original exact-profile problem. No optimization,\nnumerical experiment, or installation was used.\n\n## 1. The actual interface and notation\n\nFor an integer m>=128, put\n\n lambda=1+sqrt(2), c=lambda(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c,\n d_j=1+a c^j, H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,d_j,H_j).\n\nSet n=2^m-1 and q=n-1. The queried steps h_0,...,h_(q-1) are the word\nH_m with its final d_0 deleted. The actual final step is b in [0,2]. Define\n\n s_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, S=s_q,\n p_ij=product_(t<i)(1-h_t/d_j),\n R_i=sum_(j<m)p_ij w_j, Z_i=sum_(j<m)d_j p_ij w_j,\n x_i=u-s_i v+Z_i, g_i=v+R_i, 0<=i<=q.\n\nAll these vectors use ONE arbitrary real PSD Gram matrix. The values f_i\nform ONE array; the optimum jet is x_star=g_star=f_star=0; ||x_0||=1 is\nthe distance normalization relative to the chosen actual minimizer. Every\noriginal queried inequality is retained:\n\n Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0.       (1)\n\nThe first d_j occurs at step 2^j-1. Thus p_ij is positive before query\n2^j and zero thereafter, and R_q=Z_q=0. The recursion gives\n\n Z_i-Z_(i+1)=h_i R_i, sum_i h_i p_ij=d_j.             (2)\n\nHere and below unqualified time sums range over 0<=i<q.\n\nFor clarity about the original value budget, define\n\n C_min=f_q-<v,x_q>, eta=-C_min-||v||^2/2,\n delta_i=f_i-<v,x_i>-C_min-||R_i||^2/2.\n\nOn an actual legal function, tilting by -<v,x> makes x_q a minimizer;\nthe smooth optimum inequalities give delta_i>=0. Also eta>=0 follows\nfrom Q_(q,star). Expanding Q_(0,star) gives the exact common budget\n\n eta>=delta_0+||R_0||^2+<v,R_0>-<R_0,x_0>.          (3)\n\nNo height is set to zero and neither eta nor the norm of x_0 is discarded.\n\n\nDefine the elapsed-time quadratic\n\n J=sum_(i<q)[2s_(i+1)h_i<g_(i+1),g_i>\n       +s_(i+1)||g_(i+1)-g_i||^2+h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2].\n\nIt is the gradient form in the exact ordinary-Q identity\n\n ||x_0||^2-2S f_q+2sum_i h_i Q_(i,star)\n       +2sum_i s_(i+1)Q_(i+1,i)=||x_q||^2+J.\n\nThis identity follows by expanding the GD norm difference and summing\ns_(i+1)(f_(i+1)-f_i) by parts. No positivity of J is assumed here.\n\n## 2. Exact adjacent-swap storage and its sign obstruction\n\nThere is a useful direct calculation identifying the chronology term that\nan adjacent-step interchange spends. Consider two adjacent physical steps\nalpha,beta>0 beginning at elapsed time t_clock. Let the current modal\ncoefficients be p_j, and define actual vectors\n\n g=v+sum_j p_j w_j,\n L_1=sum_j (p_j/d_j)w_j, L_2=sum_j(p_j/d_j^2)w_j.\n\nThese are DEFINITIONS through the modal coordinates. No linear operator on\nthe physical Euclidean space is presumed: a singular common Gram can identify\ntwo modal vectors with different rates, so such an operator need not exist.\n\nLet J_ab and J_ba be the energy J defined below for the two orders (alpha,beta) and\n(beta,alpha), with the same formal modal data entering the pair and the\nsame earlier and later steps. Their endpoints, modal products, and elapsed\ntime after the pair agree. All other terms of J therefore cancel, and\n\n J_ab-J_ba=2alpha beta(beta-alpha)(t_clock+alpha+beta)\n                                <L_2,g-L_1>.        (19)\n\nHere is a polynomial verification. For rates z,w set U=z+w, V=zw and\nD=z^2+w^2-zw(z+w). For a word W define P_W(z)=product_(h in W)(1-hz).\nThe unweighted and zero-clock weighted kernels of a single step h are\n\n K_[h](z,w)=h-(h^2/2)(U-V),\n T_[h](z,w)=h(h+1)-h^3(U-V).\n\nSeparating two consecutive blocks directly in the defining energy sums gives\n\n K_AB=K_A+P_A(z)P_A(w)K_B,\n T_AB=T_A+P_A(z)P_A(w)[T_B+2S_A K_B].\n\nSubstitution of P_[h](z)P_[h](w)=1-hU+h^2V yields\n\n K_ab-K_ba=-(alpha beta(alpha-beta)/2)D,\n T_ab-T_ba=-alpha beta(alpha-beta)(alpha+beta)D.\n\nA nonzero starting clock adds 2t_clock(K_ab-K_ba). Finally D evaluated\non the entering common Gram is 2<L_2,g-L_1>, proving (19). This proof\nuses only finite polynomial identities and applies to the original real\njoint Gram, including its singular cases.\n\nThe sign in (19) is NOT fixed by the actual positive reciprocal rates.\nTake a single live rate mu=1/d_j in (0,1), absorb its positive entering\nfactor into a nonzero vector W, and set all other residuals to zero.\nThen L_1=mu W, L_2=mu^2 W, g=v+W, and\n\n <L_2,g-L_1>=mu^2[<W,v>+(1-mu)||W||^2].\n\nIt is positive when v=0 and negative when v=-(2-mu)W. Both assignments\nhave real PSD rank-one Grams and can be inserted before an actual adjacent\npair d_0,d_2, where the d_2 mode is still live. Thus neither swap order\nuniversally dominates the other as an unconditional quadratic certificate\non this quotient. This does not assert legality of either assignment.\nIndeed the negative one fails the first adjacent cocoercivity condition:\n\n <L_1,g-L_1>=-mu||W||^2<0.\n\nIn the ONE-live-rate case the two mixed terms differ by the positive factor\nmu, so an actual adjacent Q cycle fixes the sign. With several live rates,\nL_2 is not a scalar multiple of L_1. The next construction shows that even\nall the original interpolation constraints do not make the sign positive.\n\n## 3. Actual legal negative mixed curvature, on the original full word\n\nThis is a scoped counterexample to <L_2,g-L_1>>=0, not an adversary\nmatching the project's exact benchmark. It lies within a weak positive-scalar-\ngradient class already considered in the project. Its purpose is to test the\nnew local swap step with FULL legality rather than only a formal PSD Gram.\n\nUse the original word and m>=128. Choose the two actual levels\n\n d_f=d_4, d_s=d_5, r=d_s/d_f,\n v=1/[4(S+1)], epsilon_amp=v/(4r),\n w_4=-epsilon_amp, w_5=r epsilon_amp, w_j=0 otherwise.\n\nAll vectors are real scalars. Set x_0=1 and choose the free offset\nu=1-Z_0, where Z_0=d_f w_4+d_s w_5. Define x_i,g_i by Section1.\nWrite p_f=p_i4 and p_s=p_i5 when a query i is understood. Then\n\n g_i=v+epsilon_amp(-p_f+r p_s),\n L_(1,i)=epsilon_amp(p_s-p_f)/d_f,\n L_(2,i)=epsilon_amp(-p_f+p_s/r)/d_f^2.              (20)\n\nBefore the first d_f both products are positive and every factor of p_s\nis at least its corresponding factor in p_f. Afterwards p_f=0, and p_s\nremains nonnegative until it also vanishes. Therefore 0<=p_f<=p_s<=1\nfor all queries. It follows that\n\n v<=g_i<=5v/4,\n 0<=g_i-g_(i+1)=h_i L_(1,i)<=h_i epsilon_amp/d_f.    (21)\n\nEvery x_i strictly decreases, and\n\n x_q=1-sum_i h_i g_i>11/16.                         (22)\n\nTo build ONE actual function, define a continuous scalar derivative theta:\n\n theta(x)=x for x<=v;\n theta(x)=v for v<=x<=x_q;\n on [x_(i+1),x_i], theta is affine with endpoint values g_(i+1),g_i;\n theta(x)=g_0 for x>=1.\n\nOverlaps at endpoints agree. Each interpolation interval has slope\n\n (g_i-g_(i+1))/(x_i-x_(i+1))=L_(1,i)/g_i\n                      <=epsilon_amp/(d_f v)=1/(4r d_f)<1.\n\nHence theta is nondecreasing and globally1-Lipschitz. The function\n\n                         F(x)=integral_0^x theta(t) dt\n\nis everywhere finite, differentiable, convex and1-smooth, with F'(x_i)=g_i.\nIt is strictly decreasing on the negative half-line and strictly increasing\non the positive half-line. Thus its UNIQUE minimizer is0 and the true initial\ndistance is1. The GD recursion is the exact original chronological word.\n\nThe single common array of values can be written without integration:\n\n f_q=v x_q-v^2/2,\n f_i=f_(i+1)+(g_i+g_(i+1))(x_i-x_(i+1))/2.\n\nThese are exactly the values of F. Accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290 supplies\nnecessity of EVERY ordered Q inequality for this one convex1-smooth function,\nincluding all earlier/earlier pairs, every suffix comparison, and the optimum.\nNo Q inequality is dropped or checked only at neighboring points.\n\nThe heights are not all zero. Indeed C_min=-v^2/2 and eta=0 here by direct\ncalculation, while\n\n delta_i=integral_(x_q)^(x_i)(theta(x)-v)dx-(g_i-v)^2/2.\n\nOn [x_q,1] the slope of theta is at most kappa=1/(4r d_f)<1. If\nR_i=g_i-v>0, its nonnegative increasing residual derivative must have area\nat least R_i^2/(2kappa): on the interval of length R_i/kappa ending at x_i\nit is at least max{0,R_i-kappa(x_i-x)}. This interval is inside [x_q,x_i]\nbecause R_i<=kappa(x_i-x_q). Therefore\n\n delta_i>=(1/kappa-1)R_i^2/2>0 whenever R_i>0.        (23)\n\nIn particular delta_0>0. The zero value of eta is an explicit property of\nTHIS witness, not a normalization or reduction imposed on the full interface.\n\nNow consider the ACTUAL query i=2, immediately before the consecutive\nsteps alpha=d_0 and beta=d_2. Set epsilon=1/m and tau=sqrt(2). Since\n\n d_f=1+lambda^3(1+2epsilon-2epsilon^3-epsilon^4)>15,\n 12/5<c<5/2, r=c-(c-1)/d_f>23/10>2,\n d_0+d_1<7/2,\n\nthe two preceding factors give\n\n p_f=(1-d_0/d_f)(1-d_1/d_f)>1-7/(2d_f)>23/30>3/4,\n p_s/r<1/2.\n\nHere lambda^3=7+5sqrt(2)>14 and epsilon<=1/128 justify the first bound;\nthe elementary bounds on c follow from its displayed definition. Thus\n\n L_(2,2)<-epsilon_amp/(4d_f^2),\n g_2-L_(1,2)>=v-epsilon_amp/d_f>3v/4,\n\nand consequently\n\n                  <L_(2,2),g_2-L_(1,2)>\n                         <-3epsilon_amp v/(16d_f^2)<0.       (24)\n\nAt the same point the adjacent cocoercivity quantity is nonnegative:\nL_(1,2)(g_2-L_(1,2))>=0, as required. Thus replacing L_1 by the\nsecond modal curvature L_2 in that inference is false even on a fully legal\npositive-height example at the original physical parameters.\n\nFor completeness, the unqueried final point stays in the same constant-\ngradient interval for every b in [0,2]. Since S>1 gives v<1/8, (22) yields\nx_q-bv>7/16>v. Its exact payoff is therefore\n\n               2F(x_q-bv)=2v(x_q-bv)-v^2.\n\nIt lies between 3/[16(S+1)] and 1/[2(S+1)]. No comparison of this\nweak constant with1/B_n is asserted as a matching lower bound. The example\ndoes not refute the full elapsed-time storage candidate: a negative individual\nswap term can be paid by other storage terms in the original chronology.\n\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"branch_id": "max2_newton_birth_covariance", "target_claim": "All original ordinary Q inequalities force <L2,g-L1>>=0 on the actual positive-rate quotient.", "status": "refuted_by_complete_unverified_candidate", "fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290"], "finding_ids": ["83a03447bbd73458", "501aba74c8acfcdf"], "artifact": "research/actual_joint_curvature_counterexample.md", "full_package": "research/newton_birth_chain_joint_storage.md Sections7-7.1", "scope": ["Original chronological H_m", "Every m>=128", "One common real rank-one Gram and one shared value array", "Every original interpolation pair via one actual global function", "Positive delta_0; eta=0 is derived for this example only", "All final b in[0,2] covered"], "not_claimed": ["Matching exact-B payoff", "Counterexample to original-word kernel PSD", "General height reduction"]}, "glossary": {"L_1": "First modal curvature sum_j p_ij w_j/d_j; defined through modes, not an assumed physical linear operator.", "L_2": "Second modal curvature sum_j p_ij w_j/d_j².", "d_f": "Actual physical level d_4.", "d_s": "Actual physical level d_5.", "r": "Actual ratio d_s/d_f.", "epsilon_amp": "v/(4r), the positive residual amplitude.", "theta": "Explicit continuous nondecreasing1-Lipschitz derivative used to construct the actual scalar function F.", "t_clock": "Elapsed physical time before the adjacent swap.", "J_ab": "Elapsed-time energy with steps alpha,beta in that order.", "J_ba": "Energy for the reverse pair, with the same formal entering modes and same other steps.", "kappa": "The bound1/(4r d_f) on the slope of theta on[x_q,1]."}}
{"id": "cfa7997c83537273", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T23:30:25.238724+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "C66 independent UNVERIFIED actual-word counterexample prevents overextending C65: quartic epigraph projection has increasing vertical and normal-coefficient states but DECREASING effective horizontal turning state. On every actual H_m,m>=128, exact start r0=1/2,v0=4 has gradient signs +,+,-,+: first turn level1, then LOWER level0. This is a new legal mechanism boundary, not a full-horizon profile witness.", "evidence": "# C66: quartic epigraphs can reactivate lower turning levels\n\nStatus: COMPLETE UNVERIFIED construction and counterexample. This is an\nindependent follow-up after delivering C65, not a premise of that draft and\nnot a proposed solution of the original quantitative profile. No numerical\ntrajectory, optimization, installation, or verifier call was performed.\n\n## 1. The exact claim tested\n\nThe parabolic construction has a nondecreasing effective horizontal normal\nstate, which prevents lower turning thresholds from becoming active again.\nOne must not generalize that property from a quadratic epigraph to every\nconvex epigraph merely because its vertical or projection-normal state grows.\n\nFor every m>=128, keep the actual enclosing-horizon parameters\n\n    lambda=1+sqrt(2), c=lambda(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j).\n\nThe single quartic-epigraph function below, with the fixed initial state\n(r_0,v_0)=(1/2,4), has actual horizontal gradient signs\n\n    r_0>0, r_1>0, r_2<0, r_3>0\n\nunder the FIRST THREE steps of that same H_m. Its effective normal state\nstarts above d_1=ac, falls below d_0=a after the first step, and stays below\na at the next sample. Thus the first turn is at level1, and the next turn\nis at the LOWER level0. All interpolation pairs come from one global\nconvex differentiable1-smooth function, with the true closest distance.\n\nThis refutes extending the parabolic normal monotonicity and permanent\ndeactivation of lower turning levels to general convex epigraphs. It does\nnot refute C65's stated parabolic result, and it supplies no lower bound\nat the full terminal horizon2^m-1.\n\n## 2. One global legal function and its exact state\n\nLet phi_4(t)=t^4/4, and define\n\n    K_4={(t,s):s>=phi_4(t)},\n    F_4(X)=max_(g in K_4)(<X,g>-||g||^2/2).\n\nThe unique maximizer is the Euclidean projection onto the nonempty closed\nconvex set K_4, by completing the square. Projection existence follows\nfrom coercivity and closedness; strict convexity gives uniqueness. The\ntwo projection normal inequalities imply firm nonexpansiveness, hence a\n1-Lipschitz projection map. Comparing the defining maxima bounds the\nfirst-order remainder by ||Y-X||^2, so F_4 is differentiable with gradient\nequal to that projection. It is globally convex as a supremum of affine\nfunctions. These arguments establish global smoothness directly.\n\nFor r real and v>=0 define\n\n    eta=v+r^4/4,\n    u_4=eta*r^2=v*r^2+r^6/4,\n    G_4(r)=(r,r^4/4),\n    X_4(r,v)=(r+v*r^3+r^7/4,-v)=(r(1+u_4),-v).\n\nThe symbols eta and u_4 are DIFFERENT states: eta is the projection-normal\ncoefficient, whereas u_4 determines horizontal overshooting. For any\n(t,s) in K_4,\n\n    <X_4-G_4,(t,s)-G_4>\n      <=eta[r^3(t-r)-(t^4-r^4)/4]\n      =-(eta/4)(t-r)^2[(t+r)^2+2r^2]<=0.\n\nThus G_4(r) is the actual gradient at X_4(r,v). Substitution gives\n\n    F_4(X_4(r,v))=r^2/2+3v*r^4/4+7r^8/32.                (1)\n\nFor every ordered pair of such states,\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<G_i,X_j-X_i>+||G_i-G_j||^2/2\n        =<X_j-G_j,G_i-G_j>\n        =-(eta_j/4)(r_i-r_j)^2[(r_i+r_j)^2+2r_j^2]<=0.   (2)\n\nHence all pairs are legal, for any number of actual steps. There is no\nadjacent-pair relaxation.\n\nThe minimum value is0 and the minimizer set is exactly {(0,y):y<=0}.\nSuch points give a nonpositive inner product with every feasible gradient.\nIf the horizontal coordinate is nonzero, testing (t,t^4/4) for sufficiently\nsmall t of the same sign gives positive value, since the linear term\ndominates the quadratic and higher terms. If the vertical coordinate is\npositive, testing a small vertical gradient gives positive value.\nThus the true closest minimizer to X_4(r_0,v_0) is(0,-v_0), and\n\n    R_4^2=r_0^2(1+v_0*r_0^2+r_0^6/4)^2.                  (3)\n\nFor a step h>=0, the exact same-function GD recurrence is\n\n    v_plus=v+h*r^4/4,\n    r_plus+v_plus*r_plus^3+r_plus^7/4\n      =r(1+v*r^2+r^6/4-h).                               (4)\n\nFor v_plus>=0, the polynomial on the left is strictly increasing and\nsurjective, because its derivative is1+3v_plus*r_plus^2+7r_plus^6/4>=1.\nConsequently(4) specifies a unique real next state and exactly reproduces\nX_plus=X-hG. The full normalized terminal payoff, if later studied, is\n\n    [r_n^2+(3/2)v_n*r_n^4+(7/16)r_n^8]/R_4^2.             (5)\n\nThe projection-normal coefficient DOES increase at the physical steps:\n\n    eta_plus-eta=(h-1)r^4/4+r_plus^4/4>=0  for h>=1.      (6)\n\nThe effective state is eta*r^2, however, so(6) does not imply that u_4\nincreases. A nonzero horizontal turn occurs exactly when u_4<h-1.\n\n## 3. An exact actual-word counterexample, without solving any roots numerically\n\nFor m>=128 the elementary bounds\n\n    2/5<a<5/12, 127/128<=b=ac<1\n\nhold. For example sqrt(2)<99/70 gives c<73/30, hence\na>(127/128)(30/73)>2/5; also a<sqrt(2)-1<5/12.\nThe first three actual steps of H_m are (1+a,1+b,1+a).\n\nTake r_0=1/2,v_0=4. Then\n\n    u_(4,0)=257/256>1>b,\n    X_0=(513/512,-4), R_4=513/512.\n\nHere u_(4,i)=v_i*r_i^2+r_i^6/4 is the effective quartic state at sample i.\nThe first step has\n\n    v_1=4+(1+a)/64<4+3/128,\n    x_1=(1/2)(257/256-a),\n    0<x_1<773/2560<5/16.\n\nWrite T_v(t)=t+v*t^3+t^7/4. It is increasing, T_(v_1)(0)=0, and\nT_(v_1)(1/4)>1/4+4/64=5/16. The unique-root definition(4) therefore gives\n\n    0<r_1<1/4,\n    u_(4,1)<(4+3/128)/16+1/16384\n             =4121/16384<2/5<a.                          (7)\n\nThus the effective normal state has strictly DECREASED from above the\nlevel1 threshold to below the level0 threshold, although v and eta increased.\n\nAt the second step, with excess b, one has\n\n    x_2=r_1(u_(4,1)-b)<0,\n    |x_2|<b*r_1<1/4,\n    v_2=v_1+(1+b)r_1^4/4<4+3/128+1/512=4+13/512.\n\nSince T_(v_2) is odd and T_(v_2)(t)>=t for t>=0, its unique root satisfies\n-1/4<r_2<0. Consequently\n\n    u_(4,2)<(4+13/512)/16+1/16384\n             =4123/16384<2/5<a.                          (8)\n\nThe third step has excess a and therefore x_3=r_2(u_(4,2)-a)>0. Its unique\nroot r_3 is positive. This establishes the displayed sign pattern and\nthe return from a level1 turn to a level0 turn using exact inequalities.\n\nThe higher-dimensional position and vertical state at every step are\ninherited from(4). Nothing here replaces the actual H_m by H_2 with a\ndifferent physical parameter, or resets v after a turn.\n\n## 4. Literature grounding and the remaining quantitative interface\n\nThe power-epigraph projection formula is consistent with Propositions3.2\nand3.3 of Chierchia, Pustelnik, Pesquet and Pesquet-Popescu,\n\"Epigraphical splitting for solving constrained convex formulations of\ninverse problems with proximal tools\", arXiv:1210.5844v3. The downloaded\n32-page PDF and its extracted text are in\ndownloads/c66_epigraph_projection_1210.5844.{pdf,txt}; the definitions and\nboth proofs in Section3.2 were read.\n\nTheir Hilbert product norm is Euclidean here; their epigraph generator is\nproper, closed, convex and has domain all of R. Setting their power to4,\nscale to1/4 and vertical query coordinate to-v yields the scalar equation\nt+v*t^3+t^7/4=x. This is only projection background; their algorithmic\nconvergence statements do not supply a rate for the present prescribed GD\nword. Sections2--3 derive all mathematical claims here independently.\n\nThe explicit change from quadratic to quartic epigraph keeps one legal\nglobal function but separates the increasing normal coefficient eta from\nthe turning state eta*r^2. That extra curvature factor is the missing state\nin an attempted extension of the parabolic argument.\n\nThe next quantitative task would be to control an entire same-v quartic\ntrajectory, possibly with horizon-dependent r_0,v_0 or an epigraph power\ngreater than2, and compare(5) to the exact B_n. The three-step return above\nis not a useful full-horizon witness by itself. It remains possible that\nthis new proper family is also uniformly weak. No such assertion, and no\noriginal-target success, is made.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"finding_ids": ["8bb6bb90c39813ef"], "artifact": "research/c66_quartic_effective_threshold_reentry.md", "status": "UNVERIFIED; independent self-contained construction; no parabolic finding used as correctness premise", "literature": {"arxiv": "1210.5844v3", "theorem_ids": ["Proposition3.2", "Proposition3.3"], "role": "Read proof background; independently derived here."}}, "glossary": {"K_4": "Quartic epigraph{(t,s):s>=t^4/4}.", "F_4": "One global convex1-smooth function max_(g in K_4)(<X,g>-||g||²/2).", "X_4": "State(r+v*r³+r⁷/4,-v), with r real and v>=0.", "G_4": "Actual gradient(r,r⁴/4) at X_4.", "u_4": "Effective horizontal turning state(v+r⁴/4)r², which need not be monotone.", "eta_4": "Projection-normal coefficient v+r⁴/4; denotedeta locally, nondecreasing for physical h>=1.", "R_4": "True distance of the quartic initial state to the minimizer ray;513/512 for the exact example.", "T_v": "Strictly increasing odd polynomial t+v*t³+t⁷/4."}}
{"id": "4260d72c8bb3b963", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T23:33:12.051454+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "Exact planar four-vertex projection counterexample for the evaluated five-step dense path: h(t)=(sqrt2-p t,2+q t,sqrt2-p t,beta-m t/4,3/2),0<t<=1/50, admits one explicit global convex1-smooth unique-minimizer function with B5*2f(x5)>=1+7t/10. Every original normal inequality and the true initial distance are proved. Unverified joint draft; finite path only.", "evidence": "# An explicit planar adversary for the five-step dense variation\n\nStatus: **joint unverified candidate**, cumulative round 16. No optimizer or\nverifier is used in this proof. The full-model experiment suggested looking\nat two consecutive equal gradients and a later two-gradient plateau. None\nof its floating entries is used as a mathematical premise.\n\nThe accepted benchmark/interpolation conventions are b98bb807d514e80d and\n1252b8823bb5a290. All additional construction and inequalities are proved\nbelow. This is a finite path obstruction, not an unbounded-horizon result.\n\n## 1. Concrete steps and statement\n\nPut s=sqrt(2), A=3+2s, beta=sqrt(7+4s), B0=(beta+1)^2, and\n\n p=(16-11s)/3, q=(8s-10)/3, m=10s-14=q-2p.\n\nFor0<=t<=1/50 define\n\n a=s-p*t, b=2+q*t, c=beta-m*t/4,\n h(t)=(a,b,a,c,3/2).                                (1)\n\nThis is the exact balanced weighted-mean proposal evaluated as\n`n5_balanced_t002`. Indeed the primitive child's gaps are (s-1,1,s-1),\ntheir arithmetic mean is (2s-1)/3, their harmonic mean is9-6s, and their\nmidpoint is(13-8s)/3. The child direction is(-p,q,-p), of total mass m;\nthe singleton right child has zero direction, and the root changes by-m/4.\n\nFor every t in this interval there is an explicit function F_t:R^2->R,\nconvex and differentiable with1-Lipschitz gradient and UNIQUE minimizer0,\nwhose GD trajectory for h(t), started at x0=(1,0), satisfies\n\n 2F_t(x5) = (c-1)^2 J/[4(a+1)^2],                  (2)\n J=[(b-1)^2(2-a)+a(a^2-2)/2]/(a+2),\n B0*2F_t(x5)>=1+7t/10.                             (3)\n\nThe exact benchmark obeys B5>=B0. Consequently\n\n B5*W5(h(t))>=1+7t/10>1       for0<t<=1/50.        (4)\n\nNo full certificate, regardless of support, optimum incidence or PSD rank,\ncan improve the exact five-step benchmark on this path. This conclusion\ncomes from one legal original-model function, not a restricted dual array.\n\n## 2. Four concrete gradient vectors\n\nWrite z=b-1, d=c-1, and define\n\n kappa= -[z^2+(a+1)/2]/[z(a+2)],\n u=(1/(a+1),0),\n v=(kappa,sqrt(1/2-kappa^2))/(a+1),\n w=-z*u-a*v,             k=-d*w/4.                 (5)\n\nLet U=||u||^2=1/(a+1)^2. Then\n\n <u,v>=kappa*U, ||v||^2=U/2,\n ||w||^2=J*U,\n <u,w>/U=[a(a+1)/2-2z^2]/[z(a+2)]=:r_u,\n <v,w>/U=[z^2+(1-a-a^2)/2]/(a+2)=:r_v.            (6)\n\nExpanding (5) proves (6). The defining choice of kappa gives the exact\northogonality identity\n\n <w-v,-z*u-v>=0.                                   (7)\n\nAll radicals in (5) are real. Here are rational bounds proving that claim\nand all the normal inequalities used below. Squaring the rational endpoints\ngives1.4142<s<1.4143 and3.557<beta<3.558. Hence\n\n .147<p<.149, .437<q<.439, .140<m<.145,\n 1.411<=a<=1.415, 1<=z<=1.01, 2.55<=d<=2.57.        (8)\n\nEvery terminating decimal in this proof denotes the corresponding exact\nrational. From (8),\n\n -.654<kappa<-.639, .169<J<.177,\n -.1<r_u<0,               -.062<r_v<0.             (9)\n\nFor explicit checks: the numerator in -kappa lies between2.2055 and2.2276,\nits denominator between3.411 and3.44915, giving the first bound. Set\nN=z^2(2-a)+a(a^2-2)/2. The polynomial a(a^2-2)/2 is increasing throughout\n(8), so direct endpoint substitution gives .578<N<.603. Dividing by\na+2 gives the asserted J bounds (using the slightly sharper lower\nendpoint .585+1.411*(1.411^2-2)/2>.5785 when desired).\nThe numerator of r_u is at least\n1.411*2.411/2-2*1.01^2=-.3392395 and is negative; its denominator is at\nleast3.411, giving -.1<r_u<0. The numerator of r_v is at least\n1+(1-1.415-1.415^2)/2=-.2086125 and is negative; division by at least3.411\ngives -.062<r_v<0. Thus1/2-kappa^2>1/2-.654^2>0.\n\n## 3. One compact convex set and EVERY original normal inequality\n\nLet K=conv{0,u,v,w,k}. The vectors u,v are independent and\nz*u+a*v+w=0 with all three coefficients positive. Therefore0 lies in the\ninterior of the triangle conv{u,v,w}, and hence in the interior of K.\n\nUse the proposed full trajectory\n\n x0=(a+1)u, g0=u;\n x1=u,     g1=u;\n x2=-z*u,  g2=v;\n x3=w,     g3=w;\n x4=-d*w=4k, g4=k;\n x5=(5/2)k,  g5=k.                                 (10)\n\nEvery equation x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i*g_i follows immediately from (1) and (5).\nTo prove g_i=Proj_K(x_i), it suffices to check\n<r-g_i,x_i-g_i><=0 for all vertices r of K. Linear interpolation then\ngives it for every r in K. These checks cover every ordered original pair,\nincluding optimum and terminal pairs, with one common K.\n\nAt samples1 and3 the residual x_i-g_i is zero. At sample0 it is a*u.\nWe have <v,u>/U=kappa<0, <w,u>/U=r_u<0 and\n<k,u>/U=-(d/4)r_u<(2.57/4)*.1<1; the vertices0 andu also satisfy the\nnormal inequality. This checks sample0.\n\nAt sample2 its residual is y2=-z*u-v. In units of U, the inequalities\nfor vertices0 andu are respectively\n\n <-v,y2>/U=z*kappa+1/2<0,\n <u-v,y2>/U=-z+(z-1)kappa+1/2<=-1/2.\n\nThe vertex w has equality by (7), and the vertex v has equality trivially.\nWrite T=<v,y2>=(-z*kappa-1/2)U>0. By (7), <w,y2>=T. Thus\n<k-v,y2>=-(1+d/4)T<0, which checks the last vertex.\n\nAt samples4 and5 the residuals are3k and(3/2)k. It is enough to check\n<r-k,k><=0. The vertices0,w,k satisfy this directly since k=-d*w/4.\nFor the other two vertices this condition is\n\n r_u >= -d*J/4,       r_v >= -d*J/4.\n\nIt follows from (9), since d*J/4>2.55*.169/4>.1. Every original\nnormal inequality has now been checked without dropping remote pairs.\n\n## 4. A global smooth function with the correct initial distance\n\nDefine\n\n F_t(x)=max_(r in K){<x,r>-||r||^2/2}.              (11)\n\nThe maximizer is unique and equals Proj_K(x), by completing the square.\nIf P and P' are the projections of x and x', their normal inequalities\ngive ||P-P'||^2<=<x-x',P-P'>, so the projection is1-Lipschitz. Evaluating\nthe maxima in (11) at each other's maximizers yields\n\n 0<=F_t(x')-F_t(x)-<P,x'-x>\n      <=<P'-P,x'-x><=||x'-x||^2.\n\nThis proves differentiability with gradient P and1-Lipschitz gradient.\nIt is finite and convex because K is compact and (11) is a maximum of\naffine functions of x. Since0 is interior to K, for every x!=0 a sufficiently\nsmall positive multiple r=epsilon*x belongs to K and makes\n<x,r>-||r||^2/2 positive. Therefore F_t(0)=0 and0 is its UNIQUE minimizer.\nThe initial point in (10) is(1,0), so its true initial distance is exactly1.\n\nSection3 now proves that (10) is the actual GD trajectory of this single\nglobal function. At x5=(5/2)k, (11) gives F_t(x5)=2||k||^2. Thus\n2F_t(x5)=4||k||^2=d^2||w||^2/4, proving (2). Replacing F_t by L*F_t\nfor any L>0 preserves the project's normalized performance under h_i/L.\n\n## 5. Exact payoff comparison over the entire interval\n\nFor t=0, a=s,z=1 and J=(2-s)/(2+s)=1/A. Since(a+1)^2=A and\n2A=(beta-1)(beta+1), (2) is exactly1/B0.\n\nSet R(t)=B0*d^2*N/[4(a+1)^2(a+2)], with N as in section2. The preceding\nbounds give N>0, and differentiation gives\n\n (log R)'=-m/(2d)+2p/(a+1)+N'/N+p/(a+2),\n N'=2z(2-a)q-p[-z^2+(3a^2-2)/2].                  (12)\n\nUsing (8), 2z(2-a)>=1.17 and the square bracket is<1.004. Hence\nN'>1.17*.437-1.004*.149>.361. Since N<.603, (12) gives\n\n (log R)'>-29/1020+98/805+361/603+147/3415>7/10.\n\nThe last comparison is a rational inequality, obtained by multiplication\nby the positive product of the displayed denominators. Integrating from0\nto t and using exp(u)>=1+u proves R(t)>=1+7t/10, namely (3).\n\nFinally the exact benchmark recurrence in b98bb807d514e80d allows the\nprimitive three-step construction(s,2,s), of mass A=1+sum h, and a\ndominant one-step suffix(3/2), of mass4. Its mass join is\n\n G(A,4)=2A+2+2sqrt(1+2A)=(beta+1)^2=B0.\n\nThe optimal primitive mass is at least A and G is increasing in that mass,\nso B5>=B0. This argument needs NO decimal comparison of candidate splits.\nMultiply the legal payoff by B5 to obtain (4).\n\n## 6. What this explains and does not explain\n\nThe constructed path increases total step mass, and its tested finite case\npasses the earlier necessary scalar screens. A planar convex body now gives\na direct, exact obstruction on the whole interval0<t<=1/50, even when the\nentire upper certificate is reselected. The construction uses a true common\nprojection body and all original pairs; it is not a formal Gram test alone.\n\nIt does not claim to attain the full SDP optimum or to require dimension2\nin every worst case. No automatic recursive lift to n15,n31 or unbounded n\nis established here. The separate common-offset mass argument covers the\nasymptotic limitation of that particular dense family; arbitrary coordinated\nredistribution and the original minimax conjecture remain open.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "research/max_dense_projection_quadrilateral.md", "fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290", "b98bb807d514e80d"], "raw_case": "research/max_dense_06_n5_balanced_t002_raw.json", "target_claim": "The evaluated balanced dense redistribution can improve the exact benchmark.", "status": "refuted_by_joint_candidate_pending_verification", "scope": "explicit_five_step_path_only", "is_fragile": false, "fragility_reason": "", "suggested_followup": "none"}, "glossary": {"p": "(16-11sqrt2)/3, the positive leaf decrement in the five-step path.", "q": "(8sqrt2-10)/3, the positive middle-step increment in this finite path only.", "m": "10sqrt2-14=q-2p, total primitive-child direction mass.", "beta": "sqrt(7+4sqrt2), the base five-step root bridge.", "B0": "(beta+1)^2, a legal exact mass candidate satisfying B5>=B0.", "K": "The compact planar convex body conv{0,u,v,w,k} explicitly constructed in the proof.", "F_t": "max_(r in K){<x,r>-||r||²/2}, the single global projection-gradient adversary.", "kappa": "The normalized inner product -(z²+(a+1)/2)/(z(a+2)) of the first two independent gradient vectors."}}
{"id": "d033211fae094942", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T23:46:21.340481+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "C67 exact actual-trajectory counterexample to the fixed scalar-clock transfer: the natural quartic moving-normal clock C_4 can increase by more than3 on a positive sign-preserving FIRST step of every actual H_m,m>=128. Let phi(r)=r^4/4, K={(r,s):s>=phi(r)}, and F(X)=max_(g in K)(<X,g>-||g||^2/2). Completing the square makes grad F the unique Euclidean projection onto K; its two normal inequalities imply firm nonexpansiveness, hence a 1-Lipschitz gradient. Comparing the defining maxima shows differentiability, and F is convex as a supremum of affine functions. For v>=0, X(r,v)=(r+vr^3+r^7/4,-v), G(r)=(r,r^4/4), and eta=v+r^4/4. Their normal residual at any (t,s) in K is at most eta[r^3(t-r)-(t^4-r^4)/4]<=0, proving that G is the actual gradient. Every ordered Q_ij equals eta_j[r_j^3(r_i-r_j)-(r_i^4-r_j^4)/4]<=0. The minimizer set is {(0,y):y<=0}: these points have value0, while testing a small gradient on the quartic graph yields positive value if the horizontal coordinate is nonzero, and a small vertical gradient does so if the vertical coordinate is positive. Thus the true initial squared distance is the square of its horizontal coordinate.\nKeep lambda=1+sqrt2,c=lambda(1+1/m),a=(1-1/m)/c,H_0=(),H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j), m>=128. Bounds sqrt2<99/70 and sqrt2<17/12 give 2/5<a<5/12, so the FIRST actual step has 7/5<h=1+a<17/12<3/2. Start r0=2,v0=0, hence X0=(34,0),R0^2=1156. Actual GD gives x1=34-2h>31 and v1=4h>28/5. Its r1 is the unique positive solution of T_(v1)(r1)=x1, T_v(r)=r+vr^3+r^7/4. At3/2, the gap x1-T_(v1)(3/2)=65/2-(31/2)h-2187/512>37/4-2187/512>0. Hence3/2<r1<2; the upper bound follows from x1<x0,v1>v0 and monotonicity of T_v. This is a genuinely sign-preserving legal step.\nDefine C_4(r,v)=log r+(3/2)vr^2+7r^6/24 for positive r. The exact lower bound is C_4(r1,v1)-C_4(2,0)>log(3/4)+189/10-23569/1536>3+569/2560>3, using log(4/3)<1/3. Thus Delta C<=-kappa h is false for every kappa>=0 in this claimed universal form. The missing state is explicit: for g_v=T_v^(-1), partial_v C_4(g_v(x),v)=g_v(x)^2/2>0. An actual noncrossing step has Delta C_4=-integral_(x_plus)^x dy/g_v(y)+(1/2)integral_v^(v_plus)g_s(x_plus)^2 ds. The second positive integral cannot be discarded. Its upper bound is r^2 Delta v/2=h r^6/8. This refutes the fixed-clock shortcut only, not full-horizon power-family convergence, C65, or the original exact profile. No root approximation, trajectory experiment, optimization or verifier was used.", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"finding_ids": ["c91fb520fcf58307"], "target_claim": "The fixed scalar-profile clock drops on every noncrossing step after transfer to a moving-normal quartic epigraph.", "status": "refuted as an unverified proof draft", "branch_id": "C67", "artifact": "workers/max3/research/c67_power_epigraph_moving_clock.md"}, "glossary": {"C_4": "Natural quartic moving-normal clock log r+(3/2)vr^2+7r^6/24 for r>0.", "T_v": "Positive quartic horizontal map r+vr^3+r^7/4, whose inverse is g_v.", "eta": "Local projection-normal coefficient v+r^4/4 in this quartic counterexample."}}
{"id": "34ec3c403f4b605d", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-27T00:01:01.273576+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "Round17 exact active-face counterexample candidate: at the five-step source word, v=(1,1/2,-1,-1/20,-3/10) decreases Huber, unit quadratic, root-cut and EVERY member of the exact planar continuum with x1=g1,x3=g3; a distinct legal two-dimensional projection face with x1!=g1 has normalized logarithmic slope >1/4 and defeats that direction. All original normals and true distance are explicit. Thus the selected continuum is not a complete full-model separator. UNVERIFIED; no local/global optimality or asymptotic conclusion.", "evidence": "# Two distinct exact projection faces at the five-step source word\n\nStatus: **joint unverified proof draft**, cumulative round17. No optimization\nor verifier call. This is a finite structural result, not a local/global\noptimality claim and not the original asymptotic minimax theorem.\n\nThe normalized performance W_n and B_n are as in accepted facts\n1252b8823bb5a290 and b98bb807d514e80d. The proof below supplies its own\nfunctions and all ordered-pair checks, rather than relying on an unverified\nearlier finding.\n\n## 1. Source word and one common construction convention\n\nWrite s=sqrt(2), theta=s-1, nu=theta^2=3-2s,\nd0=sqrt(7+4s)-1, gamma=d0/4, and B0=(d0+2)^2. The source word is\n\n    h0=(s,2,s,1+d0,3/2).\n\nThe exact recurrence permits this primitive-three-step/dominant-one-step\njoin, so B_5>=B0. Equality of those two finite recurrence values is not\nneeded below and is not inferred from a floating-point maximizing split.\n\nFor any compact convex K containing0 use\n\n    F_K(x)=max_(g in K)(<x,g>-||g||^2/2).\n\nThe maximizer is the unique projection P_K(x). Its defining normal is\n<v-P_K(x),x-P_K(x)><=0 for every vertex v. Two such normals imply\n||P_K(x')-P_K(x)||^2<=<x'-x,P_K(x')-P_K(x)>, proving 1-Lipschitzness.\nThe inequalities obtained by evaluating the two maxima at each other's\nmaximizers bound the differentiability remainder by ||x'-x||^2. Thus F_K\nis convex differentiable and 1-smooth. Its minimizer set is\nK^polar={z:<z,g><=0 for every g in K}: the reverse implication follows by\ntesting epsilon*g and letting epsilon decrease to zero. If X0 belongs to\nthe positive cone of K, then0 is a closest minimizer, since\n<X0,z><=0 implies ||X0-z||^2>=||X0||^2 for z in K^polar.\n\n## 2. A continuum with x1=g1 and x3=g3\n\nNear the source word, write the five steps as\n\n    h=(a,b,c,1+d,e),   z=b-1,   D=2e+1.\n\nFor a parameter r>0 define\n\n    U=1/(a+1)^2,\n    kappa=-[z^2+(c+1)r]/[z(c+2)],\n    u=(1/(a+1),0),\n    v=(kappa,sqrt(r-kappa^2))/(a+1),\n    w=-z*u-c*v,\n    k=-(d/D)*w,\n    J=[z^2(2-c)+c(c^2-2)r]/(c+2).                            (1)\n\nWhenever these vectors satisfy the normal conditions below, use\nK=conv(0,u,v,w,k) and the exact trajectory\n\n    x0=(a+1)u, g0=u;\n    x1=u,     g1=u;\n    x2=-z*u,  g2=v;\n    x3=w,     g3=w;\n    x4=D*k,   g4=k;\n    x5=(e+1)k,g5=k.                                           (2)\n\nEvery update follows from (1). The choice of kappa imposes the exact identity\n<w-v,-z*u-v>=0. Also ||w||^2=J*U by expansion. The attained payoff is\n\n    E_r(h)=d^2*J/[D*(a+1)^2].                                (3)\n\nAt the source a=c=s,b=2,d=d0,e=3/2, (1) reduces to\n\n    kappa=-(r+theta)/s,  J=nu,\n    <u,w>/U=r+s-2,  <v,w>/U=(theta-r)/s.                     (4)\n\nThe Gram condition r>=kappa^2 is exactly nu<=r<=1. At sample0 the normal\nagainst u is zero. Against v and w it is nonpositive because\nkappa<0 and r+s-2<1; against k it follows from\n-gamma*(r+s-2)<=gamma*theta<1. Against0 it is negative.\nSamples1 and3 have zero residual. At sample2, residual y=-u-v, the normals\nagainst0 andu in units of U are respectively theta*(r-1)/s and r-1.\nThe w normal is zero by construction. If T=<v,y>=theta*(1-r)*U/s>=0,\nthe k normal is -(1+gamma)*T. This covers every vertex at sample2.\n\nFor samples4 and5 the residual is a nonnegative multiple of k. The normals\nagainst0,w,k hold directly. Against u andv they are equivalent to\n\n    r+s-2 >= -gamma*nu,\n    (theta-r)/s >= -gamma*nu.\n\nIt follows that the EXACT admissible r interval within this construction is\n\n    r_minus=2-s-gamma*nu <= r <= theta+s*gamma*nu=r_plus.       (5)\n\nIndeed (5) lies strictly inside [nu,1]. Its width is\nnu*((s+1)*gamma-1)>0. For instance the rational square bounds\n1.4142<s<1.4143 and 3.557<d0+1<3.558 give\ngamma>theta and gamma*theta<1, proving these assertions.\n\nThus the source has a continuum of legal full-model witnesses all giving\nE_r(h0)=1/B0: use 2(3+2s)=d0(d0+2) in (3). All old/new normals were retained. This is a continuum inside\nthe complete feasible set, not a claimed description of every tight primal.\n\nFor every fixed r strictly inside (5), all non-identical constraints just\nchecked are strict. Zero residuals and the w-v equality persist identically\nin (1)-(2). Therefore (1)-(3) remain legal for every sufficiently small\nperturbation of all five steps, with r held fixed. The square root also\nremains real by the strict Gram condition. Since u,v are independent and\nz*u+c*v+w=0 with positive coefficients,0 lies inside conv(u,v,w); the\nminimizer is then unique and x0=(1,0) has true distance1.\n\n## 3. The associated necessary first-order cone\n\nLet h(t)=h0+t*v+o(t), t decreasing to0 through positive values, where\nv=(v0,v1,v2,v3,v4) is the five-coordinate step velocity. Differentiate (3)\nat h0. Direct differentiation of J in (1) gives\n\n    L_r(v):=(log E_r(h(t)))'_(t=0)\n      =-2*v0/(s+1)+2*v1+[2(s+2)r-2]*v2\n         +2*v3/d0-v4/2.                                     (6)\n\nHence\n\n    liminf_(t down to0) [B0*W_5(h(t))-1]/t\n         >= max{L_(r_minus)(v),L_(r_plus)(v)}.                (7)\n\nTo justify the endpoints, first apply the lower witness to each fixed\ninterior r, then take the supremum of the affine function L_r over that\ninterval. No exchange of an optimized SDP and differentiation is used.\n\nAny direction whose performance decreases to first order below1/B0 must\nhave both endpoint expressions nonpositive. The balanced dense direction\ntested in the closed experiment is\n\n    v=(-p0,q0,-p0,-m0/4,0),\n    p0=(16-11s)/3, q0=(8s-10)/3, m0=10s-14.\n\nHere EVERY L_r in (5) is positive. In fact r_plus<.57, so\n2(s+2)r-2<1.9. The bounds\n.147<p0<.149, .437<q0<.439, .140<m0<.145 and d0>2.55 give\n\n    L_r > .294/2.415+.874-1.9*.149-.145/5.10 > 2/3.           (8)\n\nThis explains that local failure by exact common-function witnesses,\nindependently of the reselected dual array.\n\n## 4. An independent face with a nonzero residual at sample1\n\nThe preceding cone is incomplete. A different exact construction works for\nALL a,c,e>=0, b>1 and d>0. Put\n\n    z=b-1,  r=z/(c+1),  D=2e+1,\n    U_vec=(1,0), V_vec=(0,sqrt(r)), k=-(d/D)*V_vec,\n    K=conv(0,U_vec,V_vec,k),\n    X0=(a+b)*U_vec+(c+1)*V_vec.\n\nThe full trajectory is\n\n    X1=b*U_vec+(c+1)*V_vec,\n    X2=(c+1)*V_vec,\n    X3=V_vec,\n    X4=D*k,\n    X5=(e+1)*k,\n\nwith gradients U_vec,U_vec,V_vec,V_vec,k,k in chronological order.\nAt X0 the normal against V_vec is\nr(c+1)-(a+b-1)=-a<=0. Against0 andk it is also nonpositive, since\na+b-1=a+z>0. At X1 the V_vec normal is zero because r(c+1)=z,\nwhile0 andk have nonpositive normals. At X2 the residual is c*V_vec,\nso the normals against the orthogonal U_vec,0, and the opposite k all\nhold. X3 has zero residual. At X4 andX5 the residual is a nonnegative\nmultiple of k; U_vec is orthogonal to k and V_vec is opposite to k,\nso all normals hold. These are every original projection condition.\n\nAlso X0 belongs to the positive cone of U_vec,V_vec, so0 is a closest\nminimizer. The exact attained normalized payoff is\n\n    E_orth(h)=d^2*r/[D*((a+b)^2+r(c+1)^2)]\n            =d^2*z/[D*(c+1)*((a+b)^2+z(c+1))].               (9)\n\nAt h0, (3+2s)^2*theta=7+5s=(s+2)^2+(s+1), proving\nE_orth(h0)=1/B0. Its logarithmic derivative is\n\n    L_orth(v)=(2nu-2theta)*(v0+v2)+2nu*v1\n                 +2*v3/d0-v4/2.                            (10)\n\nFor example the coefficient of v0 in log(9) is\n-2(s+2)/(7+5s)=2nu-2theta; the coefficient of v1 is\n1-[2(s+2)+(s+1)]/(7+5s)=2nu. The c coefficient equals the a coefficient.\nThe last two coefficients follow directly from d^2/D.\n\nThis family retains a nonzero proximal residual X1-U_vec. It is therefore\nnot contained in the x1=g1 construction in section2. No ambient-rank or\ndual-support argument licenses discarding it.\n\n## 5. A concrete direction missed by the first cone and scalar screens\n\nTake the exact velocity\n\n    v_escape=(1,1/2,-1,-1/20,-3/10).                           (11)\n\nFor sufficiently small positive t every actual step of h0+t*v_escape\nis positive. The three standard legal lower tests all decrease at t=0:\n\n* The global Huber test has logarithmic derivative\n  -2*sum(v_escape)/B0=-3/(10B0)<0.\n* The unit-curvature quadratic test has logarithmic derivative\n  2*(1/2-1/(20d0)-3/5)=-1/5-1/(10d0)<0.\n* The cut at the fourth step has logarithmic derivative\n  -1/(10d0)-1/(3+2s)+3/20=-1/(10d0)-nu+3/20<0.\n\nAll witnesses in the continuum (5) also decrease. Substitution in (6) gives\n\n    L_r(v_escape)=63/20-2theta-2(s+2)r-1/(10d0)<0.\n\nFor an entirely rational bound, (5) gives r_minus>.475; then\n63/20-2*.4142-2*3.4142*.475<0, even after dropping the final negative term.\n\nNevertheless (10) gives\n\n    L_orth(v_escape)=nu+3/20-1/(10d0)>1/4,                   (12)\n\nbecause nu>.17 and d0>2.5. Thus for all sufficiently small positive t,\nthe exact legal function in section4 has normalized payoff strictly above\n1/B0, and hence W_5(h0+t*v_escape)>1/B0>=1/B_5.\n\nThis is a counterexample to the sufficiency of the stated first-order\nscreen, not a counterexample to the minimax conjecture. No additional\nconcrete numerical case was evaluated or optimization proposed. The exact\ndirection (11) is an analytic stress test with its own explicit defeating\nfunction.\n\nThe outcome explains a real limitation of the previously selected active\ngeometry: fixing the first post-step point to equal its gradient deletes\nanother source-tight face. It does not prove that the enlarged collection\nis complete or that the source word is locally optimal. A proof using the\nentire active set across growing horizons is still missing.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"plan_id": "max17_planar_active_face", "artifact": "research/max_dense_active_faces.md", "fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290", "b98bb807d514e80d"], "finding_ids": ["60cb6c975256db00", "4260d72c8bb3b963", "436df4c878e9ba06"], "status": "refuted_selected_screen_sufficiency_unverified_candidate", "target_claim": "The stated planar continuum plus the three scalar first-order tests controls all improving source variations.", "assumptions_satisfied": ["Every tested step is positive for sufficiently small positive path parameter.", "Every original projection normal is retained in each explicit defeating function.", "The initial norm equals true closest-minimizer distance."], "failed_conclusion": "All selected directional scores decrease while another exact legal face increases.", "scope": "finite_source_face_only"}, "glossary": {"E_r": "The attained payoff d^2 J/[D(a+1)^2] in the variable-norm planar five-step construction.", "r_minus": "2-s-gamma*(s-1)^2, the exact lower admissible source parameter.", "r_plus": "s-1+s*gamma*(s-1)^2, the exact upper admissible source parameter.", "E_orth": "The attained payoff d^2 z/[D(c+1)((a+b)^2+z(c+1))] in the independent orthogonal-prefix projection construction.", "v_escape": "The exact step velocity (1,1/2,-1,-1/20,-3/10)."}}
