{"id": "44d17b22a6df3b97", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-27T00:52:14.668832+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "C71 COMPLETE UNVERIFIED actual large-power counterexample to unrestricted extension of the per-turn digit-sum charge R_t<=c^(-b(t))R_0. For every m>=512 choose p=2+2m log(1024m), rho=1-1/(2m), zeta=(a+rho)/(1-h_0*rho^(p-1)-rho^(2p-1)), r_0=(p*zeta)^(1/(2p-2)),v_0=0 on the original one global power epigraph. The first step is nonturning, the second actual turn has c R_2/R_0>1+1/(256m), and the THIRD actual turn already recovers R_3<R_0/c^2. All-pair legality and true distance are proved. This refutes only the pointwise charge beyond the stated power range, not the total J bound or terminal payoff or whole power model; no full-horizon adversary payoff is claimed.", "evidence": "# C71: an actual large-power turn violates the digit-sum envelope, then recovers\n\nStatus: COMPLETE UNVERIFIED counterexample draft, fifth launch round 2,\ncumulative 13. This is an exact symbolic construction, with no trajectory\nevaluation, optimizer, verifier call, or changed allocation. Its conclusions\nconcern the displayed proposed charge, not the entire power-epigraph model.\nNo unverified finding is used as a mathematical predecessor.\n\n## 1. Exact family, failed claim, and conclusions\n\nFor each integer m>=512 put\n\n    epsilon=1/m, lambda=1+sqrt(2),\n    c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=(1-epsilon)/c,\n    d_j=a c^j, H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+d_j,H_j),\n    h_0=1+a, h_1=1+ac=2-epsilon.\n\nThe first three physical steps of H_m are h_0,h_1,h_0. In this\ndocument h_0,h_1 denote step LEVELS; physical times are 1,2,3.\nChoose the fixed-during-run real power and ratio\n\n    p=2+2m log(1024m), rho=1-1/(2m), w=rho^(p-2).\n\nDefine the following positive exact quantities:\n\n    E=h_0 rho^(p-1)+rho^(2p-1), D=1-E,\n    zeta=(a+rho)/D,\n    r_0=(p zeta)^(1/(2p-2)), v_0=0.                    (1)\n\nSection 3 proves D>0. Use this initial state on the single global function\n\n    phi_p(r)=|r|^p/p, K_p={(r,s):s>=phi_p(r)},\n    F_p(X)=max_(g in K_p)(<X,g>-||g||^2/2),\n    u=(v+phi_p(r))|r|^(p-2),\n    X_p(r,v)=(r(1+u),-v), G_p(r)=(r,phi_p(r)).           (2)\n\nWriting R_t for the absolute horizontal coordinate after physical\nstep t, and b(t) for the binary digit sum, the exact actual trajectory\nsatisfies\n\n    r_1=rho r_0>0, v_1=h_0 r_0^p/p,\n    u_1=zeta*(h_0 rho^(p-2)+rho^(2p-2)),\n    x_2<0, R_2=(ac-u_1)rho r_0,\n    c R_2/R_0>1+1/(256m)>1.                            (3)\n\nThus physical step 2 is a genuine turn and violates\n\n    R_t<=c^(-b(t))R_0 at every turn                     (4)\n\nif (4) is extended without a power restriction. Here b(2)=1.\nThis does NOT contradict the C70 range, since p/(m log m)->2.\nThe same trajectory then has\n\n    x_3>0, R_3<R_0/c^2.                                (5)\n\nSince b(3)=2, the next actual turn has already recovered the displayed\nenvelope. This exact recovery prevents interpreting the local failure\nas evidence of a useful full-horizon payoff or a failure of all\nchronological power-family bounds.\n\n## 2. Global legality and unique actual recurrence\n\nThe epigraph K_p is nonempty, closed, and convex. Completing the square\nshows that F_p is finite, convex, and its maximizer is the Euclidean\nprojection Pi_(K_p)(X). At two projections the normal inequalities imply\n||Pi_(K_p)(X)-Pi_(K_p)(Y)||^2\n<=<Pi_(K_p)(X)-Pi_(K_p)(Y),X-Y>, hence 1-Lipschitzness.\nComparing the defining maxima bounds the first-order remainder at X\nbetween zero and ||X-Y||^2. Therefore F_p is differentiable with\n1-Lipschitz gradient Pi_(K_p).\n\nFor eta=v+phi_p(r), X_p-G_p=eta*(phi_p'(r),-1).\nIts inner product with every (z,s)-G_p, (z,s) in K_p, is at most\neta*[phi_p'(r)(z-r)-phi_p(z)+phi_p(r)]<=0. Thus its actual gradient\nis G_p(r). For any two states, with f_i=F_p(X_i), the ordered residual\nis exactly\n\n    f_i-f_j+<G_i,X_j-X_i>+||G_i-G_j||^2/2\n      =<X_j-G_j,G_i-G_j>\n      =eta_j*[phi_p'(r_j)(r_i-r_j)-phi_p(r_i)\n                                      +phi_p(r_j)]<=0.  (6)\n\nEvery state and all remote ordered pairs are legal for this same\nglobal objective. Substituting the gradient into the maximum gives\n\n    2F_p(X_p(r,v))\n      =r^2+(2-2/p)v|r|^p+(2p-1)|r|^(2p)/p^2.\n\nAt (0,y), y<=0, all defining values are nonpositive and zero is\nfeasible. At x!=0, a sufficiently small boundary gradient with the\nsign of x makes the value positive, because its linear term dominates.\nAt x=0,y>0 a small positive vertical gradient does so. Consequently\n\n    min F_p=0, argmin F_p={(0,y):y<=0},\n    dist(X_p(r,v),argmin F_p)=|r|(1+u), v>=0.             (7)\n\nIn particular the denominator R_0=r_0(1+zeta) in (3) is the true\nclosest-minimizer distance.\n\nFor every actual step h, the state satisfies\n\n    v_plus=v+h|r|^p/p, x_plus=x-hr,\n    x_plus=T_(p,v_plus)(r_plus),\n    T_(p,v)(r)=r+v r|r|^(p-2)+r|r|^(2p-2)/p.             (8)\n\nT is odd, strictly increasing onto R: its derivative off zero is\n1+(p-1)v|r|^(p-2)+(2p-1)|r|^(2p-2)/p>=1, and it has derivative\n1 at zero. Equation (8) therefore defines the unique actual trajectory\nat every later step of the original H_m, without changing v or p.\n\n## 3. Exact quantitative estimates\n\nFor m>=512, sqrt(2)<99/70 gives\nc<(169/70)(513/512)<121/50<5/2. Hence 0<a<1/2 and h_0<3/2.\nThe inequality log(1-t)<=-t, 0<t<1, proves\n\n    0<w=rho^(p-2)<=exp(-(p-2)/(2m))=1/(1024m).           (9)\n\nAlso rho^(p-1)=rho w and rho^(2p-1)=rho^3 w^2. Thus\n\n    E< (3/2)w+w^2<2w, D>1-2w>1/2,\n    0<zeta<3,\n    0<zeta-(a+rho)=(a+rho)E/D<6w,\n    0<u_1=zeta*(h_0 w+rho^2 w^2)<6w.                   (10)\n\nAll these bounds have explicit constants independent of m.\n\nPut M_1=2+a. Using lambda=2+1/lambda and ac=1-epsilon,\n\n    c(ac)-M_1\n      =lambda(1-epsilon^2)-2-(1-epsilon)/(lambda(1+epsilon))\n      =2epsilon/c-lambda epsilon^2.\n\nSince 2/c>4/5 and lambda epsilon<5/1024<1/100,\n\n    c(ac)-M_1>(79/100)epsilon.                          (11)\n\nAlso c(ac)=lambda(1-epsilon^2)<lambda<5/2. Therefore\n\n    c rho(ac)-(h_0+rho)\n      =[c(ac)-M_1]-(1-rho)[c(ac)-1]\n      >(79/100)epsilon-(epsilon/2)*(3/2)\n      =epsilon/25.                                     (12)\n\nSubtracting the actual errors from (10) yields\n\n    c rho(ac-u_1)-(1+zeta)\n      >epsilon/25-[6w+(5/2)*6w]\n      >=epsilon*(1/25-21/1024)>epsilon/64.               (13)\n\nThe last strict inequality is a rational comparison:\n1/25-21/1024=499/25600>400/25600=1/64.\nSince 1+zeta<4, division proves the strict normalized gap in (3).\n\n## 4. These are the first actual steps, not independently chosen states\n\nAt the initial state, r_0^(2p-2)/p=zeta and u_0=zeta. After the\nfirst step h_0 the horizontal coordinate is\n\n    x_1=r_0(1+zeta-h_0)=r_0(zeta-a).\n\nThe defining equation zeta D=a+rho is equivalent to\n\n    zeta-a=rho+zeta*(h_0 rho^(p-1)+rho^(2p-1))\n           =rho*(1+u_1).\n\nAt v_1=h_0 r_0^p/p, the candidate r_1=rho r_0 has exactly normal\ncoefficient u_1 from (3), hence\nT_(p,v_1)(r_1)=x_1. Strict monotonicity in (8) proves that this\ncandidate is the unique actual r_1. In particular the first step is\nnonturning and nonabsorbing.\n\nThe second step is h_1=1+ac. Equations (8),(10) give\n\n    x_2=r_1(1+u_1-h_1)=(u_1-ac)rho r_0<0,\n\nsince u_1<6/(1024m)<ac=1-1/m. Thus the second step is a genuine\nturn, with v_2=v_1+h_1 r_1^p/p retained. Equations (7),(13) prove\nits failure of (4). No approximation has replaced either transition.\n\n## 5. The next actual step restores the envelope\n\nLet q_2=|r_2| be the magnitude uniquely specified by (8) at x_2<0.\nThe identity R_2=q_2(1+u_2) gives\n\n    q_2<=R_2<ac*rho*r_0<rho*r_0,\n    v_2=(r_0^p/p)*(h_0+h_1 rho^p).\n\nConsequently\n\n    u_2=v_2 q_2^(p-2)+q_2^(2p-2)/p\n       <=zeta*[(h_0+h_1 rho^p)rho^(p-2)+rho^(2p-2)]\n       <3*[(7/2)w+w^2]<12w.                            (14)\n\nThe elementary bound a>(511/512)*(50/121)>2/5, together with (9),\nimplies u_2<a. Thus the third physical step, again h_0, actually\nturns from negative to positive. Its radius satisfies\n\n    R_3=(a-u_2)q_2<a R_2<a(ac)rho r_0.                  (15)\n\nIt remains to compare this to the original radius. An exact expansion\nusing (11)'s identity gives\n\n    M_1-c(1-epsilon)^2\n      =epsilon*(lambda-2/c)+lambda epsilon^2(1-epsilon)>0.\n\nHere lambda-2/c>0 since lambda c>lambda^2>2. Therefore\nc(1-epsilon)^2-1<h_0. Multiplying by 0<rho<1 shows\nc(1-epsilon)^2 rho<h_0+rho: if the left coefficient before\nmultiplication is nonnegative it decreases, and if it is negative\nthe inequality holds because h_0>0. Finally (10) gives\n\n    c^2 a(ac)rho=c(1-epsilon)^2 rho\n       <h_0+rho<1+zeta=R_0/r_0.\n\nTogether with (15), this proves (5).\n\n## 6. What is missing from the failed charge\n\nThe actual first step reduces |r| by only the relative amount\n1/(2m), but takes its effective normal from u_0=zeta to u_1<6/(1024m).\nIndeed zeta>a+rho>1 for m>=512. The dependence on the power permits\nthis sharp normal change despite a small gradient change. Both v_1\nand v_2 are retained exactly, and v increases throughout.\n\nThus the proposed cost-free digit-sum envelope at EACH turn cannot\nbe made uniform in unrestricted horizon-dependent p. This does not\nrefute the positive J cost itself, the global bound R_t<=R_0, or the\nfinal 1/(2S_m) objective estimate outside C70's stated domain: those\nwould be different claims. In this very example the next actual short\nstep supplies compensating contraction. A full remaining-range proof\nwould need to retain the current normal/gradient state or amortize\nsuch losses over actual multi-step episodes, instead of insisting on\nthe same envelope at every individual turn.\n\nNo full-horizon lower payoff is asserted for (1). The construction\nextends uniquely through H_m by (8), but only its first three exact\nsteps are used to refute the stated charge and demonstrate recovery.\nThe original unrestricted exact-profile target remains open.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"finding_ids": ["dda58b63602b99f9", "62bd9c45d63e30f3"], "artifact": "research/c71_actual_large_power_turn_envelope_failure.md", "branch_id": "C71", "target_claim": "For all p>2 fixed during the run, every actual turn of original H_m obeys R_t<=c^(-b(t))*R_0.", "status": "refuted_at_draft_level", "assumptions_satisfied": ["Single unchanged global smooth convex power-epigraph function", "Actual original first three steps with enclosing a_m,c_m", "Inherited nondecreasing v", "Power fixed throughout run", "True closest-minimizer normalization"], "failed_conclusion": "At actual turn t=2, c R_2/R_0>1+1/(256m).", "impact": "A proof for unrestricted powers must allow local envelope deficits and retain normal/gradient state or amortize actual multi-step recovery. The next real turn already pays this particular deficit."}, "glossary": {"C71": "This complete unverified actual prefix construction; every function and transition is proved here.", "p": "The fixed-during-run power2+2m*log(1024m).", "rho": "Prescribed first-step gradient ratio1-1/(2m), not a stepsize schedule.", "h_0": "The level0 step1+a, occurring at physical steps1 and3.", "h_1": "The level1 step1+ac=2-1/m, occurring at physical step2.", "zeta": "Exact initial normal variable(a+rho)/(1-h_0*rho^(p-1)-rho^(2p-1)); v_0=0.", "w": "rho^(p-2), bounded above by1/(1024m).", "R_t": "Actual absolute horizontal coordinate after physical step t; R_0 is the true initial distance to the minimizer ray.", "q_2": "The magnitude |r_2| of the actual gradient's horizontal component after physical step2.", "b(t)": "Binary digit sum of physical time t.", "u_t": "Actual normal variable(v_t+|r_t|^p/p)|r_t|^(p-2)."}}
{"id": "a2c7ec54a8202c87", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-27T01:15:35.827726+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "max2 scope regression for the new tail theorem: the same actual-level single-swap foil obeys all the new birth-support/elapsed-clock and super-geometric tail bounds, yet its formal kernel has J<-19/10. {"id": "9efe93259e22ef06", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-27T01:36:06.614813+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "Exact scope counterexample to the implication 'positive defect on the same3-step word plus a legal first balanced join forces inherited two-row charge>1 at the fourth bridge'. With terminal ratio599/600, the actual seed has kappa>1+1/2000, an explicit optimal legal first transverse coupling, and charge<1-1/4000. No legal63-step repair is claimed.", "evidence": "Self-contained scope test, UNVERIFIED. The only accepted premise is the original Q definition and necessity in1252b8823bb5a290. Fix s=sqrt2, lambda=1+s, theta=s-1, a=(311s-16)/300 and b=(590+8s)/300. These are the t=1/100 word coefficients used in all earlier parent comparisons. The claim being tested is only a seed-plus-first-join implication, not feasibility of a later parent. For a residual y=delta*w and gradient g=w/rho, direct expansion gives Q/e=-r*rho*c_j-1; this supplies the threshold used below.\n\n## 2. All real transverse freedom and every original constraint\n\nA child input consists of a finite nonnegative word p of length ell and\nvectors x_i,g_i for 0<=i<=ell, including all duplicates and the endpoint.\nDefine y_i=x_i-g_i, e_i=<g_i,y_i>, w=x_ell=g_ell!=0, and require\n\n    x_(i+1)=x_i-p_i g_i,\n    <g_j-g_i,y_i><=0 for every i,j, e_i>=0.              (6)\n\nSet K=conv(0,g_0,...,g_ell) and\n\n    F_K(x)=max_(v in K)(<x,v>-||v||^2/2),\n    f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2, f_star=0.                (7)\n\nCompleting the square proves that the maximizer is P_K(x), the Euclidean\nprojection. Its unique minimizer condition is\n<v-P_K(x),x-P_K(x)><=0 for every v in K. Thus (6) makes g_i=P_K(x_i).\nAdding this condition at two points gives\n||P_K(x)-P_K(z)||^2<=<x-z,P_K(x)-P_K(z)>, hence P_K is 1-Lipschitz.\nEvaluation of the two maxima at the opposite maximizers gives\n0<=F_K(x+d)-F_K(x)-<P_K(x),d><=||d||^2. Consequently F_K is differentiable,\nconvex and 1-smooth, with gradient P_K, and it interpolates (7).\n\nThe original residual from fact 1252b8823bb5a290 is\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2\n        =<g_i-g_j,y_j>.                                 (8)\n\nExpansion of the inner products proves this identity. In particular,\nQ_i,star=0 and Q_star,i=-e_i. These include both optimum incidences.\n\nThe minimizer set of F_K is K^polar={z:<z,v><=0 for every v in K}.\nFor necessity, test epsilon*v in (7), divide by epsilon, and let epsilon\ndecrease to zero. Sufficiency follows directly from (7), including v=0.\nThe update equations imply x_0=w+sum_(i<ell)p_i g_i. For every minimizer z,\n<x_0,z><=0, and ||x_0-z||^2>=||x_0||^2. Zero is therefore a closest\nminimizer: the TRUE distance is R=||x_0||>0.\n\nFor two children L,R, set R_R=||x_R,0||, e=w_L/||w_L||, d=x_R,0/R_R,\nalpha=r||w_L||/R_R for the bridge 1+r, where r>0. Let E=e^perp in the\nleft child span and Dspace=d^perp in the right child span. Write\nu_perp=u-<u,e>e and v_perp=v-<v,d>d.\n\nEvery allowed pair of real isometries J_L,J_R satisfies J_R d=-J_L e.\nIt therefore has cross form\n\n    B_C(u,v)=<J_L u,J_R v>\n       =-<u,e><v,d>+<u_perp,C v_perp>,                    (9)\n\nwhere C:Dspace->E is linear and ||C||<=1. To prove necessity, project\nJ_R v_perp orthogonally onto J_L E; its norm is at most ||v_perp||.\nAll axial/transverse cross terms vanish because the transverse images are\northogonal to the common axis. No other restriction on C follows.\n\nConversely, for any real C with ||C||<=1, put S_C=(I-C^T C)^(1/2), the\npositive semidefinite real square root. In R direct-sum E direct-sum Dspace,\ndefine\n\n    J_L u=(<u,e>,u_perp,0),\n    J_R v=(-<v,d>,C v_perp,S_C v_perp).                   (10)\n\nThe equality C^T C+S_C^2=I proves both isometries, their alignment, and (9).\nZero eigenvalues are retained; nothing is clipped or discarded. In chosen\northonormal transverse bases, the exact PSD condition is\n\n    [[I,C],[C^T,I]] >=0.                                (11)\n\nIndeed its quadratic form at (z,v) is\n||z+C v||^2+<v,(I-C^T C)v>. Setting z=-Cv proves necessity, and the same\nidentity proves sufficiency. Thus (11) is equivalent to realizability,\nincluding singular cases. For the fixed 31-step children their full spans\nhave dimension 9 and C is an arbitrary 8 by 8 real matrix. The ranks 2,3,5,9\nof the zero-map seed and successive joins follow from the direct-sum\nrealization (10): the two spans intersect exactly in their aligned axis.\nThe gradients span each child space, because the seed U,V do and every\njoin retains both embedded spanning families.\n\nUse J_L(x_L,i,g_L,i,y_L,i) as all left data and\nalpha J_R(x_R,j,g_R,j,y_R,j) as all right data. Let m=ell_L+1, so that\nright child sample j has parent index m+j. The bridge equation is exact:\n\n    J_L w_L-(1+r)J_L w_L=alpha J_R x_R,0.               (12)\n\nLeft values remain f_L,i, and right values are alpha^2 f_R,j. All original\nconstraints are precisely the following list:\n\n    Q_(i,k)=Q^L_(i,k)                                      (left/left),\n    Q_(m+j,m+l)=alpha^2 Q^R_(j,l)                         (right/right),\n    Q_(m+j,i)=alpha B_C(y_L,i,g_R,j)-e_L,i,               (13L)\n    Q_(i,m+j)=alpha B_C(g_L,i,y_R,j)-alpha^2 e_R,j,       (13R)\n    Q_(i,star)=Q_(m+j,star)=0,\n    Q_(star,i)=-e_L,i, Q_(star,m+j)=-alpha^2 e_R,j.\n\nEvery i,k ranges over the entire left sample list and every j,l over the\nentire right sample list. Diagonal rows are zero. There are no other rows.\nIn particular the left endpoint/bridge sample i=ell_L has y_L,i=0, so\nQ_(m+j,ell_L)=0, while its reverse rows are\n\n    Q_(ell_L,m+j)\n      = r||w_L||^2/R_R^2 [-<x_R,0,y_R,j>-r e_R,j].       (14)\n\nThese rows are invariant under C. All terminal-residual rows are zero\nbecause y_R,ell_R=0. If a left residual happens to equal a_pos*w_L with\na_pos>0 and its gradient is w_L, the other invariant rows are\n\n    Q_(m+j,i)=a_pos||w_L||^2[-r c_R,j-1],\n    c_R,j=<g_R,j,x_R,0>/R_R^2.                          (15)\n\nEquations (13L)-(13R) are the TWO full sets of original cross normals;\nneither is replaced by a sign condition or by PSD. If and only if all their\nrows are nonpositive, (7) for the parent convex hull constructs one common\nfunction, checks every Q, and gives true initial distance R_L. The endpoint\nis W=alpha J_R w_R. For capacities A=1+sum p_L and B=1+sum p_R, and\nP=A+B+r, put kappa_L=A^2||w_L||^2/R_L^2 and\nkappa_R=B^2||w_R||^2/R_R^2. Direct substitution gives\n\n    kappa_parent=P^2 r^2 kappa_L kappa_R/(A^2 B^2).       (16)\n\nThe choice rP=AB yields their product. For identical capacities the\npositive solution is r=theta A and P=lambda A, giving (1). This ratio law\ndoes not imply legality. No ratio is used as an actual payoff when (13)\nfails.\n\n\n\n## 8. Scope test: a changed actual seed delays this particular separator\n\nThis is a separate construction on the SAME three-step word (5), not a\nreplacement of the fixed children in the preceding discovery comparisons.\nIt sharpens the scope of the result; it is NOT a legal 63-step repair.\nThere was no optimization evaluation on this changed seed.\n\nKeep the exact a,b of (5), and set\n\n    rho=599/600, Klen=a+rho, z=b-1, m=a+b,\n    eta=z/Klen, Rnew^2=m^2+Klen*z, A=1+2a+b.\n\nTake orthogonal U,V with ||U||^2=1 and ||V||^2=eta, and define\n\n    (x_0,g_0)=(mU+Klen V,U),\n    (x_1,g_1)=(bU+Klen V,U),\n    (x_2,g_2)=(Klen V,V), (x_3,g_3)=(rho V,rho V).      (33)\n\nThe residuals are y_0=(m-1)U+Klen V, y_1=zU+Klen V,\ny_2=(Klen-1)V, y_3=0. The three GD updates have steps a,b,a.\nHere Klen>1 and 0<rho<1. At samples 0 and 1 the normals against V are\n-a and 0, and the normals against 0 are -(m-1),-z. Normals against rho V\nare no larger than those against V because all the V/residual pairings\nare nonnegative. At sample 2 the normals against U and 0 equal\n-(Klen-1)eta, and the normal against rho V equals\n-(1-rho)(Klen-1)eta. At sample 3 all residuals vanish. Normals against\neach sample's own gradient vanish. This checks every distinct vertex of\nconv(0,U,V,rho V). Section 2 supplies the actual common function, values,\nand TRUE distance Rnew, since x_0=rho V+(a+b)U+aV is a nonnegative\ncombination of the actual gradients.\n\nThis seed still has positive defect, with\n\n    kappa_new=A^2 rho^2 eta/Rnew^2 >1+1/2000.            (34)\n\nIt also has an explicit legal first transverse join and an optimal signed\ninitial/gradient minimum at that join. Take r_0=theta A and use transverse\nbases U on the left and\n(Klen sqrt(eta)/Rnew, -m/Rnew) on the right in the orthonormal coordinates\n(U,V/sqrt(eta)). The latter vector is perpendicular to x_0/Rnew and has\nnorm one. Let the scalar cross map be c in [0,1]. The scaled physical\ncross matrix on the pair (U,V) is exactly\n\n    r_0 rho eta/Rnew^2 * [[Klen*c, -m*c],[-m,-z]].       (35)\n\nThis follows directly from (9) with w=rho V and\nalpha=r_0 rho sqrt(eta)/Rnew. It is realized by (10), with scalar\nS_C=sqrt(1-c^2), so no PSD correction is implicit.\n\nAll cross gradient/residual inner products in BOTH directions are\nnonpositive. To check the only possibly positive terms, remove the\npositive prefactor in (35). Pair a right U with the three nonzero left\nresiduals; the results are\n\n    Klen[(m-1)c-m], Klen[zc-m], -m(Klen-1),\n\nwhich are nonpositive because c<=1 and m-z=a+1>0. Pair a left U with\nthe three nonzero right residuals; the results are\n\n    -Klen*c, -Klen*(a+1)*c, -m*c*(Klen-1),\n\nwhich are nonpositive because c>=0. Every remaining pair uses the V row\nor column of (35), whose entries are nonpositive, with nonnegative\nresidual coefficients; rho V contributes an additional positive factor.\nThe fourth residuals are zero. Subtracting the nonnegative energies in\n(13) checks every cross Q. Thus this is an actual legal seven-step join\nfor EVERY c in [0,1], with true distance Rnew and ratio kappa_new^2.\n\nThe retained signed correlations of its right U,V gradients are\n\n    c_U=r_0 rho*m*z*(c-1)/Rnew^4,\n    c_V=-r_0 rho*eta*(m^2 c+Klen*z)/Rnew^4.\n\nChoose\n\n    c_opt=(a+1)Klen/[m(m+Klen)] in (0,1).               (36)\n\nThen c_U=c_V=-r_0 k_new/(m+Klen), where k_new=rho*z/Rnew^2.\nThe right rho V gradient has correlation rho*c_V and is less negative;\nthe left gradients have positive initial correlations. Equation\nm*g_right,U+Klen*g_right,V=-r_0 w proves that their weighted average\nhas this value for ANY transverse coupling, so (36) maximizes the\nminimum signed initial/gradient correlation over every allowed coupling\nat the first join. It is an exact argument, not a numerical optimizer.\n\nThe last queried gradient in (33) is w/rho, rather than w. Thus the\nendpoint-facing row threshold (30) becomes c_R,j>=-1/(rho*r). The\npositive weighted combination inherited from these two first-join\ngradients would, at the fourth balanced bridge r_3=theta lambda^3 A,\nhave normalized residual Charge_new-1, where\n\n    Charge_new=r_3 rho*r_0 k_new/(m+Klen)<1-1/4000.     (37)\n\nConsequently that inherited two-row certificate is strictly NEGATIVE\nfor this changed actual seed; it cannot exclude the 63-step parent.\nNeither existence of legal intervening 15-,31-step general-coupling\nchildren nor feasibility of its 63-step parent is asserted here.\n\nFor a mechanically checkable sign audit of (34),(37), their exact\nexpressions in Q(sqrt(2)) are\n\n    kappa_new-1 =\n      [-19019047380837966731+13474265781546157318*s]\n          /40842989603870102500,\n\n    1-Charge_new =\n      [341554841617983701-241357907739927883*s]\n          /603687956995287500.\n\nFor the first margin, write the displayed positive integers in its\nnumerator and denominator as P1,Q1,D1, so the numerator is -P1+Q1*s.\nThen\n\n    2(2000 Q1)^2-(2000 P1+D1)^2\n       =2441206548123238434623351342332719331750000>0.\n\nBoth compared quantities are positive, so this proves (34). For the\nsecond margin write its numerator as P2-Q2*s and its denominator as D2.\nThe quantity 4000 P2-D2 is positive, and\n\n    (4000 P2-D2)^2-2(4000 Q2)^2\n       =789713290761361657120990345679724250000>0.\n\nThis proves (37). The seven exact sign/formula checks are recorded in\nmax_transverse_changed_seed_scope_audit.json. {"id": "ca035edd81cd1916", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-27T01:51:29.619444+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "Qualitative phase nonconstancy, positivity, Lipschitz regularity, periodicity, and even the source's global phase enclosure do not imply any prescribed nonzero improvement margin or a favorable specified input ray. This refutes a proof shortcut only; the auxiliary phases below are not solutions of the exact benchmark Bellman recurrence.", "evidence": "Fix an irrational phase shift alpha, a proposed factor a0 in(0,1), and an input phase t0. Choose K>0 and epsilon_amp in(0,(1-a0)/(1+a0)). Set psi_amp(t)=K(1+epsilon_amp*cos(2*pi*(t-t0))) on R/Z. This function is positive,1-periodic,Lipschitz and nonconstant. Since its values lie between K(1-epsilon_amp) and K(1+epsilon_amp), every ratio psi_amp(t)/psi_amp(t+alpha) is at least(1-epsilon_amp)/(1+epsilon_amp)>a0. To also impose any source-shaped enclosure c<min psi_amp<max psi_amp<=c/phi with c>0 and0<phi<1, take K=c(1+1/phi)/2 and choose epsilon_amp additionally below min{1-c/K,c/(phi*K)-1}, both positive. {"id": "bf740a9c1acf0955", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-27T03:30:11.302427+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "C80 exact two-dimensional four-step obstruction DRAFT: h=(1450,2269,1656,1345)/1000 has sum168/25, satisfies every primitive single-cut test STRICTLY, and satisfies the primitive inequality for EVERY convex quadratic (all curvatures0..1, exact Bernstein certificate). UNVERIFIED; no seed or original-target result.", "evidence": "# C80: an exact two-dimensional four-step primitive obstruction\n\nStatus: UNVERIFIED joint candidate, no verifier allocation. This tests a\nspecific full primitive interface, not the original asymptotic theorem.\nThe defining construction and every required normal are given below.\n\nLet h=(a,b,c,d), with a,d>=0, b>1, c>1. Define\n\n P=a+1, B=b-1, C=c-1, D=d+1, U=b+c+d, A=1+a+b+c+d.\n\nAssume D>BC and put\n\n Kappa=B^2 C+U(D-BC), t=B^2 C/Kappa.\n\nThen Kappa>0 and0<t<1. In the Euclidean plane define\n\n u=(1,0), w=(t,sqrt(t(1-t))), v=-(B u+D w)/c,\n K=conv(u,v,w), F(x)=max_(g in K)(<x,g>-||g||^2/2).\n\nHere K is a nondegenerate triangle. Since B u+c v+D w=0 and B,c,D>0,\nzero is in its interior. Completing the square shows grad F=P_K,\nthe Euclidean projection onto K. For completeness, the projection normal\ninequalities at two points imply\n||P_K(x)-P_K(y)||^2<=<P_K(x)-P_K(y),x-y>, so the projection is1-Lipschitz.\nThe unique-maximizer formula gives\n0<=F(x+z)-F(x)-<P_K(x),z><=<P_K(x+z)-P_K(x),z><=||z||^2,\nproving differentiability. F is convex as a supremum of affine functions.\nSince0 is interior to K, for every x!=0 sufficiently small epsilon>0\nhas epsilon x in K, whence F(x)>=epsilon(1-epsilon/2)||x||^2>0.\nThus0 is its unique minimizer and F(0)=0.\n\nThe actual iterates and gradients are\n\n x=(P u,u,-B u,D w,w), g=(u,u,v,w,w).                    (1)\n\nThe four GD updates follow from c v=-B u-D w and D=d+1.\nIt remains to check that each listed gradient is the actual projection.\nThe inner products are\n\n ||u||^2=1, <u,w>=||w||^2=t,\n <u,v>=-(B+D t)/c, <v,w>=-(B+D)t/c.\n\nAt x0 the residual x0-u=a u has nonpositive pairing with v-u and w-u;\nat x1 the residual is zero. At x2 the residual y2=-B u-v satisfies\n\n <w-v,y2>=[-B^2(c-1)+Kappa t]/c^2=0,\n <u-v,y2>=-B(1-t)(c-1)/c<0.\n\nThe first equality uses the expansion\nKappa=D(2B+D)+c(D-BD+B)-B c^2\n       =B^2(c-1)+(b+c+d)(D-B(c-1)).\nAt x3 the residual d w pairs to zero with u-w and to\n-d t[1+(B+D)/c]<=0 with v-w. At x4 the residual is zero.\nThese are all vertex comparisons and establish every projection normal.\n\nPut f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2. Then for every original ordered pair\n\n Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2\n      =<g_i-g_j,x_j-g_j><=0.\n\nThe optimum comparisons hold because0 belongs to K. In fact Q_(i,star)=0\nfor all i. There is one explicit globally legal function, with true initial\ndistance P to its unique minimizer, and no missing terminal comparison.\n\nFor the intrinsic energy\n\n V_h=2sum_(i=0)^3 h_i(f_i-f_4)+sum_(i=0)^3 h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2\n                                            -s(s+1)||g_4||^2,\n\nwhere s=A-1, summing the GD norm identities and using\nf_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2 gives\n\n V_h=||x0||^2-||x4||^2-2s f4-s(s+1)||g4||^2\n     =P^2-A^2 t.                                       (2)\n\nAny original full nonnegative interpolation/PSD certificate must therefore\nobey the following additional necessary inequality in this regime:\n\n P^2 D >= BC[P^2+2BP+BU].                               (3)\n\nIndeed A=P+U and\nA^2 B^2C-P^2 Kappa=U{BC[P^2+2BP+BU]-P^2D}, so (2)>=0\nis equivalent to (3). This is only a necessary condition for b,c>1 and\nD>BC; it is not a full characterization or a global capacity obstruction.\n\n## Fixed rational specialization\n\nOne proposed exact word, selected by rounding the geometry of saved\ndiscovery record71, is\n\n h=(1450,2269,1656,1345)/1000, sum h=168/25.              (4)\n\nThe single charged fixed-candidate operation is record99 in\nmax3_four_step_seed_discovery_raw/099.json. It contains every original\nrational Gram entry, function value and ordered Q. It gives exactly\n\n t=66024801/564216346, D-BC=189067/125000,\n V_h=-2741319415267/2821081730000<0.\n\nThe exact full trace-normalized energy is\n-504402772409128/1718090401421875. Thus (4) cannot admit the full\nprimitive certificate. No numerical PSD repair, clipping, solver rank\nassumption or scan is used in this proof.\n\nNevertheless, all four primitive single-cut tests hold strictly: their\nslacks (1+prefix)(1+tail)-A(h_j-1), in chronological order, are\n\n 699/250, 577/100000, 1200347/200000, 9279/2500.          (5)\n\nThe word also passes EVERY convex1-smooth scalar quadratic, not just the\nthree numerical screening curvatures. To show this exactly, for curvature\nz in[0,1] put p0=1, p_(i+1)=(1-z h_i)p_i. Direct substitution gives\n\n V_h(z)=sum_i z h_i(1+z-z h_i)p_i^2\n                            -s z[1+(s+1)z]p4^2=z^3 R(z).\n\nWriting R(z)=sum_(i=0)^7 r_i z^i, the exact coefficients r0..r7 are\n\n 293892535459/1000000000,\n -2565070268077021/1000000000000,\n 25501482292887241/2500000000000,\n -9335047522883723193/400000000000000,\n 4094864133735484813551/125000000000000000,\n -175057366829794377658881/6250000000000000000,\n 52516866551109429450429/3906250000000000000,\n -54411016852014358527117/19531250000000000000.\n\nHere is a finite exact positive-basis certificate for R. For l=0,1/4,1/2,3/4,\nexpand R(l+x/4)=sum_(k=0)^7 beta_(l,k) binom(7,k)x^k(1-x)^(7-k).\nThe rational coefficient formula is\n\n beta_(l,k)=sum_(j=0)^k [binom(k,j)/binom(7,j)] 4^(-j)\n                                sum_(i=j)^7 r_i binom(i,j)l^(i-j). (6)\n\nThe four rows of coefficients are explicitly:\n\n l=0:\n 293892535459/1000000000,\n 5663920724774979/28000000000000,\n 118466995993825981/840000000000000,\n 89350661850225432007/896000000000000000,\n 80179881684171436848551/1120000000000000000000,\n 7019131829546593320971719/134400000000000000000000,\n 1084787387282508694745631/28000000000000000000000,\n 9355453517805011570547413/320000000000000000000000.\n\n l=1/4:\n 9355453517805011570547413/320000000000000000000000,\n 22096679133334733204006651/1120000000000000000000000,\n 47709397590871145509381441/3360000000000000000000000,\n 6015731198047189848729817/560000000000000000000000,\n 1318803156277689465811/156250000000000000000,\n 716661139723887953927849/105000000000000000000000,\n 395098446318964178528277/70000000000000000000000,\n 23757859952262975297321/5000000000000000000000.\n\n l=1/2:\n 23757859952262975297321/5000000000000000000000,\n 270121632344399129796711/70000000000000000000000,\n 48818671114313258247593/15000000000000000000000,\n 24439962963628753248587/8750000000000000000000,\n 1364515762711357617321817/560000000000000000000000,\n 1030893661419515971816909/480000000000000000000000,\n 305805648670522563365657/160000000000000000000000,\n 548894926580824071855791/320000000000000000000000.\n\n l=3/4:\n 548894926580824071855791/320000000000000000000000,\n 121544638955150754245067/80000000000000000000000,\n 654595436858189642563771/480000000000000000000000,\n 346215797597421765010013/280000000000000000000000,\n 78984917611546674402131/70000000000000000000000,\n 7602563302701652626313/7500000000000000000000,\n 431841029282863888083/546875000000000000000,\n 176902547823562831/39062500000000000000.\n\nEvery displayed numerator and denominator is positive. The Bernstein\nbasis is nonnegative and sums to1 on[0,1], so R>0 on each of these four\nclosed subintervals. Thus V_h(z)>0 for every0<z<=1, and V_h(0)=0.\nAn arbitrary positive-semidefinite quadratic of smoothness at most1\ndecomposes into its scalar eigencoordinates; V is additive across these\ncoordinates. Hence the word passes the entire convex quadratic class.\n\nEquations(1)--(6) prove a concrete scope limitation: even strict primitive\nsingle-cut tests plus ALL scalar/multidimensional quadratics do not\nsuffice for the four-step full cone at exactly the required mass.\nThis says nothing about the existence of another four-step seed.\n\n## Additional adjacent-pair filter\n\nThe same construction applies to any nonnegative word with two adjacent\nsteps b>1,c>1. Let a be the sum of all preceding steps and d the sum of\nall following steps. Use initial point(a+1)u, retain gradient u throughout\nthe preceding steps, and retain w throughout the following steps.\nAll intermediate residuals on those blocks are nonnegative multiples of\nu or w and satisfy the already displayed normals. Thus (3) is a valid\nnecessary condition at each adjacent pair whenever D>BC, without\nasserting that the prefix or suffix is primitive. In particular it may\nbe applied separately at j=0,1,2 of the original four-step word.\n\nIf a strict full-cone seed is later found, accepted04219554e41d9750 supplies\nthe conditional asymptotic lift. No such seed is supplied here, and this\nnew obstruction has not been submitted to the verifier.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "research/max3_c80_triangle_obstruction.md", "raw": "research/max3_four_step_seed_discovery_raw/099.json", "source": "research/max3_c80_exact_triangle.py", "target_claim": "All primitive single-cut tests plus the entire convex quadratic class suffice for the full four-step primitive cone.", "status": "refuted_by_complete_unverified_construction", "scope": "A specific rational word and a general adjacent-pair necessary condition; not global seed nonexistence", "fact_ids": ["04219554e41d9750"], "finding_ids": ["6695d6fd665245ef"], "new_verifier_calls": 0}, "glossary": {"Kappa": "B^2 C+U(D-BC) in the C80 triangle construction, with B=b-1,C=c-1,D=d+1,U=b+c+d.", "t_triangle": "Squared norm of w, t=B^2 C/Kappa, denoted t locally in C80.", "K_triangle": "conv(u,v,w), the nondegenerate planar triangle containing zero in its interior, denoted K locally in C80.", "R_curve": "Degree7 polynomial V_h(z)/z^3 for the fixed rational four-step counterexample, denoted R locally.", "beta_Bernstein": "Exact Bernstein coefficients from formula(6) of the attached proof."}}
{"id": "2479531e6898424b", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-27T03:30:50.282028+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "Exact four-step witness: h=(1,217/100,41/20,3/2),sum=168/25 passes all four cut tests, full-curvature product test, suffix-pair test and both cut/quadratic mixtures, but the actual capped quadratic started at2 gives V=4-(104799/50000)^2<0. Thus those tests alone cannot decide the full seed; the newly derived clipped-tail inequality catches this chronological return.", "evidence": "Consider the precise proposed sufficiency test consisting of all four cut inequalities C(h_j-1)_+<=(1+sum_(i<j)h_i)(1+sum_(i>j)h_i), the full-curvature quadratic test C|product_i(1-h_i)|<=1, the suffix pair test C|(1-h_2)(1-h_3)|<=1+h_0+h_1, and the cut/quadratic repairs at j=1,2 described below. This finite necessary-condition list is NOT sufficient for the original full primitive certificate.\n\nTake h=(1,217/100,41/20,3/2), s=168/25, C=193/25. All cuts hold: at j=0 the left side is0; at j=1 the sides are22581/2500 and91/10; at j=2 they are4053/500 and417/40; at j=3 they are193/50 and311/50. The suffix test has sides4053/1000 and417/100. The full-curvature product vanishes since h_0=1. For the repaired cuts set eta=(h_j-1)/(1+sum_(i>j)h_i), z_i=product_(t<i)(1-h_t), q=-eta z_j, beta=max(0,max_(i<=j)q(z_i-q)); their necessary expression is beta*((1+sum_(i<j)h_i)^2-C^2 eta^2)+(1+eta)eta*(1-C^2q^2)>=0. Here z_j=q=beta=0 at both cuts and the expression is positive. No certificate multipliers are asserted.\n\nNevertheless define f(x)=x^2/2 for x<=1 and f(x)=x-1/2 for x>=1. Its derivative min(x,1) is continuous, nondecreasing,1-Lipschitz, and vanishes at the minimizer0. From x_0=2 the exact GD positions are (2,1,-117/100,2457/2000,-543/2000), with gradients (1,1,-117/100,1,-543/2000). Each sampled value satisfies f_i=x_i g_i-g_i^2/2, and the last x_4=g_4. Telescoping the GD squared norms therefore gives V=2sum_(i<4)h_i(f_i-f_4)+sum_(i<4)h_i(1-h_i)g_i^2-s(s+1)g_4^2=x_0^2-C^2g_4^2=4-(104799/50000)^2<0. {"id": "618a43a2e5b13506", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-27T03:48:10.920782+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "C81 exact analytical rejection of saved record100: h=(8/5,2,471/250,309/250), sum168/25, has a rational planar triangle-projection witness with V=-(11908*73398)/(625*559^2)<0. UNVERIFIED joint construction; no new model evaluation or word proposal.", "evidence": "# C81: a four-step triangle with an interior-edge query\n\nStatus: complete new joint UNVERIFIED construction. No verifier call is\nallocated. This is analytical continuation, cumulative round19/19, of\nthe CLOSED C79 discovery allocation. No optimization, candidate sweep,\nor exact numerical replay is performed here. The only fixed schedule\nspecialized below is the already saved record100.\n\nThe accepted fact04219554e41d9750, Section10, supplies the original\nprimitive energy and its full certificate identity. All new geometry and\nenergy calculations below are proved directly rather than imported from\nthe unverified C79/C80 drafts.\n\n## 1. General actual function and four-step trajectory\n\nLet h=(a,b,c,d), with a>1, b>1, c>1, d>=0. Define\n\n B=b-1, C=c-1, D=d+1, s=a+b+c+d, A=1+s.\n\nChoose u>0 and z>0 satisfying\n\n u>=B,\n z^2 <= (u+1)((a-1)u-1),\n z^2 >= C d(D-BC)/D^2.                                  (1)\n\nThe second inequality implies (a-1)u-1>0. The third imposes\nno additional restriction if its right side is negative. Put\n\n p=(u,0), q=(-1,z), r=(-BC/D,0), K=conv{p,q,r},\n F(x)=max_(g in K) {<x,g>-||g||^2/2}.                      (2)\n\nThis nondegenerate compact triangle contains0 in the relative interior\nof its horizontal base. Completing the square shows that the unique\nmaximizer in (2) is the Euclidean projection P_K(x).\n\nFor completeness, if p_x=P_K(x), p_y=P_K(y), the two projection\nnormal inequalities give\n\n ||p_x-p_y||^2 <= <p_x-p_y,x-y>.\n\nHence P_K is1-Lipschitz. The maximizing formula gives\n\n 0<=F(x+t)-F(x)-<p_x,t>\n       <=<P_K(x+t)-p_x,t><=||t||^2,\n\nso F is differentiable with gradient P_K; it is convex as a supremum\nof affine functions. Since0 belongs to K, F>=0 and F(0)=0. Moreover\nP_K(x)=0 exactly when x is in the normal cone of K at0. Pairing x\nwith both signs of the horizontal base forces its first coordinate\nto vanish; pairing it with q then forces its second coordinate<=0.\nThus argmin F={(0,t):t<=0}.\n\nThe proposed ORIGINAL iterates and gradients are\n\n x0=(a u-1,z),       g0=(u,0),\n x1=(-1,z),         g1=(-1,z),\n x2=(B,-Bz),        g2=(B,0),\n x3=(-BC,-Bz),      g3=(-BC/D,0),\n x4=(-BC/D,-Bz),    g4=(-BC/D,0).                        (3)\n\nThe four equations x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i follow by subtraction.\nThe initial point has positive second coordinate, so its closest\nminimizer is0 and its squared distance is(a u-1)^2+z^2.\n\nWe check every required projection normal, including both terminal\norientations implicitly through the single actual function.\n\nAt x0, the residual y0=x0-g0=((a-1)u-1,z) has\n\n <q-p,y0>=z^2-(u+1)((a-1)u-1)<=0,\n <r-p,y0>=(-BC/D-u)((a-1)u-1)<0.\n\nAt x1 the residual is0. At x2, g2 is on the base because\n-BC/D<0<B<=u, and y2=x2-g2=(0,-Bz) has zero pairing with\nboth base directions and pairing-Bz^2<0 with q-g2.\n\nAt x3 the residual is y3=(-BCd/D,-Bz). Its base comparison\nwith p-r is nonpositive, and\n\n <q-r,y3>=B{C d(D-BC)/D^2-z^2}<=0.\n\nAt x4 the residual y4=(0,-Bz) has zero pairing with the base\nand pairing-Bz^2<0 with q-r. These are all vertices of K,\nso (3) gives the actual gradient of the same F at every query.\n\nFor all five indices define f_i=F(x_i)=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2.\nEvery original ordered interpolation residual has the exact form\n\n Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2\n     =<g_i-g_j,x_j-g_j><=0.                              (4)\n\nThis follows from the proved projection normals. The optimum sample0\nalso obeys all ordered comparisons. Thus no guessed rank or deleted\ngradient is needed to certify legality.\n\n## 2. Exact intrinsic energy and a necessary family of inequalities\n\nUse the energy from accepted04219554e41d9750, Section10:\n\n V_h=2sum_(i=0)^3 h_i(f_i-f4)\n       +sum_(i=0)^3 h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-s(s+1)||g4||^2.\n\nThe identity\n\n ||x_i||^2-||x_(i+1)||^2\n     =2h_i f_i+h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2\n\nfollows from (3) and f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2.\nSumming it gives\n\n V_h=||x0||^2-||x4||^2-2s f4-s(s+1)||g4||^2.\n\nHere f4=||g4||^2/2, ||x4||^2=(BC/D)^2+B^2z^2, and therefore\n\n V_h=(a u-1)^2+(1-B^2)z^2-A^2(BC/D)^2.                 (5)\n\nConsequently the original full primitive cone must satisfy\n\n (a u-1)^2+(1-B^2)z^2 >= A^2(BC/D)^2                  (6)\n\nfor EVERY u,z obeying(1). Indeed its accepted exact identity is\nV_h+sum Lambda_ij Q_ij=<M,Gram(g)>, where Lambda_ij>=0,\nthe flow holds, and M is PSD in all five original gradients.\nThe right side is nonnegative and every Q_ij<=0, so it implies V_h>=0.\nA negative value in(5) contradicts this identity directly.\n\nThis is a necessary family, not a sufficient condition. The imposed\na,b,c>1 is a scope assumption of the witness and is NOT a lossless\nrestriction of the full four-step search domain.\n\n## 3. Exact rejection of saved discovery record100\n\nKeep the already proposed schedule\n\n h=(8/5,2,471/250,309/250),   s=168/25,   A=193/25.\n\nThus B=1,C=221/250,D=559/250. Choose u=2,z=3/5.\nThe upper inequality in(1) is\n\n z^2=9/25 <=3/5=(u+1)((a-1)u-1).\n\nFor its lower inequality,\n\n C d(D-C)/D^2\n    =(221*309*338)/(250*559^2) < 9/25,\n\nbecause 221*309*338=23081682 <28123290=90*559^2.\nAll numbers in(2)--(4) are therefore rational. Explicitly,\n\n K=conv{(2,0),(-1,3/5),(-221/559,0)},\n\n x0=(11/5,3/5),   g0=(2,0),\n x1=(-1,3/5),     g1=(-1,3/5),\n x2=(1,-3/5),     g2=(1,0),\n x3=(-221/250,-3/5), g3=(-221/559,0),\n x4=(-221/559,-3/5), g4=(-221/559,0).\n\nThe exact values, if desired independently of(4), are\n\n f0=12/5, f1=17/25, f2=1/2,\n f3=(d+1/2)(221/559)^2, f4=(221/559)^2/2.\n\nEquation(5), with B=1, gives\n\n V_h=(11/5)^2-(193*221/(25*559))^2\n    =-(11908*73398)/(625*559^2)<0.                        (7)\n\nThe comparison is exact:193*221=42653>30745=55*559.\nThe initial closest-minimizer distance squared is26/5. The terminal\ngradient/value and the displayed negative energy concern the intrinsic\nprimitive criterion. They do not assert an objective minimax theorem.\n\nThis rejects the precise candidate left in the round18 handoff.\nThe handoff's numerical rank/value observations were only motivation;\nno numerical Gram, tolerance, PSD repair or approximate identity is a\npremise of this proof. No new schedule has been proposed or evaluated.\n\n## 4. Exact one-parameter elimination when b=2\n\nFor1<a<=2, b=2, c>1 and d>0 with D>C, put\n\n L_81=C d(D-C)/D^2>0,\n u_81=[2-a+sqrt(a^2+4(a-1)L_81)]/[2(a-1)].\n\nThe function H(u)=(u+1)((a-1)u-1) is strictly increasing for\nu>=1/(a-1); its derivative there is at least a>0. Its positive\nroot for H(u)=L_81 is exactly u_81. Because L_81>0,\nu_81>1/(a-1)>=1. Hence u=u_81,z=sqrt(L_81) is allowed in(1).\nEvery full primitive certificate in this regime therefore forces\n\n a u_81-1 >= A C/D.                                    (8)\n\nThe left side is positive, so(8) is equivalent to(6) in this\nparticular specialization. This explicitly eliminates the witness\nparameters; it is not a complete classification of b=2 schedules,\nlet alone of all four-step schedules.\n\n## 5. Retrieval and correctness boundary\n\nBefore proving, fact_search found the accepted conditional bridge and\nthe general interpolation fact1252b8823bb5a290, but no accepted version\nof(1)--(8). Shared-memory search found C80 and older projection-lift\ndrafts, whose claims are not used as facts here.\n\nThe required Matlas query returned three unrelated descent results;\nnone is used. The existing exact local copy of Taylor--Hendrickx--Glineur,\narXiv1502.05666, Sections2.4--2.5 and the proof of Theorem4, was reread\nfor the conjugate construction. All needed projection/differentiability\nclaims are proved above, so this candidate imports no external theorem.\n\nThe original four-step existence question and original asymptotic\ntarget remain OPEN. Accepted04219554e41d9750 gives the conditional lift\nif a different actual full seed is found. {"id": "b1592acfdb474277", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-27T04:53:54.352974+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "NEW exact necessary-system survivor: h(t)=(7/5,9/4,33/25+t,7/4), 0<=t<=10^-12, passes EVERY previous max2 scalar cut/quadratic/clipped/triangle/backward-lift test, the entire C82 family, and ALL C83 curvatures 0<k<=1. This refutes global coverage by the CURRENT witness system, not full-seed infeasibility; no Lambda/M certificate is supplied.", "evidence": "# Exact survivor of the complete current scalar necessary-test system\n\nStatus: self-contained joint proof DRAFT, not a verifier-accepted fact.\nThis file establishes a limitation of a specified collection of necessary\nconditions. It does NOT supply nonnegative multipliers or a full PSD seed.\nNo optimizer, numerical parameter search, grid, verifier, or installation\nwas used. The two tiny exact rational calculations expand the fixed\npolynomial identities printed below; they do not search for a schedule.\n\n## 1. Exact schedule and variable-capacity family\n\nFor 0<=t<=1/100 define\n\n h(t)=(7/5,9/4,33/25+t,7/4),\n a=h0-1=2/5, b=h1-1=5/4, c=h2-1=8/25+t, d=h3-1=3/4,\n C=1+sum h=193/25+t,\n P=2+a=12/5, Q=3+a+b=93/20,\n D=2+d=11/4, H=3+c+d=407/100+t.\n\nAll previous max2 scalar tests, and the complete C82 triangle family,\nare STRICTLY satisfied throughout 0<=t<=1/100. In addition, every\nquadratic-curvature test and every C83 cut-repair test is STRICTLY\nsatisfied for every 0<k<=1 throughout 0<=t<=10^(-12).\n\nThus there is an exact survivor at total sum168/25 and a concrete\ninterval of survivors with strictly larger sums. In particular an\nattempt to prove emptiness from these scalar tests cannot succeed.\nThis is not a restriction or a reduction of the original feasible set\nonto a fixed-capacity slice; it is a constructed family inside the\nnecessary-test region.\n\nThe original full interface remains twenty Lambda_ij>=0, i!=j in0..4,\nLambda_ii=0, L=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda, T_ii=1,T_it=h_t for t<i,\nL^T e=(-2h0,-2h1,-2h2,-2h3,2sum h)^T, and\n\n diag(h0(2-h0),...,h3(2-h3),-s(s+2)) + (LT+T^T L^T)/2 >=0,\n s=sum h.\n\nNo Lambda or matrix remainder is claimed in this file. Accepted\n04219554e41d9750 gives the conditional asymptotic implication only\nif an actual full certificate is subsequently constructed.\n\n## 2. Compact box, every cut, and the elementary nonlinear tests\n\nOn our interval, 193/25<=C<=773/100<87/10, 8/25<=c<=33/100,\nand all four shifts are positive. The endpoint bounds a,d<77/97 and\nmiddle bounds b,c<1552559/1081453 hold: b=5/4 gives\n4*1552559-5*1081453=802971>0, and c< b. Thus the strict step box\nfrom the earlier joint draft is satisfied.\n\nThe four cut inequalities, in order, are\n\n Ca <= C-1-a,\n Cb <= P H,\n Cc <= Q D,\n Cd <= C-1-d.                                           (1)\n\nThe first and fourth have margins respectively\n(3/5)C-7/5>0 and C/4-7/4>0. The second has margin\n\n PH-Cb=59/500+(23/20)t>0.                                (2)\n\nFor the third, Cc<=25509/10000<64/25, whereas QD=1023/80>12.\nThese bounds follow by multiplying the upper endpoints of C and c.\nEvery prefix/reversed-prefix cut recurrence is merely a rearrangement\nof (1), so no extra cut has been dropped. The concavity-derived endpoint\nsum condition follows from the two middle cuts: if E=4+a+d, adding\nb<=P(C-P)/(C+P), c<=D(C-D)/(C+D), and using the negative second\nderivative of z(C-z)/(C+z), gives E>=2C(C-1)/(3C+1).\nThe pointwise endpoint and middle cut bounds follow directly from (1).\n\nThe actual unit-curvature product test, suffix product test, and\nchronological clipped-tail test are, respectively,\n\n C a b c d<=1,   C c d<=Q,\n C |bc-(1+d)min(bc,1)|<=P.                              (3)\n\nHere ab=1/2, bc<=33/80<1, and Cc<64/25. Consequently their left\nsides are less than\n\n (3/8)(64/25)=24/25<1,\n (3/4)(64/25)=48/25<Q,\n (15/16)(64/25)=12/5=P.\n\nAll comparisons are strict because 25509<25600.\nAt t=0 the exact full product is579/625 and the exact clipped\nleft side is579/250, whose gap from P is21/250.\n\nThe scalar mixed first-prefix test has u=7/5 and eta=b/H. Its expression\nis\n\n u^2+u-1+u(2-u^2)eta-a C^2 eta^2\n   =59/25+(7/125)eta-(2/5)(C eta)^2.\n\nBy (1), C eta<P=12/5. Therefore this expression exceeds7/125>0.\nIts domain a eta<1 also follows since a eta<ab/4=1/8.\n\nFor the two unit-curvature cut repairs, the base energy is\nV0=(1+prefix sum)^2-C^2 eta^2>0 by (1). The auxiliary terminal\nvalue q is q=1/(2H) at cut1, and q=-c/(2D) at cut2. Hence\n\n C|q|=C/(2H)<1 at cut1, since 2H-C=C-73/10>0,\n C|q|=Cc/(2D)<(64/25)/(11/2)<1 at cut2.\n\nThe auxiliary energy1-C^2 q^2 is strictly positive. Thus every repair\nexpression beta V0+eta(1+eta)(1-C^2 q^2) is positive for beta>=0,\nincluding the beta=0 case. No denominator with an unproved sign is used.\n\n## 3. All three adjacent triangles and the first backward lift\n\nThe adjacent-triangle tests, with the selected consecutive shifts\nB,E, preceding capacity P0 and following capacity D0, read\n\n B E [1+B(P0+C)/P0^2] <= D0.                            (4)\n\nFor the first pair, its left side is\nab[1+a(1+C)] < (1/2)[1+(2/5)*9]=23/10<H.\nFor the middle pair, bc<=33/80, P+C<11, and P^2>5. Therefore its\nleft side is less than\n\n (33/80)[1+(5/4)*11/5]=99/64<11/4=D.\n\nFor the last pair, cd<=99/400, Q+C<13 and Q^2>21. Its left side\nis less than\n\n (99/400)[1+(33/100)*13/21]\n <(99/400)(5/4)=99/320<1.\n\nThe strengthened middle triangle has the additional nonnegative\nparameter max((1+a)(a^2+2a-1)/a,0). In this family\n\n a^2+2a-1=-1/25,\n\nso this parameter equals zero and the strengthened test is exactly\nthe already strict middle inequality. This is an actual branch check,\nnot deletion of an active max term.\n\n## 4. Both last-pair lifts and the earliest-normal guard\n\nDefine the last-triangle quantities\n\n eta=c/D,\n K=c^2 d+(c+d+2)(1-cd)=c+d+2-cd(d+2),\n r=c^2 d/K,\n Hpre=2+a+b=73/20, Apre=1+a+b=53/20,\n Z=(c+r)/(1+d),\n qlate=(1+(1-b)eta)/b,\n qearly=[eta+(Apre eta-1)Z]/Apre,\n Jpre=1-2Hpre eta+Hpre^2 max(qlate,qearly).\n\nThe required inequalities are C^2 r<=min(Jpre,Q^2) and\n\n C^2 max(eta^2,r)<=Jquad,\n Jquad=[(1-b eta)Q^2+(1+eta)/(a^2 b)]/(1+b).             (5)\n\nAll denominators are positive: eta<3/25, cd<1/4, c+d+2>3, so\nK>3-(1/4)(11/4)=37/16>2. Also\n\n r < ((33/100)^2*(3/4))/2 <1/20,\n eta^2<9/625<1/20, C^2<64.\n\nThus every left side in (5) and C^2 r is less than16/5.\nOn the right, qlate>(1-3/100)/(5/4)=97/125>3/4,\nHpre>7/2,Hpre<4, and eta<3/25. Therefore\n\n Jpre >=1-2Hpre eta+Hpre^2 qlate\n       >1-24/25+147/16>9.\n\nAlso Q^2>21. For Jquad use b eta<3/20, a^2 b=1/5 and1+b=9/4:\n\n Jquad >[(17/20)*21+5]/(9/4)=457/45>10.\n\nThese bounds already retain the full max in Jpre. In fact the earliest\nnormal is inactive here for a proved reason: Apre eta<(53/20)(3/25)<1.\nSince Z>0, the explicit formula gives\nqearly<eta/Apre<eta<3/25<3/4<qlate. No shortcut based only on\ncd<1 or b eta<1 has been made.\nThe other max is also correctly determined: d=3/4>sqrt(2)-1, so\nr>eta^2. This follows from\n\nd(d+2)^2-K=(c+d+2)(d(d+2)-1)>0.\n\nAt t=0, K=241/100,r=192/6025,eta=32/275,\nqlate=1068/1375, and Jquad=529303/49500.\n\n## 5. The entire C82 interior-edge triangle family survives\n\nFor clarity, the interface is rederived here in this family's notation.\nTake U>0,z>0, with\n\n U>=b,\n z^2 <= (U+1)(a U-1),\n z^2 >= c(1+d)(D-bc)/D^2.                              (6)\n\nUse vertices p=(U,0), q=(-1,z), w=(-bc/D,0), and the convex\nfunction F(x)=max_{v in conv(p,q,w)}(<x,v>-||v||^2/2).\nIts gradient is projection onto that triangle. The original jets are\n\n x0=((1+a)U-1,z), g0=(U,0),\n x1=(-1,z), g1=(-1,z),\n x2=(b,-bz), g2=(b,0),\n x3=(-bc,-bz), g3=(-bc/D,0),\n x4=(-bc/D,-bz), g4=(-bc/D,0).\n\nThe initial normal comparison with q is z^2-(U+1)(aU-1)<=0;\nthe other initial comparison is nonpositive since aU-1>=0.\nAt1 the normal is0. At2 its normal is(0,-bz), and U>=b places\ng2 on the base. At3 the comparison with q is\nb[c(1+d)(D-bc)/D^2-z^2]<=0, and the base comparison is nonpositive.\nAt4 the normal is again vertical downward. These checks include every\ntriangle vertex and the optimum0 in its base, hence all original\ninterpolation pairs. The four GD identities follow by subtraction.\nSaturated values f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2 telescope, giving energy\n\n E(U,z)=((1+a)U-1)^2+(1-b^2)z^2-C^2(bc/D)^2.           (7)\n\nThe complete parameter-eliminated C82 test is the infimum of (7)\nover (6), including zero-height limits. We bound the whole domain,\nso no unproved formula for that infimum is needed.\nSince a=2/5, (6) forces U>=5/2. Also 1-b^2=-9/16<0, so\nE(U,z)>=q(U), where\n\n q(U)=((7/5)U-1)^2-(9/16)(U+1)((2/5)U-1)-C^2(bc/D)^2.\n\nIts quadratic coefficient is347/200>0 and\nq'(5/2)=(7/5)(71/16)>0. Thus q is strictly increasing for U>=5/2.\nUsing Cc<64/25 and b/D=5/11 gives\n\n E(U,z)>=q(5/2)=25/4-(5 Cc/11)^2\n              >25/4-25/16=75/16>0.\n\nThis includes all branches, boundary limits and the exact infimum.\nIt proves survival of C82, not sufficiency of any scalar test.\n\n## 6. All curvatures in the full quadratic and C83 families\n\nThis section first fixes t=0. For 0<k<=1 and j in{0,1,2,3}, put\n\n R_j=sum_(i>j)h_i, H_j=sum_(i<=j)h_i, E_j=1+k R_j,\n p_0=1, p_i=product_(v<i)(1-k h_v),\n q_j=k p_(j+1)/E_j.\n\nThe auxiliary trajectory follows the curvature-k quadratic through\nquery j, and subsequently has constant gradient q_j, ending at\nx4=q_j/k. Its values before or at j are k p_i^2/2 and afterwards\nq_j^2/(2k)+q_j^2 sum_(v>=i)h_v. This array is only an auxiliary\narray; it need not be legal before a repair. Direct substitution in\n\n V=2sum_(i<4)h_i(f_i-f4)+sum_(i<4)h_i(1-h_i)g_i^2\n   -s(s+1)g4^2\n\ngives\n\n V_j(k)=k sum_(i<=j)h_i[1+k(1-h_i)]p_i^2\n        -H_j[1/k+s+R_j+1]q_j^2\n       =k P_j(k)/E_j^2,\n P_j=E_j^2 sum_(i<=j)h_i[1+k(1-h_i)]p_i^2\n       -H_j[1+k(s+R_j+1)]p_(j+1)^2.                   (8)\n\nFor the substitution, the suffix values give\n2sum_(i>j)h_i sum_(v>=i)h_v=R_j^2+sum_(i>j)h_i^2;\ncombining with the suffix gradient diagonals leaves R_j(R_j+1)q_j^2.\nSubtracting s(s+1)q_j^2 and the prefix terminal values yields (8).\nIn particular j=3 is the actual quadratic F(x)=k x^2/2 for all queries.\n\nThe constant and linear coefficients of P_j vanish for every schedule.\nThe constant is H_j-H_j. Write T_j=sum_(i<=j)h_i sum_(v<i)h_v;\nthen 2T_j=H_j^2-sum_(i<=j)h_i^2. The coefficient of k in the prefix\nsum is H_j-H_j^2. Hence the coefficient of k in P_j is\n2R_j H_j+H_j-H_j^2-H_j(s+R_j+1-2H_j)=0,\nsince s=H_j+R_j. Thus P_j=k^2 Q_j, where Q_j is a polynomial.\n\nFor j=0, Q_0 is linear and its endpoint values are\n\n Q_0(0)=256368/3125, Q_0(1)=475104/15625,\n\nboth positive. For j=1,2,3, all coefficients in the exact Bernstein\nexpansions on [0,1/2] and [1/2,1] are positive, as printed below.\nSince the Bernstein basis polynomials are nonnegative and sum to1,\nQ_j(k)>0 throughout [0,1], and V_j(k)>0 throughout 0<k<=1.\n\nFor the C83 repair at a selected cut j=0,1,2, its base cut energy is\nV0_j=(1+sum_(i<j)h_i)^2-C^2 eta_j^2>0, eta_j=(h_j-1)/(1+R_j).\nThe entire curvature test has form\n\n beta_j(k) V0_j + eta_j(1+eta_j) V_j(k)>=0,\n\nwhere beta_j(k) is the maximum of0 and the finite residual-repair\nratios. In particular beta_j(k)>=0, including all changing maximizer\nbranches and beta=0. Both energies have just been proved positive,\nso the expression is strictly positive for every curvature and every\nbranch. No active-residual formula or denominator sign is discarded.\nFor j=3 the auxiliary is already the full legal quadratic and its\npositive energy has been checked directly. The curvature-zero limit\nis obtained by continuity; no division by k=0 is asserted.\n\n## 7. Exact polynomial identity certificate\n\nFor a degree m polynomial Q(k)=sum_i A_i k^i, its Bernstein coefficients\non [0,1] are B_v=sum_(i<=v) A_i binom(v,i)/binom(m,i).\nThis identity follows by expanding k^i in the degree-m Bernstein basis.\nTo obtain the halves, repeatedly replace consecutive coefficients by\ntheir average. The first entries down this triangular array give the\ncoefficients on [0,1/2], and the last entries in reverse row order give\nthose on [1/2,1]. This follows from splitting each degree-m Bernstein\nbasis at its midpoint, or directly from the binomial theorem.\n\nThe following exact integer arrays specify Q and the two expansions.\nEach array is divided by the stated positive denominator. These are\nsmall, fixed polynomial identities, checked with rational arithmetic;\nno sample of k or optimization result is used as a premise.\n\nj=1; power denominator=250000; power numerators=[50793540, -177528981, 237836151, -110684385]\nleft: denominator=2000000; numerators=[406348320, 169643012, 91495138, 61220313]; all positive=True\nright: denominator=2000000; numerators=[61220313, 30945488, 48543712, 3330600]; all positive=True\nj=2; power denominator=2500000000; power numerators=[633255870000, -3594456911400, 8982042049995, -11659202708349, 7719844643973, -2066390991819]\nleft: denominator=400000000000; numerators=[101320939200000, 43809628617600, 22226486235180, 13253106636042, 9010773691434, 6728505601455]; all positive=True\nright: denominator=400000000000; numerators=[6728505601455, 4446237511476, 4124034276126, 2990914095504, 3383629498560, 2414712384000]; all positive=True\nj=3; power denominator=2500000000; power numerators=[738069932500, -6435214284525, 25550374460520, -58327870129494, 81650300956812, -69585624017973, 33277390306776, -6867072767016]\nleft: denominator=2800000000000; numerators=[826638324400000, 311821181638000, 137675698349600, 70890394016824, 41454390035320, 26681369684405, 18427013864050, 13413025887415]; all positive=True\nright: denominator=2800000000000; numerators=[13413025887415, 8399037910780, 6625417777865, 4813868801830, 4552116709024, 2617934431520, 4409755955200, 396992512000]; all positive=True\n\n## 8. A concrete interval above the threshold also survives all curvatures\n\nEvery Bernstein coefficient in Section7 and each endpoint of Q_0\nexceeds1/10. This is an exact comparison of the displayed integers.\nWe retain the same degree for Q_j under variation of t.\n\nFor a polynomial A(k), let ||A||_1 be the sum of the absolute values\nof its coefficients. This norm is submultiplicative by convolution.\nThroughout 0<=t<=1/100, all h_i<3, s<7,R_j<7,H_j<7.\nOnly h2 depends on t, with derivative1. Thus for i<=4,\n\n ||p_i||_1<=256, ||partial_t p_i||_1<=64,\n ||p_i^2||_1<=65536, ||partial_t(p_i^2)||_1<=32768.\n\nIn the prefix sum Z_j=sum_(i<=j)h_i[1+k(1-h_i)]p_i^2,\ni<=3, so ||p_i^2||_1<=4096. The linear coefficient polynomial\nhas norm h_i^2<9, and its derivative has norm at most6. Therefore\n\n ||Z_j||_1<=147456,\n ||partial_t Z_j||_1<=4(6*4096+9*32768)=1277952.\n\nAlso ||E_j||_1<=8, ||partial_t E_j||_1<=1,\n||1+k(s+R_j+1)||_1<=16, and its derivative norm is at most2.\nDifferentiating (8), and using H_j<=7 and partial_t H_j<=1, yields\n\n ||partial_t P_j||_1\n <=16*147456+64*1277952+30*65536+112*32768\n =89784320 <10^8.\n\nDivision by k^2 just deletes the two identically zero coefficients,\nso the same bound holds for partial_t Q_j. Each Bernstein coefficient\nhas derivative magnitude bounded by this norm, because\n0<=binom(v,i)/binom(m,i)<=1; midpoint subdivision takes convex\ncombinations and preserves the bound. Consequently for\n0<=t<=10^(-12), every displayed positive coefficient changes by\nless than10^(-4). It remains greater than1/10-1/10000>0.\nThis proves the all-curvature assertions on that exact interval.\n\nSections2--5 already proved every other scalar test throughout the\nlarger interval 0<=t<=1/100. Thus, for example, the exact rational word\n\n (7/5,9/4,33/25+10^(-12),7/4)\n\nhas sum168/25+10^(-12) and passes the ENTIRE specified scalar system,\nincluding the continuum of curvatures and all C82 parameters.\n\n## 9. Exact failed implication and what remains open\n\nThe failed implication is:\n\n 'All the cut, quadratic, clipped-tail, adjacent-triangle, backward-lift,\n repaired-last-triangle, C82 and C83 inequalities together exclude every\n four-step schedule with sum at least168/25.'\n\nThe displayed rational family disproves that implication. It does not\nprove existence of full Lambda/M certificates, and no converse from\nthese witnesses is supplied. Even an explicit full-cone obstruction for\nthis family would not eliminate all other schedules.\n\nAll cut slacks are genuinely positive. At t=0 the critical second cut\nhas slack59/500 and the last cut has slack9/50. Neither generic\ncut saturation nor an automatic tail restriction is a legal inference.\nThe remaining exact constructive question is the original full\n20-multiplier/five-gradient feasibility problem for an explicit word;\nthe remaining global exclusion question needs a witness or coefficient\nargument beyond the now-refuted scalar coverage claim.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"plan_id": "max2_global_endpoint_slack_decision", "target_claim": "The simultaneous current scalar necessary tests exclude every four-step word with sum>=168/25.", "status": "refuted_by_exact_necessary_system_survivor", "artifact": "workers/max2/research/four_step_seed_necessary_system_survivor.md", "sha256": "548d49e086d2f6cf6ac9ac08d5927ce1b1966dd48992cf9a084cc33db616b16d", "scope": "Necessary-condition system only; full seed feasibility and original target remain open.", "related_findings": ["81720922d2d132d7", "749c14575c110748", "a7c38e9640fbd13c", "d0a11afae2401c50", "6d992d34120a0c16", "32380fa0da39d710"], "facts": ["04219554e41d9750", "1252b8823bb5a290"], "new_optimization": 0, "new_fact_submit": 0, "fixed_exact_polynomial_identity_checks": 2}, "glossary": {"h_surv": "Four-step exact word h(t)=(7/5,9/4,33/25+t,7/4).", "t_surv": "Exact parameter in[0,10^-12]; Sections2--5 permit the larger[0,1/100].", "C_surv": "1+sum h_surv=193/25+t_surv.", "Q_j_surv": "Polynomial P_j(k)/k^2 in Section6; P_j is defined explicitly by the curvature-prefix/constant-tail energy.", "V_j_surv": "Auxiliary primitive energy k^3 Q_j_surv(k)/(1+k R_j)^2, derived in Section6.", "R_j_surv": "Sum of schedule steps strictly after selected cut j.", "H_j_surv": "Sum of schedule steps through selected cut j.", "k_surv": "Quadratic curvature in(0,1]."}}

A missing partial-tail clipped-return test rejects this entire family\n\nThe previous sections prove survival of the PRE-EXISTING scalar system.\nThey do not imply a seed, and the following new actual function now\nproves that every displayed word is INFEASIBLE in the full original cone.\nThe original global decision remains open.\n\nHere is a new necessary inequality for every four-step full seed with\nh=(1+a,1+b,1+c,1+d), all a,b,c,d>0:\n\n                 C b d <= (2+a)(2+c).                 (9)\n\nNo ordering, fixed capacity or cut saturation is assumed. Define\nalpha=b/(2+c)>0 and the scalar function\n\n f(x)=x-1/2 for x>=1,\n f(x)=x^2/2 for -alpha<=x<=1,\n f(x)=-alpha*x-alpha^2/2 for x<=-alpha.\n\nIts derivative clamp(x,[-alpha,1]) is continuous, nondecreasing and\n1-Lipschitz, so f is one globally convex differentiable1-smooth\nfunction with unique minimizer0. Start at x0=P=2+a. The first four\nqueries and gradients are exactly\n\n x0=P,      g0=1,\n x1=1,      g1=1,\n x2=-b,     g2=-alpha,\n x3=-alpha, g3=-alpha.\n\nIndeed x2=-(2+c)alpha and the next step has length1+c.\nThe last point is x4=d*alpha>0, with g4=min(d*alpha,1).\nEvery sample of this function satisfies f_i=x_i*g_i-g_i^2/2.\nTelescoping the actual GD recursion therefore gives the exact primitive\nenergy V=x0^2-(x4+s*g4)^2, s=C-1.\n\nIf d*alpha<=1, then x4=g4 and V=P^2-C^2 d^2 alpha^2.\nIf d*alpha>1, then g4=1 and x4+s>C>P, giving V<0 directly.\nThus a full original nonnegative flow/PSD certificate necessarily\nforces (9). Conversely, if (9) is violated, this exact function has\nnegative V in either case. Accepted04219554e41d9750, Section10,\nthen contradicts the full certificate identity, retaining every formal\ncoordinate and every original multiplier. This argument requires no\nunproved prefix or tail heredity.\n\nFor h(t) from Section1, d*alpha<15/32<1. The difference between the\ntwo sides of (9) is\n\n C b d-P(2+c)=3339/2000-(117/80)t>0\n                                         for0<=t<=1/100.\n\nHence the entire family passing the old scalar tests, including C82\nand C83 on0<=t<=10^(-12), fails the ORIGINAL full certificate cone.\nAt t=0 all data are rational:\n\n alpha=125/232,\n x=(12/5,1,-5/4,-125/232,375/928),\n g=(1,1,-125/232,-125/232,375/928),\n V=(12/5)^2-(2895/928)^2\n   =-(3339*25611)/(25*928^2)<0.\n\nEvery original interpolation pair is legal because these are the\nactual samples of the single explicit smooth convex function above.\nThis is a concrete missing constraint, not a violation inferred from\nindependently chosen jets or a numerical Gram.\n\nThe old system's global-coverage implication is still refuted by\nSections1--8.
